# Later Wittgenstein — Complete Learning Session

> **Syllabus, verbatim:** *Later Wittgenstein : Meaning and Use; Language-games; Critique of Private Language* — UPSC Philosophy Paper I, Section A, item 8. Three printed limbs and one method. Meaning is investigated through **use**, uses are organised as **language-games**, and the critique of **private language** is what that investigation does when it is turned on the inner life.
> **Evidence discipline:** ✅ marks doctrine on which the standard sources converge, ⚠️ marks analytical reconstruction for exam use, and ❓ marks a live scholarly dispute or a contested attribution. Remarks are cited by **§ number**, because Part I numbering is identical across editions and translations, and works are cited by **author, title and year of composition and of publication** wherever the two differ — this topic is full of posthumous texts, and the examiner can test the gap. Model answers below are independent learner practice and are **never** official UPSC keys: UPSC publishes no descriptive model answer for Philosophy Paper I.
> **Terminology rule:** the English concept leads and the German term follows immediately in parentheses — *language-game (Sprachspiel)*, *form of life (Lebensform)*, *family resemblance (Familienähnlichkeit)*, *criterion (Kriterium)*, *symptom (Symptom)*, *avowal (Äußerung)*, *surroundings (Umgebung)*, *the dawning of an aspect (das Aufleuchten eines Aspekts)*, *when language idles or goes on holiday (wenn die Sprache feiert)*. Wittgenstein wrote in German; the *Investigations* is a **posthumous compilation**, so a rendering is always reported as a rendering and never as the author's printed English.

*Concept spine: read it downward. One abandoned essence, one change of question, three printed limbs, one firewall against behaviourism, one method — and a fence around what belongs to other clauses.*

> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the three limbs, "U-G-P".** **U**se → **G**ames → **P**rivate language. Write the limbs in that order and the answer has a spine before it has content, because the third limb is a *consequence* of the first two and collapses into assertion if it is written first.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the four supports, "O-F-R-T".** **O**stensive definition needs background; **F**amily resemblance removes the demand for an essence; **R**ule-following supplies the normativity; **T**herapy states what the whole exercise is for. These four are not decoration: without them the printed limbs cannot be executed.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the private-language target, "L-O-U".** **L**ogically private sensations, fixed by private **O**stension, and therefore in principle **U**nteachable. A candidate who cannot state all three has almost certainly attacked a secret code instead.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the firewall, "C-D-A".** **C**riteria are grammatical, they are **D**efeasible, and the first person uses an **A**vowal rather than a report. Those three words are the entire difference between Wittgenstein and behaviourism.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the hedge that must never be dropped, "LARGE CLASS".** §43 says *for a large class of cases — though not for all*. Quoting "the meaning of a word is its use in the language" without that clause is the single most frequent misquotation in this syllabus item, and it converts a careful remark into the slogan Wittgenstein was avoiding.

### ANSWER-WORTHINESS — WHAT EARNS MARKS AND WHAT DOES NOT

| Classification | Content |
|---|---|
| **CORE MAINS** | Meaning as **use** with the **§43 qualification** stated and explained; the rejection of the **Augustinian picture (§1)** as *one primitive language-game* rather than as a falsehood; words as **tools** and understanding as **mastery of a technique**; **ostensive definition** and the sample, and why pointing presupposes a place in grammar; **family resemblance (§66–67)** with *"Don't think, but look!"* and the exact scope of what it denies; the **language-game (*Sprachspiel*)** with the **builders' "Slab!"** and the list at §23; **form of life (*Lebensform*)** at §19, **agreement in judgments** (§241–242), the **lion** remark and the **bedrock** of §217; the **rule-following paradox (§201)** and its resolution in a grasping of a rule that is **not an interpretation**; the **critique of private language (§243–271)** with the exact target, the **diary "S" (§258)**, the **beetle-in-a-box (§293)** and the five-step criterion-of-correctness core; the **criteria/symptom** distinction with its **defeasibility**, **§580**, the **avowal (§244)**, and the **behaviourism charge answered at §304 and 307–308**; **philosophy as therapy** (§38, 109, 115, 123, 124, 128, 133, 255, 309) and **perspicuous representation**; the **break from the *Tractatus*** with its actual reasons; and all **seven routed 2018–2025 question-parts** |
| **CORE SUPPORTING** | The **colour-exclusion** difficulty that pressured the independence of elementary propositions and prompted the 1929 return to philosophy — a **pressure**, not a one-step refutation; the treatment of **solipsism** at *Tractatus* 5.62 through saying and showing, retained because the 2019 part routes solipsism through the later critique; **Kripke's** sceptical reading (*Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language*, 1982) reported as **"Kripke's Wittgenstein"** and set against the anti-sceptical reading of Baker and Hacker and of McDowell; the **Crusoe / solitary speaker** objection and the distinction between logical privacy and physical solitude; the **provenance rail** — posthumous publication in 1953, Anscombe and Rhees as editors, the 2009 fourth edition revised by Hacker and Schulte, and the renaming and renumbering of Part II as *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment* |
| **SUPPORTING** | **Aspect-seeing** and the **duck-rabbit** (Part II §xi / PPF; the figure is **Jastrow's**, 1899) as bounded enrichment for the experience of meaning; ***On Certainty*** (notes of 1949–51, published 1969) and hinge certainty as a comparison only; the *Blue and Brown Books* (dictated 1933–35, published 1958) as the source of the **criterion/symptom** vocabulary and the **angina** example; the contrast with **logical positivism**, which shares the anti-metaphysical direction but not the single criterion |
| **OPTIONAL ADVANCED** | Whether the "sceptical solution" is a legitimate Wittgensteinian move or a constructive theory his own method forbids; whether language requires an **actual community** or only standards of correctness that are **in principle shareable**; and whether "meaning is use" is a substantive theory of meaning or a therapeutic reminder against essentialism |
| **NOT OWNED HERE** | The picture theory, logical atomism, saying and showing, and *"Whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent"* as **doctrine** (Topic 06 — they appear here only as the position being revised); the verification principle, pseudo-statements and the Vienna Circle (Topic 07); Quine's holism and Strawson's persons (Topic 11); fideist and Wittgensteinian **religious-language** applications of *form of life* (Paper II); Ryle, Austin and speech-act theory as systems of their own |

⚠️ **Ownership boundary, stated once.** This clause owns **7 of 112** Western Philosophy question-parts in the 2018–2025 corpus, and **2021 is the only year without an owned part**. Two consequences follow, and both are visible in the first paragraph of a script. First, the clause is examined **through its transition**: two of the seven parts (2023 and 2025) ask directly why the early position was abandoned, so a candidate who cannot state the *Tractatus* position accurately cannot answer 35 of the 100 marks this clause has carried. The early doctrine must therefore be known well enough to be **left behind precisely** — and then left alone, because expounding it is Topic 06's work. Second, three of the seven parts (2019, 2020 and 2024) are private-language parts asked in three different ways, which means the argument must be held as a **structure** rather than as two illustrations: a candidate who can only retell the diary and the beetle has anecdotes where the examiner wanted a proof.

> **Complete the Basic block first.** Interpretive depth — whether Kripke's sceptical solution is Wittgensteinian at all, whether an actual community is required, and whether meaning-as-use is a theory or a reminder — is deliberately withheld until the clearly labelled optional block after the practice sections. Nothing in that block is needed to write a complete answer to any of the seven routed questions.

### ORIGIN AND CHRONOLOGY — THE MINIMUM NEEDED TO AVOID ANACHRONISM

⚠️ This clause is unusual: almost every text it depends on was **published after its author had died**, and the exam rewards a candidate who says so accurately in one clause. The rail below prevents the six commonest anachronisms — saying that Wittgenstein *published* the *Investigations*; dating the English *Tractatus* to 1921; attributing the duck-rabbit drawing to Wittgenstein; citing "Part II §xi" as though the numbering were stable; treating Kripke's reading as Wittgenstein's own statement; and treating the *Tractatus* as though it had said nothing about solipsism or about the logical order of ordinary language.

```text
   THE EARLY POSITION THAT IS LATER REVISED
   1921  Logisch-Philosophische Abhandlung, published in German
   1922  Bilingual TRACTATUS LOGICO-PHILOSOPHICUS
         NEVER date the English text 1921                          TOPIC 06
         Contents that matter HERE: one general propositional form;
         sense through PICTURING; elementary propositions asserted to be
         logically INDEPENDENT; solipsism handled at 5.62 through
         saying and showing; and the explicit claim that the
         propositions of our everyday language are, JUST AS THEY ARE,
         in perfect logical order
        |
   THE INTERVAL, AND THE CRACK THAT REOPENED THE QUESTION
   1922-28 Wittgenstein is out of academic philosophy - village
         schoolteacher, gardener, architect of his sister's house
   1929  He returns to Cambridge. The COLOUR-EXCLUSION problem is
         acknowledged in print in 'Some Remarks on Logical Form'
         (Proceedings of the Aristotelian Society, supplementary
         volume): 'this spot is red' and 'this spot is green' cannot
         both be true, yet the Tractatus required elementary
         propositions to be independent
         WARNING  this is a PRESSURE on the early system, not a
         one-step refutation, and Wittgenstein himself later
         disowned the 1929 paper
   1929-32 Conversations with SCHLICK and WAISMANN. Verificationist
         formulations do occur there - which is why Topic 07 can cite
         them - but Wittgenstein was NEVER a member of the Circle
        |
   THE LATER METHOD IS DICTATED, THEN WRITTEN
   1933-35 THE BLUE AND BROWN BOOKS are DICTATED to students
         (published 1958). Source of the CRITERION / SYMPTOM
         distinction and of the ANGINA example. Say 'in the Blue
         Book', never 'Wittgenstein wrote'
   1936-49 PHILOSOPHICAL INVESTIGATIONS Part I is composed and
         repeatedly rearranged. It is a book of REMARKS
   1945  Wittgenstein's own preface is written for the projected book
   1949-51 The notes later published as ON CERTAINTY are written; the
         last entries date from days before his death
   1951  WITTGENSTEIN DIES. He published, in his lifetime, one
         philosophical book and one article
        |
   EVERYTHING ELSE IS POSTHUMOUS - AND THE DATES ARE EXAMINABLE
   1953  PHILOSOPHICAL INVESTIGATIONS, edited by G. E. M. ANSCOMBE and
         RUSH RHEES, translated by Anscombe, German and English on
         facing pages. Part I: 693 numbered remarks
   1958  The Blue and Brown Books
   1969  ON CERTAINTY (Uber Gewissheit), notes of 1949-51
   1982  KRIPKE, Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language. Kripke
         presents it as the argument AS IT STRUCK HIM, not as
         exegesis. Standard scholarly shorthand: 'Kripkenstein'
   2009  4th edition, revised by P. M. S. HACKER and JOACHIM SCHULTE.
         Part II is RENAMED 'Philosophy of Psychology - A Fragment'
         and RENUMBERED. So 'PI Part II section xi' (older editions)
         = 'PPF sections 111ff' (4th edn). Cite the duck-rabbit as
         'Part II xi / PPF', never as a bare number
        |
   ONE FIGURE THAT IS NOT HIS
   1899  JASTROW publishes the DUCK-RABBIT. Wittgenstein REPRODUCES
         it. Never attribute the drawing to Wittgenstein
```

⚠️ **What the rail earns you.** Four separate marks are commonly available from it, and none costs more than a clause: that the *Investigations* is **posthumous** and **editorially arranged**; the **1921/1922** split for the *Tractatus*; the **2009 renaming and renumbering** of Part II, which is why a bare "Part II §xi" citation is unsafe; and the fact that **Kripke's reading is a reading**, which is the cheapest way to show that a candidate has read about the debate rather than only about the doctrine.

### SESSION 1 — THE BREAK FROM THE *TRACTATUS*: WHAT CHANGED, WHY IT CHANGED, AND WHAT DID NOT

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The same man wrote two philosophies. The first says that a sentence means something because it is a picture of a possible fact; the second says that we should stop looking for the one thing that makes all sentences meaningful and instead look at the many different jobs words actually do. The second philosophy is not a correction of a detail in the first. It withdraws the question the first was answering.

**Technical definition:** The **later philosophy** is the position developed in *Philosophical Investigations* — composed between 1936 and 1949 and published **posthumously in 1953**, edited by G. E. M. Anscombe and Rush Rhees — which abandons the *Tractatus* commitment to **one general propositional form**, to sense as **depiction** of a possible state of affairs through a shared **logical form**, and to a **depth analysis** terminating in logically independent elementary propositions. In its place it puts a **descriptive**, **grammatical** investigation of the **use** of expressions in **language-games** embedded in **forms of life**. ⚠️ The break is a change of **method and of the demand for essence**, not a move from an "artificial" to an "ordinary" language: the *Tractatus* itself (4.002, 5.5563) already insists that the propositions of everyday language are, just as they stand, in perfect logical order.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Later Wittgenstein does not refute the *Tractatus* so much as withdraw the question it was answering: the demand that something single and hidden explain how any sentence can mean anything is not answered better, it is diagnosed as the source of the trouble — and the one thing that survives the change is the conviction that philosophy's business is clarification rather than discovery.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **one general propositional form** — the precise commitment that is dropped, and the phrase that shows you know *what* was dropped
- **picturing / logical form** — the early account of sense, named accurately in four words
- **colour exclusion** — the internal difficulty, correctly labelled a **pressure**
- **Augustinian picture (§1)** — the model of meaning that both books' opponents share
- **description, not explanation (§109)** — the methodological change in three words
- **continuity of clarification** — what does *not* change, and the mark most candidates leave on the table

**How to use them:** name *one general propositional form* in the first sentence rather than "picture theory", because it identifies the exact commitment abandoned; introduce **colour exclusion** with the word "pressure" attached, since calling it a refutation is a marked error; and reserve **continuity of clarification** for the verdict, where it converts a description of two positions into an argument about one philosopher.

#### Plain-language visual — the same man, two pictures of meaning

```text
                 ONE PHILOSOPHER, TWO QUESTIONS
   ==========================================================================
     EARLY  (Tractatus: German 1921,      LATER (Investigations, composed
            bilingual 1922)                      1936-49, published 1953)
   --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------
     QUESTION                         | QUESTION
     'What must a sentence BE, for it | 'How is this expression actually
      to be capable of sense at all?'  |  USED, and what puzzles us here?'
   --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------
     ANSWER                           | ANSWER
     It must PICTURE a possible state | Look. There are countless different
     of affairs; it shares a LOGICAL  | uses, learned in training, woven
     FORM with what it depicts        | into activities. No single essence
                                      | is required for them to be language
   --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------
     METHOD                           | METHOD
     ANALYSIS down to elementary      | DESCRIPTION: assemble reminders,
     propositions; then draw the      | compare cases, display grammar;
     limit of the sayable and be      | 'philosophy leaves everything as it
     silent about the rest            | is' (124)
   --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------
     WHAT BOTH SHARE                                                        |
     Philosophy is not a body of doctrine but an ACTIVITY of clarification; |
     philosophical trouble comes from MISREADING the workings of language;  |
     ordinary language is not accused of being defective                    |
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** imagine someone who spends years asking "what is the one thing all tools have in common that makes them tools?" and then one morning opens the toolbox and notices that a hammer, a ruler, a pot of glue and a saw have nothing single in common — and that nothing has gone wrong. The workshop still works. That is the shape of the change. The early Wittgenstein wanted the hidden common feature; the later Wittgenstein says the demand for it was the disease, not the diagnosis.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The break is about **essence**, not about vocabulary | he does not say ordinary language is bad and should be replaced |
| The *Tractatus* is not a positivist book | its criterion of sense is **depiction**, not verification — Topic 07's boundary |
| Colour exclusion is a **pressure** | it strains elementary independence; it does not prove no analysis was possible |
| 1929 is the hinge year | the return to Cambridge and the acknowledged difficulty |
| The 1953 book is **posthumous** | never write "Wittgenstein published the *Investigations*" |

> **One-line memory:** the early book asks what language *must* be; the later book asks what we are *doing* with it — and the second question makes the first look like a craving rather than an enquiry.

```text
   5 A COMPLETE ANALYSIS TERMINATES    No example of a Tractarian 'simple
     IN SIMPLES                        object' was ever produced. 'Simple' and
                                       'composite' turn out to be RELATIVE to
                                       a language-game (PI 47): is a chair
                                       simple? relative to what?
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT IS NOT DROPPED - AND SAYING SO IS WHERE THE VERDICT MARK LIVES
   * Philosophy as an ACTIVITY of clarification, not a science
   * The diagnosis that philosophical problems arise from MISUNDERSTANDING
     the logic / grammar of our language
   * The refusal to add a competing metaphysical theory
   * Respect for ordinary language: the Tractatus says everyday propositions
     are 'in perfect logical order' JUST AS THEY ARE (4.002, 5.5563)
   ==========================================================================
   THE TWO TRAPS THIS DIAGRAM EXISTS TO KILL
   X 'He moved from an ideal language to ordinary language' - the Tractatus
     never held ordinary language to be defective
   X 'Colour exclusion refuted the Tractatus' - it pressured ONE commitment
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The 2023 fifteen-mark part asks for the **main reasons** for the shift, and the 2025 twenty-mark part asks for the **transition** with a quoted statement. Both are answered by the left-hand column paired with the right-hand column — five commitments, five reasons — and both are damaged by a script that treats the change as a change of subject-matter rather than of the demand for essence.

#### 1. What the *Tractatus* actually claimed ✅

✅ The early work holds that a proposition has sense because it is a **logical picture** of a possible state of affairs: the elements of the proposition go proxy for objects, and the arrangement of those elements depicts a possible arrangement of the objects. What proposition and fact must share is **logical form**, and logical form itself cannot be said — it **shows** itself. The general propositional form is given as *"This is how things stand."*

⚠️ Two features of the early book must be stated correctly or the later break cannot be described correctly. First, the *Tractatus* does **not** propose to replace ordinary language: at 4.002 and 5.5563 it insists that everyday propositions are in perfect logical order as they stand, and that only the outward form of language disguises the thought. Second, the *Tractatus* does **not** ignore the self and the limits of the subject: it handles **solipsism** at 5.62, where what the solipsist means is said to be correct but incapable of being said, showing itself instead in the limits of language. Both points matter directly: the first blocks the commonest caricature of the transition, and the second is the reason the 2019 part can route solipsism through the later critique without inventing a topic.

#### 2. The internal pressures ✅⚠️

✅ **Colour exclusion.** "This spot is red all over" and "this spot is green all over" cannot both be true. If they are elementary — and they look as though nothing further can be analysed out of them — then elementary propositions are not logically independent, which the *Tractatus* required. Wittgenstein acknowledged the difficulty publicly in **"Some Remarks on Logical Form" (1929)**, the paper he wrote on returning to Cambridge.

⚠️ **State the strength of this correctly.** It is a difficulty that opens a system, not a proof that closes it. The 1929 paper attempts a repair, Wittgenstein soon regarded that repair as unsatisfactory, and he later disowned the paper. Writing "colour exclusion refuted the picture theory" converts a philosopher's long dissatisfaction into a single knock-down move and is one of the marked errors in this clause.

✅ **The missing simples.** The *Tractatus* asserted that analysis terminates in simple objects, but no example was ever given. §47 of the *Investigations* turns this into a diagnosis rather than a complaint: "simple" and "composite" have no absolute sense. Is a chair simple? Are its legs? Is the visual image of the chair simple? The question has an answer only once a language-game fixes what is to count as a part — which means the early requirement was not unsatisfied but **unintelligible as stated**.

✅ **The failure of ostension to found naming.** If names mean by standing for objects, something must connect name to object. Pointing looks like the connection — and §28–32 show that it is not, because a learner shown a sample can take it to teach colour, shape, number, material or place. That argument is Session 3's business, and it is the pressure that converts a difficulty about *analysis* into a difficulty about *meaning as such*.

#### 3. The positive change: from analysis to description ✅

✅ *"We must do away with all explanation, and description alone must take its place"* (§109, in substance), and the problems are to be solved *"not by giving new information, but by arranging what we have always known."* This is not modesty. It is a claim about what philosophical trouble **is**: not ignorance of a further fact, but disorientation among facts we already possess.

The consequence for method is exact: instead of asking *what must meaning be*, we ask *how is this word used here* and *how is this word used there*, and we place the two side by side. The tool is **perspicuous representation** — an arrangement that lets us "see connections" (§122). ⚠️ Note that description in this sense is not passive reporting: assembling reminders is a piece of work, and Wittgenstein's own text is full of arguments, comparisons and reductios. A script that turns "no theory" into "no argument" has swapped one dogma for another, and Wittgenstein's own §133 speaks of **methods, in the plural**, "like different therapies".

#### 4. What continuity survives — the mark most scripts miss ⚠️

⚠️ Four things survive the change, and naming them is what separates a report of two positions from an account of one philosopher:

| Continuity | Early statement | Later statement |
|---|---|---|
| Philosophy is an **activity**, not a body of doctrine | philosophy clarifies propositions; its result is not "philosophical propositions" | philosophy is a **battle against the bewitchment of our intelligence by means of language** (§109) |
| Philosophical trouble comes from **misreading language** | the outward form of language disguises the thought (4.002) | a **picture held us captive** (§115); language **idles** (§38) |
| Ordinary language is **not** the culprit | everyday propositions are in perfect logical order (5.5563) | philosophy **leaves everything as it is** (§124) |
| The book is **self-consuming** | the ladder must be thrown away after climbing it (6.54) | there is no doctrine, only **methods, like different therapies** (§133) |

⚠️ This is also where the sharpest available criticism lives: the objection that the therapeutic method is itself a philosophical thesis about philosophy applies to **both** books in structurally the same way, and Wittgenstein accepts a version of it in each. Saying so in one sentence is a first-class exam observation, because it shows the transition as a development inside one problem rather than as a change of mind between two.

#### 5. How to state the transition without caricature ⚠️

❌ "He moved from an artificial ideal language to ordinary language." — The *Tractatus* held ordinary language to be in order.
❌ "He replaced the picture theory with the use theory." — He replaced the **demand for one theory** with an instruction to look; §43's hedge exists to prevent "use" becoming a second essence.
❌ "The later Wittgenstein is a logical positivist." — The positivists impose a **single criterion** (verifiability) on meaning; the later Wittgenstein's objection to the *Tractatus* applies with equal force to any single criterion, which is why he is a **pressure on** Topic 07 and not a member of it.
❌ "He abandoned logic." — He abandoned the claim that one calculus underlies all use; grammar, rules and normativity remain central throughout.
✅ **The safe formula:** *the later work retains the early aim of dissolving philosophical confusion, and abandons the early assumption that dissolving it requires exhibiting one hidden logical essence common to all meaningful language.*

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The shift from the *Tractatus* to the *Investigations* is best described not as the replacement of one theory of meaning by another but as the abandonment of the demand that meaning have a single hidden essence, and the substitution of description for analysis as the philosopher's method.
> **Named evidence.** The *Tractatus* offers one general propositional form, sense as depiction through shared logical form, and an analysis terminating in logically independent elementary propositions; by 1929 the colour-exclusion difficulty had shown, in "Some Remarks on Logical Form", that two apparently elementary propositions can exclude one another, and §47 of the *Investigations* shows that "simple" has no sense apart from a language-game that fixes what counts as a part. Against that background §109 states that description must take the place of explanation, and §23 exhibits the diversity — ordering, thanking, cursing, play-acting, calculating — that no single form covers.
> **Analysis.** Each early commitment falls to a different pressure, and the pressures are cumulative rather than singular: the diversity of use undermines one form, the failure of ostension undermines naming as the foundation of meaning, colour exclusion strains independence, and the absence of any exhibited simple makes the terminus of analysis unstatable. What connects them is a single diagnosis — that a picture of how language *must* work had been mistaken for a discovery about how it *does*.
> **Qualification.** The transition should not be caricatured as a movement from artificial to ordinary language, since the *Tractatus* already held everyday propositions to be in perfect logical order, and colour exclusion is a pressure on one commitment rather than a refutation of the system; what genuinely survives is the conception of philosophy as clarifying activity, so that the two works are continuous in aim while opposed in the assumption that clarification requires an essence.

#### Exam link

- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks) asks for the **main reasons** that led to the shift. §2 supplies them, §3 supplies the positive replacement, §4 supplies the "critically discuss" half.
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks) quotes a statement contrasting an ideal language with the ways languages are actually used. §1 prevents the caricature the quotation invites, and §5 supplies the safe formula for the verdict.
- **2018 Q1(c)**, **2022 Q1(c)** and the three private-language parts all begin more strongly if the first sentence places the doctrine as the *replacement of an essence-demand*, which is this session's contribution to every other answer.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "He moved from ideal language to ordinary language" | The *Tractatus* says everyday propositions are in perfect logical order (4.002, 5.5563) |
| "Colour exclusion refuted the *Tractatus*" | It **pressured** elementary independence; Wittgenstein's own 1929 repair failed and he disowned that paper |
| "Wittgenstein published the *Investigations* in 1953" | It is **posthumous**, edited by Anscombe and Rhees; he died in 1951 |
| "The *Tractatus* said nothing about the self or solipsism" | 5.62 treats solipsism through saying and showing — which is why the 2019 part is answerable |
| "The picture theory was replaced by the use theory" | §43 is hedged precisely to stop "use" becoming a second essence |
| "The later Wittgenstein is a verificationist" | The objection to one essence applies to one criterion too; Topic 07 is a rival, not a continuation |

#### Revision notes

- *Tractatus*: German **1921**, bilingual **1922**; one general propositional form; sense = **depiction** through shared **logical form**; elementary propositions **independent**; analysis ends in **simples**.
- *Investigations*: composed **1936–49**, published **posthumously 1953**, edited **Anscombe and Rhees**; Part I = **693** numbered remarks.
- Five commitments dropped: one form · depiction as the essence of sense · naming as foundation · elementary independence · analysis into simples.
- Five reasons: diversity of use (§23) · ostension's insufficiency (§28–32) · **colour exclusion** (1929, a **pressure**) · "simple" is game-relative (§47) · the craving for generality diagnosed rather than satisfied.
- Method changes from **analysis** to **description** (§109), aiming at **perspicuous representation** (§122).
- Four continuities: philosophy as **activity** · trouble from **misreading language** · ordinary language **not** at fault · the book **consumes itself** (6.54 / §133).
- 1929 = return to Cambridge; 1933–35 = *Blue and Brown Books* dictated (published 1958).

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE TRANSITION

```text
TRACTATUS: one general form -> sense by picturing -> shared logical form
   |                     -> analysis into independent simples
   |
   +--> PRESSURE 1  diversity of actual use (PI 23) - no one form covers it
   +--> PRESSURE 2  ostension does not fix meaning (PI 28-32)
   +--> PRESSURE 3  colour exclusion (1929) - independence strained
   +--> PRESSURE 4  'simple' has sense only inside a game (PI 47)
   |
   v
DIAGNOSIS: the demand for ONE HIDDEN ESSENCE was a CRAVING, not a finding
   |
   v
INVESTIGATIONS: look at USE -> language-games -> forms of life
   method: DESCRIPTION, not explanation (109); perspicuous representation (122)
   |
   v
CONTINUITY: philosophy = clarifying ACTIVITY; ordinary language not at fault
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the question is withdrawn, not answered better - and the same
   philosopher is at work on both sides of the change
```

### SESSION 2 — MEANING AND USE: THE AUGUSTINIAN PICTURE, THE TOOLBOX, AND THE HEDGE AT §43

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Asking what a word means usually feels like asking what it stands for — as if every word were a label and meaning were the thing behind the label. Wittgenstein's proposal is to stop hunting for the thing and to watch what people do with the word: how it is taught, when it is used, what counts as using it correctly, what would show that someone had not understood it. For a very large range of words, that is all there is to find.

**Technical definition:** The doctrine of **meaning as use** holds that, *"for a large class of cases — though not for all — in which we employ the word 'meaning' it can be defined thus: the meaning of a word is its use in the language"* (**§43**). "Use" here means an expression's **norm-governed role** within a practice: the circumstances in which it is applied, the training by which it is learned, the moves it permits, and the standards by which application counts as correct or incorrect. ⚠️ The doctrine is directed against the **Augustinian picture of language** (**§1**) — the model on which every word names an object and every sentence combines names — which Wittgenstein treats not as a false theory but as **one primitive language-game mistaken for the essence of all language**.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Wittgenstein's claim that meaning is use is not the discovery of what meaning secretly was all along; it is the removal of a question — the question "what does this word *stand for*?" — which had seemed compulsory only because one primitive model of language had been mistaken for its essence.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **Augustinian picture (§1)** — name the target before naming the thesis
- **"for a large class of cases — though not for all" (§43)** — the hedge, quoted, not paraphrased
- **tools in a toolbox (§11)** — the image that does the philosophical work in one line
- **mastery of a technique** — understanding, defined without a mental episode
- **norm-governed role** — the phrase that keeps "use" from meaning "usage frequency"
- **the lever image (§12)** — uniform appearance concealing different functions

**How to use them:** open on the **Augustinian picture**, because a thesis stated without its target reads as an assertion; quote the **§43 hedge** verbatim in the same paragraph, since dropping it is the single commonest misquotation in this clause; and use **norm-governed role** the first time you write "use", to close off the "use means whatever people happen to do" reading before the examiner can raise it.

#### Plain-language visual — the toolbox, and what the label-model cannot handle

```text
   THE LABEL MODEL SAYS:   word ---- stands for ----> thing
   ==========================================================================
   IT WORKS TOLERABLY FOR      'table'  'chair'  'apple'  'Ram'
   IT COLLAPSES IMMEDIATELY FOR
      'the'        - which object?          'if'      - which object?
      'five'       - point to the five      'not'     - point to the not
      'perhaps'    - a mood? a thing?       'meaning' - a further object??
      'pain'       - a private object? -> Session 8 shows where that ends
   ==========================================================================
   THE TOOLBOX MODEL SAYS:  word ---- has a JOB in an activity ----> use
       hammer  saw  ruler  glue-pot  screwdriver  nails
       They look like one kind of thing lying in one box. Their FUNCTIONS
       have nothing single in common. (PI 11)
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE LEVER WARNING (PI 12)
       In a locomotive cab all the handles LOOK alike. One is a crank that
       can be moved continuously; one is a switch with two positions; one
       is a brake-lever that works harder the harder you pull. UNIFORM
       APPEARANCE, DIFFERENT FUNCTION. Words are like the handles.
   ==========================================================================
   THE RESULT
       'What does it mean?'  becomes  'How is it used, and how was that use
       learned, and what would count as getting it wrong?'
```

**In simple words:** the label-model is not stupid — it is the first thing anyone would think, and it is roughly right for a small class of words. What is wrong is the generalisation. Once you assume every word must be a label, you start inventing invisible objects for the words that plainly have no visible object: a mental image behind "meaning", a private sensation behind "pain", an abstract entity behind "five". Wittgenstein's method is to refuse the first step, and most of the invented objects then have nothing to do.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| §1 quotes **Augustine**, not a modern opponent | the picture is ancient, natural and therefore hard to see as a picture |
| The Augustinian picture is not *false* | it is one **primitive language-game** over-generalised |
| "Use" ≠ **usage frequency** | a rare word is not a less meaningful word |
| "Use" ≠ **usefulness** | a useless sentence can be perfectly meaningful |
| "Use" ≠ **whatever I choose** | correctness is built into the practice, not into the speaker |

> **One-line memory:** don't ask what the word points at; ask what the word is *for*, who taught it to you, and what would count as using it wrongly.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the thesis, its hedge, and the four things it does not say

```text
   PI 43, WITH ITS ARCHITECTURE MADE VISIBLE
   ==========================================================================
   'For a large class of cases - THOUGH NOT FOR ALL - in which we employ the
    word "meaning" it can be defined thus: the meaning of a word is its use
    in the language.'
   ==========================================================================
   |----------------|  |-----------------------|  |----------------------|
   | SCOPE LIMITER  |  | WHAT IS BEING DEFINED |  | THE DEFINITION       |
   | 'a large class |  | our EMPLOYMENT of the |  | USE IN THE LANGUAGE  |
   |  of cases,     |  | word 'meaning' - not  |  | i.e. the norm-       |
   |  though not    |  | a metaphysical entity |  | governed ROLE of the |
   |  for all'      |  | called Meaning        |  | expression in a      |
   |                |  |                       |  | practice             |
   |----------------|  |-----------------------|  |----------------------|
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT THE REMARK DOES NOT SAY - FOUR REFUSALS, EACH WORTH A LINE
   X It does NOT say that meaning is FREQUENCY of employment
   X It does NOT say that meaning is USEFULNESS or practical payoff
   X It does NOT say that any use a speaker likes is thereby CORRECT
   X It does NOT offer a UNIVERSAL DEFINITION of 'meaning' - the hedge is
     doctrine: proper names, for instance, are handled separately at 79,
     and 'the bearer of a name' is expressly distinguished from 'the
     meaning of a name' at 40
   ==========================================================================
   THE THREE POSITIVE MOVES THAT REPLACE THE LABEL MODEL
   1 REJECT the referential generalisation .......... PI 1, 10, 11, 12
   2 REDESCRIBE understanding as MASTERY OF A
     TECHNIQUE, shown in going on correctly ......... PI 143-155, 199
   3 RELOCATE meaning in CONTEXT: the same words in a
     different game do different work ............... PI 117, 432
   ==========================================================================
   THE WORKED CASE THE EXAMINER CAN CHECK
   'Water!'  shouted at a fire      = an order / an alarm
             asked at a table       = a request
             written on a phial     = a label / a classification
             said while pointing at
             a chemical formula     = an identification
   SAME WORD. SAME SOUND. FOUR JOBS. The 'meaning' is not a fifth thing
   standing behind the four.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** This is the diagram to redraw first in the 2023 and 2025 transition answers, because it shows exactly what replaced picturing — and it is also the diagram that protects you in the 2018 and 2022 parts, since both family resemblance and forms of life are *consequences* of taking use seriously.

#### 1. The Augustinian picture, quoted and diagnosed ✅

✅ §1 opens with a passage from Augustine's *Confessions* describing how the child learns language: elders name objects, move towards them, and the child grasps that the sound stands for the thing. Wittgenstein's comment is precise and often misread. He does **not** say the description is false. He says it gives us *"a particular picture of the essence of human language"*: that individual words name objects, that sentences are combinations of such names, and that in this picture we find *"the roots of the following idea: every word has a meaning; this meaning is correlated with the word; it is the object for which the word stands."*

⚠️ The diagnosis, at §2–§4, is a comparison rather than a refutation: he constructs the **builders' language** — a complete primitive language in which "Slab!", "Block!", "Pillar!", "Beam!" are the whole vocabulary — and observes that Augustine's account fits *that* language perfectly. So the picture is not wrong; it is **a description of one language-game, offered as the essence of all**. ⚠️ Writing "Wittgenstein refuted Augustine's theory of meaning" loses this, and with it the entire method: the later Wittgenstein's characteristic move is to shrink an alleged essence into one case among many.

#### 2. Words as tools; handles that look alike ✅

✅ **§11:** *"Think of the tools in a tool-box: there is a hammer, pliers, a saw, a screwdriver, a rule, a glue-pot, glue, nails and screws. The functions of words are as diverse as the functions of these objects."* ✅ **§12:** in the cabin of a locomotive there are handles all looking more or less alike — but one is a crank movable continuously, one a switch with two positions, one a brake-lever that resists more the harder it is pulled.

⚠️ The philosophical work these images do is exact, and worth stating rather than merely quoting: uniformity of **appearance** across words tempts us to expect uniformity of **function**, and the expectation is a grammatical illusion. That is why a list of examples is not a substitute for the point. The point is that we were misled by the *look* of language — nouns lined up like nouns — into assuming one job.

#### 3. Understanding as mastery of a technique ✅

✅ If meaning were an object, understanding would be a private encounter with it — a mental picture, a flash, an inner "now I've got it". §143–155 examine exactly that suggestion through the case of a pupil learning to continue a numerical series, and §199 concludes that *"to understand a sentence means to understand a language. To understand a language means to be master of a technique."*

The argument has three steps:
1. The inner "aha" can occur without understanding — the pupil who feels the flash and then continues wrongly did not understand.
2. Understanding can be present without any distinctive inner episode — the fluent reader has no "aha" at each word.
3. What settles the question in every actual case is whether the person **goes on correctly** in the appropriate circumstances.
⚠️ **Step 3 is not behaviourism**, and the difference must be stated: the criterion of understanding is what one does, but performance is **defeasible** — someone can understand and be unable to perform (fatigue, distraction, paralysis), and can perform once by luck without understanding. Wittgenstein's claim is about **what the concept of understanding requires**, not about an identity between understanding and behaviour. That distinction is Session 9's business and is the reason it exists.

#### 4. Context: the same words, different work ✅

✅ Meaning is not fixed in isolation. "Water!" is an alarm, a request, a label or an identification depending on the game it is a move in; "It is five o'clock on the sun" (§350–§351) has an impeccable surface and no settled use; "I know I am in pain" looks like a report and does no reporting work at all (§246). ⚠️ The conclusion is not that words have no stable meanings but that stability is conferred by **regular practice with standards**, not by a correlated object. That is why the doctrine leads directly to language-games (Session 5) and to forms of life (Session 6) rather than stopping at "context matters".

#### 5. The three misreadings that cost the most marks ⚠️

| Misreading | Why it is wrong | The one-line correction to write |
|---|---|---|
| "Meaning is **how often** a word is used" | Frequency is a statistical fact about a population, not a normative role | Use is a **role**, not a rate |
| "Meaning is **whatever the speaker intends**" | This restores a private mental determinant, which the private-language argument then destroys | A speaker can **misuse** a word, and that possibility is what makes it a word |
| "Meaning is use, therefore anything goes" | Grammar, training and correction supply standards without a hidden essence | No essence is **not** no norm |

⚠️ There is also a genuine tension worth conceding rather than hiding, because conceding it earns the "critically discuss" mark: if meaning is use, how do we distinguish a **new** use from a **wrong** one? Poetic extension, metaphor, technical redefinition and simple error all look, from outside, like departures from established practice. Wittgenstein's resources are grammar, training and the surroundings of the utterance; whether those resources fix the boundary in every case is a live objection and appears in Session 13's ledger.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The doctrine that the meaning of a word is its use is not a rival theory of what meaning *is* but a redirection of enquiry away from the search for a correlated object and towards the norm-governed role an expression has in a practice.
> **Named evidence.** §1 quotes Augustine's account of learning language and identifies in it a picture on which every word names an object and every sentence combines names; §2–§4 answer it not by refutation but by constructing the builders' language of "Slab!", "Block!", "Pillar!", "Beam!", for which the picture is exactly right — showing it to be one primitive language-game generalised into an essence. §11 replaces the picture with the tool-box, §12 with the locomotive handles that look alike and work differently, and §43 states the thesis with its own limitation: "for a large class of cases — though not for all".
> **Analysis.** The redirection dissolves rather than answers a series of questions: what "the" or "five" or "perhaps" stands for; what object understanding consists in encountering; what sort of entity a meaning is. §199's formula — to understand a language is to be master of a technique — replaces an inner episode with an ability displayed in going on correctly, and §143–155 show why: the inner flash can occur without understanding and understanding can occur without the flash, so the flash was never the criterion.
> **Qualification.** The thesis is hedged in its own statement and must be quoted with the hedge, since Wittgenstein separates a name's bearer from its meaning at §40 and treats proper names distinctly at §79; nor does "use" mean frequency, usefulness or the speaker's private intention, since a speaker can misuse a word and that very possibility is what makes it a word. The residual difficulty, which Wittgenstein does not fully settle, is how established use is to be distinguished from innovation and from error without appeal to the essence he has removed.

#### Exam link

- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks): §1–2 supply the positive content of the "use-theory" the question names, and §5 supplies the critical half.
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks): the quoted statement contrasts an ideal language with "the ways in which languages are actually used" — §1, 2 and 4 are the exposition of that phrase, and the §43 hedge prevents the answer from replacing one essence with another.
- **2018 Q1(c)** (10 marks): family resemblance is the *reason* meaning-as-use does not need an essence, so this session is the necessary first paragraph of that answer.
- **2020**, **2024** (private language): the diary argument only bites because meaning requires a **rule of use**; without this session, the third limb has no premise.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Quoting "the meaning of a word is its use" without the hedge | §43 says "for a large class of cases — though not for all"; the hedge is doctrine |
| "Wittgenstein refuted Augustine's theory" | §2–4 show the picture fits **one** primitive language-game; it is over-generalisation, not falsehood |
| "Use = usage frequency / usefulness" | Use is a **norm-governed role**; a rare or useless sentence can be fully meaningful |
| "Meaning is use is a theory of meaning" | It is a reminder against essentialism; whether it is nevertheless a theory is the optional-block dispute |
| "Understanding is just behaviour" | Understanding is **mastery of a technique**; performance is defeasible in both directions |
| "The bearer of the name is its meaning" | §40 separates them explicitly — when Mr N. N. dies, the name does not become meaningless |

#### Revision notes

- **§1** Augustine → picture: word names object, sentence combines names, meaning = the object correlated with the word.
- **§2–4** builders' language ("Slab!", "Block!", "Pillar!", "Beam!") — the picture fits **this** game; that is its status.
- **§11** tools in a tool-box · **§12** locomotive handles: uniform appearance, diverse function.
- **§40** the **bearer** of a name ≠ the **meaning** of a name.
- **§43** the thesis **with** its hedge: "for a large class of cases — though not for all".
- **§199** to understand a language = to be **master of a technique**; **§143–155** the pupil and the series.
- Use is a **norm-governed role**: not frequency, not usefulness, not private intention.
- Live objection: how to separate **new use** from **wrong use** — conceded, not concealed.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — MEANING AND USE

```text
QUESTION 'What does this word mean?'
   |
   +--> LABEL MODEL (Augustinian picture, PI 1): find the object it stands for
   |        works for  'table', 'Ram'
   |        collapses for  'the', 'if', 'five', 'perhaps', 'meaning', 'pain'
   |        STATUS: not false - ONE PRIMITIVE GAME over-generalised (2-4)
   |
   +--> TOOLBOX MODEL (11) + LEVER WARNING (12): uniform look, diverse function
   |
   v
THESIS (43): for a LARGE CLASS of cases - THOUGH NOT FOR ALL - the meaning
   of a word is its USE in the language
   |
   +--> USE = norm-governed ROLE in a practice: circumstances + training +
   |      permitted moves + standards of correctness
   +--> NOT frequency · NOT usefulness · NOT private intention · NOT licence
   |
   v
UNDERSTANDING (199) = MASTERY OF A TECHNIQUE, shown in going on correctly
   inner flash: neither necessary nor sufficient (143-155)
   |
   v
LEADS TO  family resemblance (no essence needed) -> language-games (how uses
   are organised) -> forms of life (what holds them in place)
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the question 'what does it stand for?' is not answered better -
   it is shown to have been the wrong question for most of language
```

### SESSION 3 — OSTENSIVE DEFINITION, SAMPLES AND TRAINING: WHY POINTING IS NOT A FOUNDATION

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** It feels obvious that language gets connected to the world by pointing: you show a child a red patch, say "red", and the word is fixed. Wittgenstein's observation is that pointing on its own settles nothing, because the child has to already know *what kind of thing* is being pointed out — the colour? the shape? the number? the material? the direction? Pointing teaches only to someone who has already been prepared to be taught.

**Technical definition:** An **ostensive definition** explains a word by pointing at an object or sample and uttering the word. Wittgenstein argues (**§28–38**) that ostensive definition is **systematically ambiguous**: *"an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted in every case"* (§28). It succeeds only where the learner's **place in the language is already prepared** (§31) — where training, attention and the surrounding language-game have fixed what role the sample is to play. A **sample** used in explanation belongs to the **means of representation** rather than to what is represented: the colour chart used to explain "sepia" functions as a **standard within a practice**, not as an object whose nature guarantees its own application.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Ostensive definition looks like the point at which language touches the world, and Wittgenstein's argument is that it is nothing of the kind: pointing can teach only where a practice has already decided what is being pointed at, so the supposed foundation turns out to presuppose the building it was to support.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"can be variously interpreted in every case" (§28)** — the argument in one quoted clause
- **the place in the language is already prepared (§31)** — the positive condition, quoted
- **sample as part of the method of representation (§50)** — why the sample is not a super-object
- **training (*Abrichtung*)** — the primitive teaching that precedes explanation (§5, §6)
- **regress of interpretations** — the link forward to §201
- **the chess-king analogy (§31)** — showing what "already prepared" means

**How to use them:** state the ambiguity with **§28**'s clause quoted, then immediately give the positive condition from **§31**, because an answer that only shows ostension failing sounds sceptical; use the **chess-king** analogy to make "already prepared" concrete in one sentence; and end by pointing forward to the **regress of interpretations**, which turns this session into the premise of the rule-following argument rather than an isolated observation.

#### Plain-language visual — one act of pointing, five possible lessons

```text
   THE TEACHER POINTS AT TWO RED SQUARES AND SAYS  'TWO'
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT MIGHT THE LEARNER TAKE THE WORD TO MEAN?
     'TWO'  ->  the NUMBER            (correct, as it happens)
     'TWO'  ->  the COLOUR red        (why not? the samples are red)
     'TWO'  ->  the SHAPE square      (why not? the samples are square)
     'TWO'  ->  the MATERIAL card     (why not? both are card)
     'TWO'  ->  THIS PARTICULAR PAIR  (a proper name for these objects)
     'TWO'  ->  the DIRECTION over there (the finger does point somewhere)
   ==========================================================================
   NOTHING IN THE POINTING ITSELF EXCLUDES ANY OF THESE.
   Adding words does not end the problem, it postpones it:
     'This NUMBER is called two'  - only helps if 'number' is already known
     'This COLOUR is called sepia' - only helps if 'colour' is already known
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT ACTUALLY MAKES IT WORK
     PRIOR TRAINING          the learner has been drilled into a practice
     A PREPARED PLACE (31)   the language already has a slot for a
                             number-word, and the learner can recognise it
     THE SURROUNDING GAME    counting, sorting, ordering, checking
     CORRECTION              the teacher intervenes when the learner
                             generalises wrongly, and this is the point at
                             which meaning is actually fixed
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** teaching by pointing is like handing someone a chess piece and saying "this is the king". If they already play a board game with pieces, ranks and moves, the sentence teaches them something. If they have never seen a game at all, the sentence tells them almost nothing — they do not yet know that pieces have *roles*. §31 uses exactly this comparison, and it is the cheapest way to make the argument vivid in an answer.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The problem is not doubt | it is **indeterminacy**: nothing in the gesture selects a category |
| Adding "this colour…" helps | but only by borrowing understanding you already have |
| Training (*Abrichtung*) precedes explanation | §5–§6: the child is drilled before it is told |
| The sample is a **tool**, not a thing described | §50: the standard metre "has no length" in the game where it is the standard |
| The moral is **not** scepticism | ostension works — it just is not a foundation |

> **One-line memory:** pointing teaches only those who already know what sort of question the pointing answers.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the failure of ostension as a foundation, and what replaces it

```text
   THE FOUNDATIONALIST HOPE                THE ACTUAL STRUCTURE
   ==========================================================================
   WORD                                    WORD
     ^ meaning fixed by                      ^ meaning fixed by
   OSTENSIVE DEFINITION                    ROLE IN A LANGUAGE-GAME
     ^ which fixes itself by                 ^ sustained by
   THE OBJECT POINTED AT                   TRAINING + CORRECTION + SURROUNDINGS
                                             ^ within which
                                           OSTENSION AND SAMPLES ARE USEFUL
                                           INSTRUMENTS - not foundations
   ==========================================================================
   THE THREE STEPS OF THE ARGUMENT
   1 AMBIGUITY (28)  'an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted
                      in every case'. Pointing at two nuts and saying 'two'
                      may teach number, colour, shape, material or name
   2 THE APPARENT REPAIR (29-30)  add a category word: 'this NUMBER is
                      called two'. But that only works if the learner
                      already has the category. The repair BORROWS what it
                      was meant to supply
   3 THE REAL CONDITION (31)  the ostensive definition explains the use of
                      a word 'when the place is already prepared'. Compare:
                      'this is the king' teaches a chess-learner who knows
                      the shape of a game, and teaches almost nothing to
                      someone who has never seen a game at all
   ==========================================================================
   SAMPLES: WHAT KIND OF THING THEY ARE  (50, 16)
   * A sample used in explaining a word belongs to the MEANS OF
     REPRESENTATION, like the colour chart or the standard metre
   * PI 50: of the standard metre in Paris one can say neither that it is
     one metre long nor that it is not - not because of a mysterious
     property, but because that is its ROLE in the game of measuring
   * So the sample does not carry its own instructions. It is a tool whose
     use has to be learned like any other
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT THIS SESSION SETS UP  ->  if no explanation is self-applying, then
   understanding cannot consist in attaching a further INTERPRETATION. That
   is exactly the paradox of PI 201, and this is where the private-language
   argument gets its premise.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** This diagram is the hinge of the whole clause. The 2023 and 2025 transition answers use it to explain why naming could not remain the foundation of meaning; the 2020 and 2024 private-language answers use it because a **private** ostensive definition is exactly an ostensive definition stripped of the training, correction and prepared place that make public ostension work.

#### 1. The argument from ambiguity ✅

✅ **§28:** one can ostensively define a proper name, a colour word, a number word, the name of a point of the compass — *"an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted in every case."* Point at two nuts and say "This is called 'two'", and the learner may take "two" to name that group of nuts, or their colour, or their kind, or their shape. Nothing in the pointing decides.

⚠️ **The exact strength of the claim.** Wittgenstein is not saying that we cannot in fact teach words by pointing — obviously we can, and do, all day. He is saying that when we do, the success is not explained by the pointing. Something else is carrying the weight, and the philosophical picture that made ostension look foundational had concealed it. ❌ Reading the passage as scepticism about ostension is a marked error; the passage is an inventory of what ostension needs.

#### 2. Why adding a category-word does not repair the foundation ✅

✅ **§29–30:** the natural repair is to say "This **number** is called 'two'", or "This **colour** is called 'sepia'". But then the word "number" or "colour" must already be understood — and if it is explained ostensively in turn, the same ambiguity recurs. ✅ §30 draws the conclusion: *"one has already to know (or be able to do) something in order to be capable of asking a thing's name."*

⚠️ This is a **regress**, and naming it as such is what raises an answer above narration. Each explanation stands in need of an explanation of the same kind, and the series terminates not in a self-explaining explanation but in **something one does**: reacting to training, going on in the trained way. That terminus is §201's "way of grasping a rule which is not an interpretation" and §217's "This is simply what I do", and the connection should be stated explicitly in any answer about either.

#### 3. The prepared place, and the chess-king ✅

✅ **§31:** *"When one shows someone the king in chess and says: 'This is the king', this does not tell him the use of this piece — unless he already knows the rules of the game up to this last point: the shape of the king."* Wittgenstein's conclusion is that an ostensive definition *"explains the use — the meaning — of the word when the overall role of the word in language is clear"*, or, in the formula worth quoting: **when the place is already prepared**.

⚠️ **What "prepared" does and does not mean.** It does not mean that the learner must already possess the concept being taught — that would be viciously circular and would make learning impossible. It means the learner must already occupy the **practice**: be able to attend, to imitate, to be corrected, to recognise that a word is being introduced and that words have roles. Wittgenstein's account of that entry-level condition is **training (*Abrichtung*)** at §5–§6: the child is not first told what words mean and then set to use them; it is drilled into use, and explanation becomes possible later. ⚠️ *Abrichtung* is the word used for training an animal, and the choice is deliberate: the primitive stage is pre-explanatory. That is a point of interpretation and should be marked as one.

#### 4. Samples belong to the method of representation ✅

✅ **§16:** if colour samples are shown along with the words in a language-game, it is most natural to count the samples as part of the **language** itself, not as objects the language is about. ✅ **§50:** of the standard metre in Paris one can say neither that it is one metre long nor that it is not — *"this is, of course, not to ascribe any extraordinary property to it, but only to mark its peculiar role in the language-game of measuring with a metre-rule."*

⚠️ The philosophical payoff is large and directly examinable. If a sample were an ordinary object whose nature fixed the word's application, then a **private** sample — a remembered sensation — might do the same job in a private language. §50 removes that possibility in advance: a sample functions as a **standard** only because a practice of comparing, applying and correcting has given it that office. A private sensation has no such office, which is why the diary argument (Session 8) is not an isolated puzzle but the direct consequence of this session.

#### 5. What survives: ostension is useful, not foundational ⚠️

⚠️ The positive residue should be stated explicitly, since "critically discuss" questions reward a candidate who says what a critique **leaves standing**:
1. Ostensive teaching genuinely works, and Wittgenstein never denies it.
2. Samples are indispensable instruments of explanation — colour charts, the standard metre, a paradigm shade.
3. What fails is only the **philosophical office** ostension was given: the office of connecting language to reality once and for all, from outside all practice.
4. Meaning is therefore constrained but not arbitrary: human reactions, materials, purposes and established practice narrow what can count as teaching or as following. ⚠️ **No essence is not no norm.**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Ostensive definition cannot be the foundation of meaning, because it explains a word only where a practice has already fixed what is being indicated — so the supposed point of contact between language and world presupposes the language it was to ground.
> **Named evidence.** §28 states that an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted in every case: pointing at two nuts while saying "two" may teach the number, the colour, the shape, the material or a proper name. §29–30 show that the obvious repair — "this *number* is called two" — merely borrows an understanding of "number", so that "one has already to know (or be able to do) something in order to be capable of asking a thing's name". §31 gives the positive condition through the chess-king: being shown the king teaches the piece's use only to someone who already knows the shape of the game, and the ostensive definition works "when the place is already prepared". §50 adds that a sample belongs to the means of representation, since the standard metre is neither one metre long nor not.
> **Analysis.** The argument is a regress argument rather than a sceptical one. Every explanation of a word stands in need of an explanation of the same kind, and the series does not terminate in a self-interpreting item; it terminates in trained action, which is why §5–§6 place *Abrichtung* before explanation and why §201 will deny that grasping a rule is attaching an interpretation. The immediate consequence for the third printed limb is decisive: a private ostensive definition is precisely ostension stripped of training, correction and prepared place, so whatever weight public ostension could not bear alone, private ostension cannot bear at all.
> **Qualification.** None of this makes ostensive teaching ineffective or samples dispensable; what is refused is only the philosophical office of grounding meaning from outside every practice. Nor does the argument make meaning conventional in the sense of arbitrary, since human reactions, the materials involved and established applications constrain what can count as teaching and as following — the absence of an essence is not the absence of a norm.

#### Exam link

- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks): ostension's failure is one of the "main reasons" for the shift and is the reason *naming* could not stay foundational.
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks): the quoted contrast between an ideal language and actual use is sharpened by showing that even the most "direct" link — pointing — is practice-dependent.
- **2020 Q4(c)** and **2024 Q1(d)** (private language): this session supplies the premise. A private ostensive definition is an ostensive definition minus every condition §31 identified.
- **2018 Q1(c)**: §31's "prepared place" is the same idea that makes family-resemblance concepts teachable without a definition.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Wittgenstein says we cannot teach words by pointing" | We plainly can; his claim is that the pointing is not what does the work |
| "Adding 'this colour' solves the problem" | §29–30: it borrows the understanding it was to supply, and the regress restarts |
| "The prepared place means already having the concept" | It means already occupying the **practice** — attending, imitating, being corrected |
| "The standard metre has a mysterious property" | §50: it has a **role** in the game of measuring, not an extraordinary length |
| "Samples are objects the language describes" | §16, §50: they belong to the **means of representation** |
| Treating this session as separate from private language | It **is** the premise of the diary argument; say so |

#### Revision notes

- **§28** an ostensive definition "can be variously interpreted in every case" — number, colour, shape, material, proper name, direction.
- **§29–30** the category-word repair borrows understanding: "one has already to know (or be able to do) something in order to be capable of asking a thing's name."
- **§31 chess-king:** "This is the king" teaches only one who knows the shape of the game — the **place must already be prepared**.
- **§5–6 training (*Abrichtung*)** precedes explanation; the term is that used for animal training, and the choice is deliberate.
- **§16, §50** samples belong to the **means of representation**; the standard metre is neither one metre long nor not.
- Structure: **ambiguity → failed repair → prepared place → regress → trained action**, which is §201's terminus.
- Positive residue: ostension **works**; what fails is its philosophical office as a foundation.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — OSTENSION AND SAMPLES

```text
POINTING + WORD  ('two', while indicating two nuts)
   |
   +--> AMBIGUITY (28): number? colour? shape? material? this pair? direction?
   |
   +--> REPAIR ATTEMPT (29): 'this NUMBER is called two'
   |        -> only works if 'number' is already understood (30)
   |        -> REGRESS: each explanation needs an explanation of the same kind
   |
   v
CONDITION (31): the ostensive definition explains the use WHEN THE PLACE IS
   ALREADY PREPARED   [chess-king: teaches only one who knows what a game is]
   |
   +--> what prepares the place: TRAINING (Abrichtung, 5-6) + the surrounding
   |    game + CORRECTION when the learner generalises wrongly
   |
   +--> SAMPLES (16, 50): part of the MEANS OF REPRESENTATION; the standard
   |    metre is neither one metre long nor not - that is its ROLE
   |
   v
TERMINUS: explanation ends not in a self-interpreting item but in TRAINED
   ACTION   ->  PI 201 'a way of grasping a rule which is not an
   interpretation'  ->  PI 217 'This is simply what I do'
   |
   v
CONSEQUENCE FOR LIMB 3: private ostension is ostension minus training,
   minus correction, minus a prepared place - so it cannot fix a standard
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: pointing does not connect language to the world; a practice
   already connected to the world makes pointing intelligible
```

### SESSION 4 — FAMILY RESEMBLANCE: "DON'T THINK, BUT LOOK!" AND THE END OF THE ESSENCE-DEMAND

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Ask what all games have in common and you will find nothing that fits every case: not winning and losing (patience), not competition (ring-a-ring-a-roses), not skill (dice), not amusement (professional sport is work). Yet "game" is a perfectly good word that nobody has trouble using. Wittgenstein's point is that a word can be in perfect order without there being one feature shared by everything it applies to.

**Technical definition:** **Family resemblance (*Familienähnlichkeit*)** is the relation Wittgenstein finds among the things a general term covers when no single feature is common to them all: *"a complicated network of similarities overlapping and criss-crossing"* — *"sometimes overall similarities, sometimes similarities of detail"* (**§66**). He names it at **§67**: *"I can think of no better expression to characterise these similarities than 'family resemblances'; for the various resemblances between members of a family — build, features, colour of eyes, gait, temperament, etc. etc. — overlap and criss-cross in the same way."* The instruction that introduces it is **§66**'s *"Don't think, but look!"* — an instruction about **method** before it is a claim about concepts.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Family resemblance is not the discovery that concepts are vague; it is the removal of a demand — the Socratic demand that anyone who uses a word competently must be able to state what all its instances share — and once that demand goes, the claim that language is a game stops being a metaphor and becomes a description.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"Don't think, but look!" (§66)** — the methodological instruction, quoted
- **overlapping and criss-crossing similarities** — the exact image, not "vagueness"
- **the thread and its fibres (§67)** — the strength-without-a-core analogy
- **boundary that can be drawn but has not been (§68–69)** — the precise status of open texture
- **"blurred edges" and the photograph (§71)** — why an inexact concept is still usable
- **extensible / open-textured concept** — the technical label for the result

**How to use them:** lead with the **method** (*"Don't think, but look!"*) rather than with the conclusion, because the examiner in 2018 asked how family resemblance *helps* Wittgenstein — which is a question about argumentative work, not about definition; and reach the **thread analogy** early, since it pre-empts the standard objection that resemblance without a core cannot hold a concept together.

#### Plain-language visual — five games, and the feature that keeps failing

```text
   LOOK FOR THE ONE THING THEY ALL SHARE
   ==========================================================================
                    | AMUSE- | COMPE- | WIN /  | SKILL  | RULES  | LUCK
                    | MENT   | TITION | LOSE   |        |        |
   -----------------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------
   CHESS            |  yes   |  yes   |  yes   |  yes   |  yes   |  no
   PATIENCE         |  yes   |  no    |  yes*  |  some  |  yes   |  yes
   RING-A-RING-      |  yes   |  no    |  no    |  no    |  yes   |  no
     A-ROSES        |        |        |        |        |        |
   THROWING A BALL  |  yes   |  no    |  no    |  some  |  no    |  no
     AT A WALL      |        |        |        |        |        |
   OLYMPIC FINAL    |  not   |  yes   |  yes   |  yes   |  yes   |  some
                    | for the|        |        |        |        |
                    | player |        |        |        |        |
   ==========================================================================
   EVERY COLUMN HAS AT LEAST ONE 'no'.  There is no row of all-yes features.
   * even 'winning' shifts sense: in patience one wins against the cards
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT WE SEE INSTEAD:  chess ~ patience (rules, winning)
                         patience ~ ball-at-wall (solitary, pastime)
                         ball-at-wall ~ ring-a-ring (no winner, bodily play)
                         ring-a-ring ~ chess (rules, turns)
   A CHAIN OF OVERLAPS, WITH NO LINK RUNNING THROUGH THE WHOLE.
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** think of a large family photograph. One brother has the father's nose and the mother's eyes; a sister has the father's build and neither nose nor eyes; a cousin has the gait and the temperament and nothing else. Everyone looks unmistakably like a member of that family, and no single feature runs through all of them. Wittgenstein says that many of our most important words — *game*, *language*, *number*, *proposition*, *understanding* — hang together in exactly that way.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Family resemblance is a claim about **some** concepts | "triangle" and "prime number" are still sharply defined |
| It denies a **requirement**, not a possibility | it says an essence is not *needed*, not that one can never be found |
| It is not the claim "no two games share anything" | plainly they do; there is simply no feature shared by **all** |
| Blurred edges do not disable a concept | §71: an indistinct photograph is often exactly the picture we need |
| The boundary can be drawn **for a purpose** | §69: we can fix one; we have not needed to |

> **One-line memory:** the word works because the family is real, not because a hidden ancestor is common to every member.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the argument's four moves, and the four things it does not claim

```text
   MOVE 1 - THE METHOD  (PI 66)
     'Don't say: "There MUST be something common, or they would not be
      called 'games'" - but LOOK and SEE whether there is anything common
      to all.'
     -> The word MUST is doing the damage. It is a demand carried to the
        facts, not a result read off them.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   MOVE 2 - THE SURVEY  (PI 66)
     Board-games, card-games, ball-games, Olympic games, war-games,
     ring-a-ring-a-roses, patience, throwing a ball at a wall.
     Amusement fails · competition fails · winning and losing fails ·
     skill fails · rules fail · luck fails.
     RESULT: 'a complicated network of similarities overlapping and
     criss-crossing'.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   MOVE 3 - THE NAME AND THE ANALOGY  (PI 67)
     FAMILY RESEMBLANCES: build, features, colour of eyes, gait,
     temperament - overlapping and criss-crossing.
     THE THREAD: 'we extend our concept ... as in spinning a thread we
     twist fibre on fibre. And the strength of the thread does not reside
     in the fact that some one fibre runs through its whole length, but
     in the overlapping of many fibres.'
     -> STRENGTH WITHOUT A CORE. This single image answers the objection
        that a concept without an essence must fall apart.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   MOVE 4 - THE STATUS OF THE BOUNDARY  (PI 68-71, 75-77)
     68  The concept of number is a family too: cardinal, rational, real -
         related as fibres, not as species of one genus
     69  We can DRAW a boundary for a special purpose. 'We do not know the
         boundaries because none have been drawn' - not because they are
         hidden
```
