```text
     70  'But then the use of the word is unregulated!' - It is not
         everywhere circumscribed by rules; nor is tennis, and tennis is
         still a game
     71  The blurred concept: 'Is an indistinct photograph always
         preferable to a sharp one? Isn't the indistinct one often exactly
         what we need?'
   ==========================================================================
   FOUR THINGS THE DOCTRINE DOES NOT CLAIM  - each is a marked error
   X That NO concept has necessary and sufficient conditions
   X That no two games have anything in common
   X That definition is impossible or worthless
   X That anything may therefore be called a game (unlimited resemblance)
   ==========================================================================
   THE STANDARD OBJECTION, AND THE REPLY THE EXAMINER WANTS
   OBJECTION  Everything resembles everything in SOME respect. A network of
              'similarities' is therefore no constraint at all: a chair
              resembles chess in being man-made.
   REPLY      Relevance is fixed by the PRACTICE, not by bare resemblance:
              by what we do with games, how the word is taught, which
              extensions are accepted and which are corrected. The concept
              is bounded by USE, not by definition.
   RESIDUE    Whether 'use' bounds it tightly enough in every case is a
              genuine open question - concede it, and the answer earns the
              'critically' mark.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The 2018 ten-mark part asks how family resemblance **helps** Wittgenstein uphold that "Language is a game". The right-hand column of Move 4 is the answer's second half, and the Objection–Reply–Residue block is its conclusion.

#### 1. Why the doctrine is a *reply*, not a discovery ✅

✅ The setting is §65, where an imagined interlocutor confronts Wittgenstein with exactly the objection his own early work would have pressed: you talk about language-games but never say what the essence of a language-game — and so of language — is; you have let yourself off the very part of the investigation that once gave you most trouble. §66 is the answer to that challenge.

⚠️ This context is worth two sentences in any answer, because it fixes the doctrine's **job**. Family resemblance is not offered as a contribution to the theory of concepts; it is offered to show that Wittgenstein's refusal to define "language" is not an evasion. Once the demand for one essence is diagnosed as a demand rather than a requirement, the entire method of Sessions 2 and 5 becomes legitimate. ⚠️ That is why the 2018 question is phrased as "helps him to uphold" — it asks for the argumentative service the concept performs.

#### 2. The survey, done properly ✅

✅ Wittgenstein does not assert that games have nothing in common; he **conducts an inspection**: *"look for example at board-games, with their multifarious relationships. Now pass to card-games; here you find many correspondences with the first group, but many common features drop out, and others appear."* Ball-games retain much and lose much. In ring-a-ring-a-roses there is amusement but no competition and no winner. Patience is played alone. Throwing a ball at a wall against one's own record has skill and luck but no opponent.

⚠️ **The methodological point is more important than the list.** Any single candidate-feature can be defeated by producing a case, and the survey shows how: not by refuting a definition in the abstract but by walking through instances until the proposed common feature fails. This is precisely the "description, not explanation" of §109 in operation, and an answer that says so has connected the doctrine to the method.

#### 3. The thread: strength without a core ✅

✅ **§67:** *"And I shall say: 'games' form a family."* And of the extension of the number concept: *"we extend our concept of number as in spinning a thread we twist fibre on fibre. And the strength of the thread does not reside in the fact that some one fibre runs through its whole length, but in the overlapping of many fibres."*

⚠️ This is the image that does the argumentative work, because it answers the natural anxiety — *if there is no common core, what holds the concept together?* — with a counter-example from ordinary life. A rope is strong; no fibre runs its length. ⚠️ The image also fixes the doctrine's limits: a thread is not infinitely extensible, and fibres must actually overlap. Resemblance is not enough; **continuity of overlap within a practice** is what the analogy asserts.

#### 4. Open texture: exactly how far the concept is undetermined ✅

✅ **§68:** the concept of number is itself a family — cardinal, rational, real, complex — related through overlapping affinities, and one may say "the concept of number is defined as the logical sum of these", but that is a decision to draw a boundary, not the discovery of one. ✅ **§69:** *"How should we explain to someone what a game is? … And this is how we play it."* And: we do not know the boundaries **because none have been drawn** — though *"we can draw a boundary — for a special purpose"*. ✅ **§70–§71:** the interlocutor protests that an unbounded concept is unusable; Wittgenstein replies that the use of the word "game" is *not everywhere circumscribed by rules* — but neither is tennis, which has no rule for how high one may throw the ball, and tennis remains a game.

⚠️ **Open texture is not arbitrariness**, and this is the sentence that must appear in an answer. Training, examples, correction and established applications give family-resemblance concepts a **local normative shape** even without one essence. A candidate who writes "so anything can be called a game" has stated the objection and mistaken it for the doctrine.

#### 5. Why this licenses "language is a game" ⚠️

⚠️ Assembling the 2018 answer requires four connected steps, and it is worth writing them as steps:
1. **The demand disarmed.** Because a general term need not have an essence, the refusal to define "language" is not a defect in the account.
2. **The analogy earned.** "Game" is itself the paradigm family-resemblance concept, so calling linguistic activities *language-games* is not an evasion by metaphor — it is the application of the very concept that showed essences to be unnecessary.
3. **The positive picture supplied.** Games have training, rules, moves, standards and points **without** an essence. That is exactly the combination language was thought to require an essence to possess — so the analogy delivers precisely what the *Tractatus* wanted from logical form, without the metaphysics.
4. **The relativism blocked.** Since games are norm-governed, describing language as a family of games does not make meaning arbitrary; it locates the norms in practice rather than in an essence.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Family resemblance allows Wittgenstein to describe language as a family of games without owing anyone a definition of language, because it shows that a general term can be in perfect working order even where no feature is common to all its instances.
> **Named evidence.** §65 puts the objection in the interlocutor's mouth — that Wittgenstein nowhere says what the essence of a language-game is — and §66 replies with an instruction rather than a thesis: "Don't say: 'There must be something common' … but look and see." The survey of board-games, card-games, ball-games, Olympic games, ring-a-ring-a-roses and patience defeats amusement, competition, winning and losing, skill and luck in turn, leaving "a complicated network of similarities overlapping and criss-crossing"; §67 names the relation from the resemblances among members of a family and illustrates its strength by the spun thread, whose strength lies not in one fibre running the whole length but in the overlapping of many.
> **Analysis.** The doctrine therefore performs a specific argumentative service. It removes the Socratic requirement that competent users be able to state necessary and sufficient conditions, and in doing so it converts "language is a game" from a metaphor into a description: games possess training, rules, moves and standards without possessing an essence, which is exactly the combination the *Tractatus* believed only a shared logical form could supply. §69–71 fix the status of the resulting boundary — it is undrawn rather than hidden, may be drawn for a special purpose, and its absence no more disables "game" than the absence of a rule about throwing height disables tennis.
> **Qualification.** The claim is bounded in three ways that a careless answer loses: it does not assert that no concept admits of strict definition, since "triangle" plainly does; it does not assert that no two games share any feature; and it does not license the inference that anything may be called a game, because the relevant similarities are selected by the practice in which the word is taught and corrected. The strongest surviving objection is that bare resemblance is too cheap to constrain anything — everything resembles everything somehow — and Wittgenstein's reply, that relevance is fixed by use rather than by resemblance, is persuasive without being demonstrative.

#### Exam link

- **2018 Q1(c)** (10 marks) — *"How does the notion of 'family resemblance' help Wittgenstein to uphold that 'Language is a game'? Discuss."* §5's four steps are the skeleton of the answer; §3's thread image is the sentence that earns the analytical mark; §4's open-texture caution is the qualification.
- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks): the demand for one essence is precisely what colour exclusion and the missing simples had strained, so family resemblance is the constructive half of the "reasons for the shift".
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks): it explains why the later position does not simply install "use" as a new essence.
- **2022 Q1(c)** (10 marks): "form of life" is likewise a family-resemblance notion and must not be turned into a doctrine with sharp edges.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Family resemblance proves definitions are impossible" | It shows **not all** concepts require them; "triangle" and "prime" still do |
| "No two games share any property" | Many share several; there is simply no feature shared by **all** |
| "So anything can be called a game" | Relevance is selected by the practice; open texture is not arbitrariness |
| "It is a theory of vagueness" | §71 treats blurred edges as often **exactly what we need**, not as a defect |
| "Wittgenstein says we don't know where the boundary is" | §69: none has been **drawn**; we may draw one for a purpose |
| Listing games instead of arguing | The 2018 verb is "help … to uphold"; the marks are in the argumentative service |

#### Revision notes

- **§65** the interlocutor's challenge: you never say what the essence of language is. Family resemblance is the **reply**.
- **§66** *"Don't think, but look!"*; the survey defeats amusement, competition, winning/losing, skill, rules and luck in turn; result = *"a complicated network of similarities overlapping and criss-crossing"*.
- **§67** the name (**family resemblances**: build, features, eyes, gait, temperament) and the **thread**: strength from overlapping fibres, not one fibre running through.
- **§68** number is itself a family; **§69** boundaries are **undrawn**, not hidden, and may be drawn for a purpose; **§70** tennis has no rule for throwing height; **§71** the indistinct photograph is often what we need.
- Four denials: not "no concept has an essence"; not "no two games share anything"; not "definition is worthless"; not "anything counts".
- Objection: everything resembles everything. Reply: **practice selects the relevant similarities**. Residue: whether it always does so tightly enough.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — FAMILY RESEMBLANCE

```text
CHALLENGE (65): 'You never say what the ESSENCE of language is'
   |
   v
INSTRUCTION (66): 'Don't think, but LOOK!'  - the word MUST is the disease
   |
   +--> SURVEY: board · card · ball · Olympic · ring-a-ring · patience ·
   |      ball-at-wall.  amusement X  competition X  win/lose X  skill X
   |      rules X  luck X
   |
   v
RESULT (66): a complicated network of SIMILARITIES OVERLAPPING AND
   CRISS-CROSSING
   |
   v
NAME + ANALOGY (67): FAMILY RESEMBLANCE; the THREAD - strength from
   overlapping fibres, no single fibre running the whole length
   |
   +--> BOUNDARY (68-71): undrawn, not hidden · drawable for a purpose ·
   |      not everywhere circumscribed - nor is tennis · blurred edges are
   |      often exactly what we need
   |
   v
SERVICE RENDERED: no essence is REQUIRED  ->  'language is a game' is a
   description, not an evasion  ->  games have rules, training, moves and
   standards WITHOUT an essence
   |
   v
OBJECTION everything resembles everything | REPLY practice selects relevant
   similarities | RESIDUE does use always bound it tightly enough?
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the demand for one essence is removed, not satisfied - and the
   concept survives, as a rope survives without a single fibre
```

### SESSION 5 — LANGUAGE-GAMES: LANGUAGE WOVEN INTO ACTIVITY, THE BUILDERS, AND GRAMMAR

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** A language-game is not a game *about* language. It is any stretch of language taken together with the activity it belongs to — giving an order and obeying it, describing an object, reporting a measurement, telling a joke, asking, thanking, cursing, praying. Wittgenstein invents simple ones, describes real ones, and puts them side by side, because seeing two different uses next to each other cures the illusion that there must be one thing that all uses are.

**Technical definition:** A **language-game (*Sprachspiel*)** is *"the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven"* (**§7**). The term is used for three distinct purposes and an answer should separate them: (i) for **invented primitive languages** used as objects of comparison (§2's builders); (ii) for **the processes of naming and repeating words** by which children are trained into language (§7); and (iii) for the **actual, complete practices** of a functioning language, of which §23 gives a long, deliberately heterogeneous list. **Grammar**, in Wittgenstein's extended sense, is the body of rules and distinctions that fix what makes sense within such a practice — and grammatical remarks, unlike empirical ones, describe what we call a thing rather than what it is like.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The language-game is not an analogy borrowed to decorate a theory of meaning; it is an instrument of comparison, and its whole point is that placing two uses side by side does what no definition of "language" could do — it makes the craving for a single essence visibly unnecessary.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven" (§7)** — the definition, quoted
- **objects of comparison (§130–§131)** — the exact methodological status of invented games
- **the builders: "Slab!", "Block!", "Pillar!", "Beam!" (§2)** — the worked case
- **§23's list** — ordering, describing, reporting, speculating, play-acting, joking, thanking, cursing, greeting, praying
- **"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life" (§23)** — the bridge to Session 6
- **grammar / surface and depth grammar (§664)** — the tool that exposes category-confusion

**How to use them:** define the term with **§7** quoted, then immediately state the **object-of-comparison** status from §130–131, because that single move blocks the objection that Wittgenstein has replaced a theory with an anecdote; use the **builders** as the worked case rather than as a story; and quote **§23**'s closing clause to make the transition to forms of life a citation instead of an assertion.

#### Plain-language visual — one order, one action, one complete language

```text
   THE BUILDERS' LANGUAGE  (PI 2)
   ==========================================================================
     A (the builder)                       B (the assistant)
        |                                     ^
        |  calls out  'Slab!'                 |  brings a slab
        +-------------------------------------+
        |  calls out  'Block!'                |  brings a block
        |  calls out  'Pillar!'               |  brings a pillar
        |  calls out  'Beam!'                 |  brings a beam
   ==========================================================================
   FOUR WORDS.  NO GRAMMAR OF THE SCHOOLBOOK KIND.  NO SENTENCES.
   AND YET: 'Conceive this as a COMPLETE primitive language.'
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT THE EXAMPLE ESTABLISHES - four things, all examinable
   1 A language can be complete FOR ITS PURPOSE without describing anything
   2 The words are not names in the Augustinian sense: 'Slab!' is nearer to
     an ORDER than to a label. Its meaning is its role in the transaction
   3 Meaning is displayed in the TRAINED RESPONSE - B's bringing the slab
   4 A description of Augustine's picture fits THIS game exactly. That is
     the picture's true status: one game, not the essence of all
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT THE EXAMPLE DOES NOT ESTABLISH
   X That real languages have no grammar or structure
   X That primitive is 'better' or more fundamental
   X That every language-game is as simple as this one
   OBJECTS OF COMPARISON (130-131): invented games are 'set up as objects of
   comparison which are meant to throw light on the features of our language
   by way not only of similarities, but also of DISSIMILARITIES'
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** the builders are not a theory of language and not a claim about how language began. They are a measuring stick laid alongside our language. Held up against them, the question "but what do all these words *stand for*?" starts to look like asking what a hammer and a glue-pot both point at.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| §7 defines the term to include **the actions** | not the words alone, and not the words plus a context |
| Invented games are **objects of comparison** | not hypotheses about origins or about children |
| The builders' language is **complete** | complete for its purpose — that is the whole of the claim |
| §23's list is deliberately **heterogeneous** | the disorder is the argument |
| Games are norm-governed | rules by **training and correction**, often without a rulebook |

> **One-line memory:** language-games are not what language is *like*; they are how language is *looked at*.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the three uses of the term, §23's list, and what "grammar" means here

```text
   USE 1  INVENTED PRIMITIVE LANGUAGES         PI 2, 8, 48, 130-131
     Purpose: OBJECTS OF COMPARISON. Not simplified models of our
     language, not hypotheses about its origin. They illuminate BY
     DIFFERENCE as much as by likeness.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   USE 2  THE TRAINING PROCESSES BY WHICH LANGUAGE IS LEARNED        PI 7
     Naming, repeating after someone, pointing and saying. 'I shall also
     call the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it
     is woven, the language-game.'
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   USE 3  ACTUAL COMPLETE PRACTICES                                  PI 23
     'Giving orders, and obeying them - Describing the appearance of an
      object, or giving its measurements - Constructing an object from a
      description (a drawing) - Reporting an event - Speculating about an
      event - Forming and testing a hypothesis - Presenting the results of
      an experiment in tables and diagrams - Making up a story; and reading
      it - Play-acting - Singing catches - Guessing riddles - Making a joke;
      telling it - Solving a problem in practical arithmetic - Translating
      from one language into another - Asking, thanking, cursing, greeting,
      praying.'
     AND THE CLOSING CLAUSE THAT MATTERS MOST:
     'the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a FORM OF LIFE'
   ==========================================================================
   WHY THE LIST IS DISORDERLY ON PURPOSE
     A tidy taxonomy would suggest an underlying principle of division.
     The list mixes the trivial with the solemn, the theoretical with the
     bodily, precisely to defeat that suggestion. 23 also says: 'this
     multiplicity is not something fixed, given once for all; but new types
     of language, new language-games, as we may say, come into existence,
     and others become obsolete and get forgotten.'
   ==========================================================================
   GRAMMAR - WITTGENSTEIN'S EXTENDED SENSE
     * Rules and distinctions that fix what MAKES SENSE in a practice
     * 371 'ESSENCE is expressed by grammar'  |  373 'Grammar tells what
       kind of object anything is'  -> the question 'what IS an X?' becomes
       'how is the word X used?'
     * 496 grammar is not an explanation but a DESCRIPTION of the use of
       signs
     * 664 SURFACE grammar (how a word is constructed and sounds) vs DEPTH
       grammar (its actual employment). 'I mean...' and 'I intend...' look
       alike on the surface; their depth grammar differs
     * The diagnostic use: 'It is 5 o'clock on the sun' (350-351) is
       grammatically impeccable and has no determined use. The trouble is
       not falsity - it is that no game has been specified
   ==========================================================================
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The 2018 part needs Use 3 and the §23 closing clause; the 2023 and 2025 transition parts need Use 1 and the object-of-comparison status; every private-language answer needs the grammar row, because the whole third limb is a grammatical investigation of sensation-words.

#### 1. The definition, and the reason for the word "game" ✅

✅ **§7:** having described the builders and the training processes, Wittgenstein writes: *"I shall also call the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven, the 'language-game'."* The unit of investigation is therefore never the sentence alone. It is sentence-plus-circumstance-plus-activity-plus-response.

⚠️ **Why "game" and not "practice" or "institution"?** Because games are the paradigm of something rule-governed without an essence (Session 4) — which is exactly what Wittgenstein needs language to be. The word also carries three further connotations he uses: that there are **moves**, that there is **training**, and that a move is intelligible only within the game. ❌ It does **not** carry the connotations of triviality, of arbitrary invention or of competition, and the objection "language is too serious to be a game" trades on precisely the connotations he does not use.

#### 2. The builders, worked rather than told ✅

✅ **§2:** *"Let us imagine a language … The language is meant to serve for communication between a builder A and an assistant B. A is building with building-stones: there are blocks, pillars, slabs and beams. B has to pass the stones, and that in the order in which A needs them. For this purpose they use a language consisting of the words 'block', 'pillar', 'slab', 'beam'. A calls them out; — B brings the stone which he has learnt to bring at such-and-such a call. — Conceive this as a complete primitive language."*

The four things the example establishes are set out in the plain-language visual above. ⚠️ The subtlest is the second. Is "Slab!" a word or a sentence? §19 raises exactly this and refuses to settle it by fiat: it may be an elliptical form of "Bring me a slab", or the "elliptical" sentence in our language may be a lengthened form of theirs. §20 draws the moral — whether a word functions as an order does not depend on its length but on its place in the practice. ⚠️ This is where the example turns from illustration into argument, and where a script that merely narrates it loses the mark.

#### 3. The heterogeneous list, and the historicity of games ✅

✅ **§23** first asks how many kinds of sentence there are and answers: *"there are countless kinds"* — and, crucially, *"this multiplicity is not something fixed, given once for all; but new types of language, new language-games, as we may say, come into existence, and others become obsolete and get forgotten."* Then comes the list — orders, descriptions, measurements, constructing from a drawing, reporting, speculating, hypothesis, tables and diagrams, story, play-acting, catches, riddles, jokes, practical arithmetic, translation, asking, thanking, cursing, greeting, praying — and the closing clause: *"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life."*

⚠️ Three examinable consequences follow, and they are worth numbering in an answer:
1. **Against one essence** — the list's disorder is the argument, since a common feature covering praying, measuring and cursing is not going to be found by looking harder.
2. **Against the primacy of description** — describing is *one item on the list*, not the paradigm of which the rest are deviations. The *Tractatus*, and logical positivism after it, had made fact-stating the centre.
3. **Language is historical** — games arise and die. This is what allows Wittgenstein to treat conceptual change without a theory of conceptual change.

#### 4. Rules, training, and why this is not conventionalism ✅

✅ Language-games are norm-governed, but the norms are typically instilled by **training, examples and correction** rather than stated in a rulebook. §54 makes the distinction explicitly: a rule may be taught explicitly, or it may be learned as one learns a game — by watching how others play, and by being corrected. §31 (Session 3) has already shown that explicit statement cannot be the foundation in any case.

⚠️ **Three cautions that keep this from collapsing into conventionalism.** First, "rule-governed" does not mean "everywhere circumscribed by rules" (§68, §84): games have unregulated regions and remain games. Second, the participants do not choose the rules the way a committee chooses a constitution; they are trained into them. Third — and this is the point that separates Wittgenstein from a merely sociological account — the possibility of **correction** is what makes something a rule at all, and correction is not the same as majority behaviour. Session 7 supplies the argument; here it is enough to mark that "game" implies **standards**, not caprice.

#### 5. Grammar, surface and depth ✅

✅ **§371:** *"Essence is expressed by grammar."* ✅ **§373:** *"Grammar tells what kind of object anything is."* The metaphysical question *what is a number? what is time? what is the mind?* is thereby transformed into a question about the use of a word — which is the therapeutic method (Session 11) in its working form. ✅ **§496:** grammar does not explain but **describes** the use of signs. ✅ **§664:** in the use of a word one may distinguish **surface grammar** — how it is constructed, what part of speech it appears to be — from **depth grammar**, its actual employment. "I mean this" and "I intend this" have similar surface grammar and different depth grammar.

⚠️ **The diagnostic pay-off.** §350–351 supply the standard case: "It is 5 o'clock on the sun" is impeccable on the surface and has no settled application, because our practice of telling the time is tied to local noon on the earth. The sentence is not **false**; no game has been specified in which it makes a move. ⚠️ This is the exact form the private-language diagnosis will take: the diarist's "S" is not a lie and not a falsehood, it is a sign for which no game has been provided.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The language-game is best understood as an instrument of comparison rather than as a theory: by taking as the unit of investigation "the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven", Wittgenstein makes the diversity of linguistic function visible and thereby makes the demand for a single essence look like a craving rather than a requirement.
> **Named evidence.** §2 constructs the builders' language of "block", "pillar", "slab" and "beam" and instructs us to conceive it as a *complete* primitive language; §7 gives the definition, and §19–20 refuse to settle by fiat whether "Slab!" is a word or a sentence, since what makes it an order is its place in the transaction rather than its length. §23 states that there are countless kinds of sentence, that new games arise and old ones are forgotten, and lists orders, descriptions, measurements, reports, hypotheses, tables, stories, play-acting, riddles, jokes, translation, asking, thanking, cursing, greeting and praying, before concluding that "the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life". §130–131 fix the status of the invented cases as objects of comparison meant to illuminate by dissimilarity as much as by likeness.
> **Analysis.** Three consequences follow directly. The disorder of §23's list is itself the argument against one essence, since no feature covering praying, measuring and cursing will be found by looking harder. Describing appears in the list as one item, which demotes fact-stating from the paradigm it had been in the *Tractatus* and in logical positivism. And because games are norm-governed through training and correction rather than through codification, the account supplies standards without an essence — which is what the *Tractatus* had believed only a shared logical form could do.
> **Qualification.** The analogy must not be pressed where Wittgenstein does not press it: language-games are not trivial, not invented at will, and not everywhere circumscribed by rules, any more than tennis is; nor are the invented languages hypotheses about the origin of language or about child development. Their function is comparative, and an account that treats them as simplified models of our own language has already lost the methodological point.

#### Exam link

- **2018 Q1(c)** (10 marks): the second half of the answer — family resemblance removes the essence-demand, and the language-game supplies the positive description that replaces it.
- **2022 Q1(c)** (10 marks): §23's closing clause is the textual bridge to "form of life" and should be quoted there.
- **2023 Q4(c)** and **2025 Q3(a)**: §130–131 explain what replaced analysis — comparison, not a rival theory.
- **2020 Q4(c)**, **2024 Q1(d)**: the grammar row (§350–351, §664) is the model for the private-language verdict: not false, but no game specified.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Language-games are games about language" | §7: language **plus the actions into which it is woven** |
| "The builders' language is a model of real language" | §130–131: an **object of comparison**, illuminating by dissimilarity too |
| "Language-games are arbitrary, invented at will" | They are entered by **training**; correction is possible; standards are real |
| "Wittgenstein says language has no rules" | It is not **everywhere** circumscribed by rules — nor is tennis (§68, §84) |
| Treating description as the paradigm case | §23 lists describing as **one** kind among many; that demotion is the point |
| Reading §23 as a taxonomy | Its disorder is deliberate and is itself the argument |

#### Revision notes

- **§7** definition: *the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven*.
- **§2** builders: "block", "pillar", "slab", "beam"; a **complete primitive language**; **§19–20** whether "Slab!" is word or sentence is settled by **role**, not length.
- **§23** countless kinds; the multiplicity is **not fixed**; games arise and are forgotten; the list (orders, measurements, hypotheses, tables, story, play-acting, riddles, jokes, arithmetic, translation, asking, thanking, cursing, greeting, praying); closing clause: **part of an activity, or of a form of life**.
- **§130–131** invented games are **objects of comparison** — by dissimilarity as much as similarity.
- **Grammar:** §371 essence is expressed by grammar · §373 grammar tells what kind of object anything is · §496 description not explanation · §664 **surface** vs **depth** grammar.
- Diagnostic model: **§350–351** "It is 5 o'clock on the sun" — impeccable surface, no specified game.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — LANGUAGE-GAMES

```text
UNIT OF INVESTIGATION (7) = LANGUAGE + THE ACTIONS INTO WHICH IT IS WOVEN
   |
   +--> USE 1  invented primitive languages  (2, 8, 48)
   |      builders: 'Slab!' 'Block!' 'Pillar!' 'Beam!' - COMPLETE for its
   |      purpose; meaning shown in the TRAINED RESPONSE; 19-20 word or
   |      sentence is settled by ROLE, not by length
   |      STATUS (130-131): OBJECTS OF COMPARISON, illuminating by
   |      DISSIMILARITY as much as by likeness
   |
   +--> USE 2  the training processes by which language is learned (7)
   |
   +--> USE 3  actual practices (23): orders · descriptions · measurements ·
   |      reports · hypotheses · tables · stories · play-acting · riddles ·
   |      jokes · arithmetic · translation · asking · thanking · cursing ·
   |      greeting · praying     ->  'part of an activity, or of a FORM OF LIFE'
   |      and: NEW GAMES ARISE, OLD ONES ARE FORGOTTEN
   |
   v
GRAMMAR: 371 essence is expressed by grammar | 373 what kind of object
   anything is | 496 description, not explanation | 664 SURFACE vs DEPTH
   |
   +--> DIAGNOSTIC (350-351): 'It is 5 o'clock on the sun' - not false; NO
   |      GAME SPECIFIED.  This is the exact form the verdict on 'S' takes
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: comparison replaces analysis, and the multiplicity that
   defeats one essence is the same multiplicity that keeps meaning ordered
```

### SESSION 6 — FORMS OF LIFE: AGREEMENT IN JUDGMENTS, THE LION, AND BEDROCK

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Language-games do not float. They are played by creatures who share reactions, needs, activities and ways of going on: we point and follow the finger rather than staring at the hand, we find the same things similar, we count and measure and get the same results, we wince at the same injuries. That shared background, which is mostly not stated and mostly not chosen, is what Wittgenstein gestures at with "form of life".

**Technical definition:** A **form of life (*Lebensform*)** is the shared pattern of activity, natural reaction, training and settled response that a language-game presupposes and within which linguistic agreement is possible. The term is deliberately sparse: it occurs only a handful of times in the *Investigations* — at **§19**, **§23**, **§241**, and in the material now printed as *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment*. Its three load-bearing statements are: *"to imagine a language means to imagine a form of life"* (**§19**); *"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life"* (**§23**); and the answer to the interlocutor's charge of relativism at **§241**: *"It is what human beings say that is true and false; and they agree in the language they use. That is not agreement in opinions, but in form of life."* **§242** adds the further condition: agreement in **definitions** and, however strange it sounds, agreement in **judgments**.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> "Form of life" is Wittgenstein's name for what must already be shared before agreement or disagreement is even possible, and the reason it cannot be turned into a doctrine of cultural relativism is that a relativist needs the very thing it describes — a stable practice within which claims can conflict.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"To imagine a language means to imagine a form of life" (§19)** — the shortest quotable statement
- **agreement in judgments, not in opinions (§241–242)** — the discrimination that carries the tenability mark
- **"If a lion could talk, we could not understand him"** — cited to Part II / PPF, not to Part I
- **"This is simply what I do" (§217)** — bedrock, with its correct location in rule-following
- **natural history (§25, §415)** — the naturalist reading's textual anchor
- **the sparse-occurrence caution** — mark the interpretation as interpretation

**How to use them:** the 2022 part says *"establish the tenability"*, which is a demand for a **defended thesis**; so state the discrimination between agreement in opinions and agreement in judgments in the first two sentences, since it is what makes the notion tenable rather than merely evocative; and place the sparse-occurrence caution near the end, where it reads as scholarship rather than as hedging.

#### Plain-language visual — what has to be in place before anyone can agree or disagree

```text
   TWO PEOPLE DISAGREE ABOUT THE LENGTH OF A TABLE
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT THEY DISAGREE ABOUT ......... whether it is 180 cm or 182 cm
                                       an AGREEMENT IN OPINIONS is absent
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   WHAT THEY MUST ALREADY SHARE FOR THE DISAGREEMENT TO EXIST AT ALL
     * that laying a rule alongside an object is how length is settled
     * that the marks on the rule are read in this direction, not that
     * that a result is repeatable, and a wildly varying result means
       something went wrong
     * that 'longer' and 'shorter' apply as they do to these cases
     * that when we count together we mostly reach the same number
   THIS is AGREEMENT IN JUDGMENTS - and it is not a shared opinion, because
   nobody was ever asked. It is the trained practice inside which opinions
   become possible.
   ==========================================================================
   PI 242, in substance: communication by language requires agreement not
   only in DEFINITIONS but - queer as it may sound - in JUDGMENTS. If
   measuring results varied wildly from person to person, there would be no
   such thing as measuring - not 'inaccurate measuring'.
   ==========================================================================
   THE TEST THAT MAKES THE NOTION TENABLE
     Remove the shared background -> you do not get DISAGREEMENT
                                  -> you get NO SUBJECT-MATTER AT ALL
     That asymmetry is the whole argument, and it is what a relativist
     reading of 'form of life' cannot accommodate.
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** two chess players can argue about whether a move was wise. They cannot argue about whether the bishop moves diagonally — not because that is beyond dispute in some mystical sense, but because someone who denies it is not disagreeing with them, he is not playing. Form of life is Wittgenstein's name for the level at which denying something removes you from the game rather than putting you on the other side of it.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The term is **sparse** | a handful of occurrences; do not build a system and call it his |
| Agreement in **judgments**, not opinions | the discrimination that answers the relativism charge |
| It includes **natural** as well as cultural facts | §25, §415: commanding, questioning and playing are part of our natural history |
| §217's bedrock belongs to **rule-following** | quote it there, and only extend it here with a marker |
| The lion remark is from **Part II / PPF** | not from the numbered Part I |

> **One-line memory:** you cannot disagree with someone until you already agree with him about a great deal that neither of you has ever mentioned.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the four things "form of life" is doing, and the two readings of it

```text
   JOB 1  IT COMPLETES THE ACCOUNT OF LANGUAGE-GAMES               19, 23
     A game needs players, training and circumstances. 'To imagine a
     language means to imagine a form of life.' 23: 'the speaking of
     language is PART OF an activity, or of a form of life.'
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   JOB 2  IT ANSWERS THE RELATIVISM CHARGE                       241, 242
     Interlocutor: 'So you are saying that human agreement decides what is
     true and what is false?'
     Reply: 'It is what human beings SAY that is true and false; and they
     agree in the LANGUAGE they use. That is not agreement in OPINIONS but
     in FORM OF LIFE.'
     242: communication needs agreement in DEFINITIONS and - queer as it
     sounds - in JUDGMENTS. If results of measuring varied wildly, there
     would be no measuring, not inaccurate measuring.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   JOB 3  IT MARKS WHERE JUSTIFICATION STOPS                   217, 211
     'If I have exhausted the justifications I have reached bedrock, and my
     spade is turned. Then I am inclined to say: THIS IS SIMPLY WHAT I DO.'
     CAUTION: this is said of RULE-FOLLOWING. Using it to define 'form of
     life' is a common and marked slide - mark it as an extension.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   JOB 4  IT SETS THE LIMIT OF INTELLIGIBILITY                 PPF / II xi
     'If a lion could talk, we could not understand him.'
     WHAT IT SAYS: vocabulary alone does not confer understanding where
     activities, reactions and natural history diverge far enough.
     WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY: that cultures are mutually sealed, that
     translation is impossible, or that criticism across practices is
     illegitimate. Lions are not a culture.
   ==========================================================================
   THE TWO READINGS - NAME BOTH, THEN TAKE A SIDE
   NATURALIST / ANTHROPOLOGICAL          CULTURALIST
     'form of life' = the shared           'forms of life' (plural) =
     NATURAL HISTORY of human beings:      historically specific practices:
     shared reactions, the human body,     religious, scientific, legal
     common needs, primitive behaviour     forms of discourse
     ANCHORS: 25 'commanding, asking,      ANCHORS: the plural occurrences;
     chatting are as much a part of our    23's historicity ('new games come
     natural history as walking, eating,   into existence, others become
     drinking, playing'; 415 'what we      obsolete'); the manifest variety
     are supplying are really remarks      of practices
     on the natural history of human
     beings'
   ==========================================================================
   VERDICT TO DEPLOY  The readings are not exclusive: the SHAPE of the
   background is natural, its CONTENT is largely cultural. What the text
   does not supply is a theory of adjudication between practices - and
   that gap, not relativism, is the honest limitation to concede.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The 2022 part asks you to **establish the tenability** of language as form of life. Jobs 1–3 establish it; Job 4 and the two readings are where the "critically" content lives; the verdict line at the bottom is the concession that makes the defence credible.

#### 1. What §19 actually says, and what it does not ✅

✅ **§19** begins with imagined languages — one consisting only of orders and reports in battle, one of only questions and expressions for answering yes and no — and says: *"And to imagine a language means to imagine a form of life."* The context is therefore the **builders**: the sentence arrives while Wittgenstein is asking whether "Slab!" is a word or a sentence.

⚠️ **The consequence of that context is examinable.** The remark is not a grand thesis about culture; it is the observation that you cannot specify a language without specifying what its speakers *do*. A language of orders and reports in battle presupposes battle, ranks, obedience, danger. Change the doing, and the language is a different language — which is why it makes no sense to describe a vocabulary and then ask, separately, what activity it belongs to.

#### 2. Agreement in judgments — the passage that does the real work ✅

✅ **§241** stages the objection directly. The interlocutor says: *"So you are saying that human agreement decides what is true and what is false?"* Wittgenstein's reply: *"It is what human beings say that is true and false; and they agree in the language they use. That is not agreement in opinions but in form of life."*

✅ **§242** continues: *"If language is to be a means of communication there must be agreement not only in definitions but also (queer as this may sound) in judgments."* And the measuring example: if the results of measuring varied wildly from person to person, we would not have *inaccurate* measuring; we would have no measuring at all.

⚠️ **Why this is the tenability argument.** The relativist reading says: agreement makes things true. Wittgenstein's reply distinguishes two levels. What is **true or false** is what people *say*, and that is settled by how things are — the table is 180 cm or it is not. What agreement supplies is the **practice within which saying anything determinate is possible**. Remove the agreement and you do not get widespread error; you get the disappearance of the subject-matter. ⚠️ That asymmetry — error versus no-subject-matter — is the sentence to write, because it shows why "agreement in form of life" is not the thesis that truth is consensus.

#### 3. Bedrock, and where it belongs ✅

✅ **§217:** *"'How am I able to obey a rule?' … If I have exhausted the justifications I have reached bedrock, and my spade is turned. Then I am inclined to say: 'This is simply what I do.'"* ✅ **§211:** in giving reasons for continuing a series as I do, my reasons will soon give out — and then I shall act, without reasons.

⚠️ **Two cautions, both frequently violated.** First, §217 is a remark about **rule-following**, and although it is legitimately connected to the form-of-life background, using it as a *definition* of form of life is an interpretive extension that should be marked as one. Second, "this is simply what I do" is **not** "this is simply what we all do, so majority behaviour is the standard". Wittgenstein is describing the terminus of *justification*, not proposing a criterion of correctness. Session 7 shows why the difference matters: a community-behaviour criterion would reintroduce the very collapse of "right" into "seems right" that the private-language argument exploits, only at group level.

#### 4. The lion, correctly cited and correctly limited ✅

✅ *"If a lion could talk, we could not understand him."* The remark belongs to the material formerly printed as Part II §xi, now *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment*; cite it that way rather than by a Part I section number.

⚠️ **What it establishes.** Understanding an utterance requires more than decoding a vocabulary: it requires enough shared activity, reaction and natural history for us to know what the speaker is *doing*. A lion's life — its interests, its body, its behaviour — offers too little purchase; even fluent lion-speech would leave us unable to tell an assertion from a threat, a joke from a complaint.

❌ **What it does not establish.** It is not a proof that cultures are mutually unintelligible, and Wittgenstein's own remarks work in the opposite direction: the *Investigations* repeatedly imagines exotic tribes and then **describes** what they are doing (§143–207 passim), which presupposes that anthropological understanding is possible. ⚠️ The honest formulation is that intelligibility has **conditions** and that those conditions can be more or less well met — a caution against assuming universal transparency, not a doctrine of sealed worlds.

#### 5. Establishing tenability: the defended thesis ⚠️

⚠️ The 2022 directive is unusual and should be obeyed literally: *establish the tenability*. That means the answer must (a) state what would make the notion untenable, (b) show that it does not follow, and (c) concede what genuinely remains open.

| What would make it untenable | Why it does not follow | What remains open |
|---|---|---|
| If "form of life" meant that consensus makes statements true | §241 separates *what is said* (true or false) from *the language they agree in*; §242's asymmetry shows removal yields no subject-matter, not mass error | Whether the line between the two levels is always sharp |
| If it made every practice immune to criticism | Shared natural history and cross-practice comparison give purchase; Wittgenstein himself describes and criticises practices | Wittgenstein supplies no theory of rational adjudication between practices |
| If it were a doctrine built on a word used a handful of times | The work is done by §241–242 and §23, which are argued, not by the phrase itself | How much systematic weight the phrase can bear is genuinely contested |
| If "natural history" made it biological determinism | §25 and §415 place linguistic activities alongside walking and eating as *natural history*, which is a remark about the kind of description offered, not a causal thesis | The naturalist/culturalist balance is an interpretive dispute |

⚠️ **The verdict to write:** the notion is tenable as a description of the **enabling background** of language and untenable as a **theory** of truth, of cultural incommensurability, or of the ultimate justification of practices — and Wittgenstein's own sparse use of the phrase is evidence that he intended the first and not the second.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Language as a form of life is tenable when it is read as a claim about the enabling background of linguistic practice rather than as a theory of truth, because what agreement supplies is not the truth of what is said but the possibility of saying anything determinate at all.
> **Named evidence.** §19 states that to imagine a language means to imagine a form of life, and does so while asking whether the builders' "Slab!" is a word or a sentence — that is, while showing that a vocabulary cannot be specified apart from an activity. §23 closes its list of countless language-games with the clause that speaking a language is part of an activity, or of a form of life. §241 answers the charge that human agreement decides truth by distinguishing agreement in opinions from agreement in form of life, and §242 adds that communication requires agreement not only in definitions but, queer as it sounds, in judgments — for if the results of measuring varied wildly from person to person, we should not have inaccurate measuring but no measuring at all.
> **Analysis.** The measuring example carries the argument, because it exhibits an asymmetry a relativist reading cannot use: the removal of shared judgments does not produce widespread error, which would presuppose a subject-matter, but the disappearance of the subject-matter itself. Agreement therefore operates one level below truth-value, as the condition of determinate assertion rather than as its ground, and this is why disagreement — including radical disagreement — is evidence of a shared practice rather than of its absence.
> **Qualification.** Three limits must be conceded. The phrase occurs only a handful of times, so an elaborate doctrine erected on it should be presented as interpretation rather than as Wittgenstein's stated theory. The lion remark, which belongs to the material now printed as *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment*, warns that intelligibility has conditions but does not establish that cultures are sealed from one another, since Wittgenstein himself repeatedly describes imagined alien practices. And while shared natural history and cross-practice comparison constrain relativism, the text supplies no theory of rational adjudication between practices — which is the genuine gap, and a more honest concession than the relativism charge it is usually confused with.

#### Exam link

- **2022 Q1(c)** (10 marks) — *"Establish the tenability of later Wittgenstein's motion of language as form of life."* ⚠️ The official English line prints **"motion"**; reproduce the printed word, then say in one clause that the intended notion is language as a form of life, and proceed. §5's table is the answer's structure.
- **2018 Q1(c)**: forms of life are what stop the family-resemblance account from collapsing into "anything goes".
- **2019 Q3(b)**, **2020 Q4(c)**, **2024 Q1(d)**: the private-language argument needs a standard of correctness that is not the present impression; the form-of-life background is where such standards live.
- **2025 Q3(a)**: this is the terminus of the transition — the *Tractatus*'s logical form is replaced not by another formal structure but by a shared practical background.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Form of life = cultural relativism" | §241: agreement in **form of life**, not in opinions; removal yields no subject-matter, not mass error |
| "Human agreement decides truth" | What is true or false is what human beings **say**; agreement is in the **language** they use |
| "§217 defines form of life" | §217 is a **rule-following** remark; extending it here is interpretation and must be marked |
| "The lion remark proves incommensurability" | It states a condition of intelligibility; Wittgenstein describes imagined alien practices throughout |
| Silently correcting the 2022 printed "motion" | Reproduce the printed wording, gloss it in a clause, and move on |
| Building a large doctrine on the phrase | It occurs a handful of times (§19, §23, §241, PPF); the arguments are at §241–242 |

#### Revision notes

- **§19** *"To imagine a language means to imagine a form of life"* — said in the context of the builders.
- **§23** *"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life"*.
- **§241** agreement in **form of life**, not in **opinions**; what is true or false is what human beings **say**.
- **§242** agreement in **definitions** *and* in **judgments**; wildly varying measurement results = **no measuring**, not inaccurate measuring.
- **§217** bedrock, spade turned, *"This is simply what I do"* — a **rule-following** remark.
- **§25, §415** commanding, questioning, chatting belong to our **natural history**.
- Lion remark: **Part II §xi / PPF** — a condition of intelligibility, not sealed cultures.
- Verdict: tenable as the **enabling background**; untenable as a theory of truth, incommensurability or ultimate justification.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — FORMS OF LIFE

```text
LANGUAGE-GAME (23) is PART OF an activity, or of a FORM OF LIFE
   |
   +--> 19  to imagine a language = to imagine a form of life
   |      (said while asking whether 'Slab!' is a word or a sentence)
   |
   v
CHALLENGE (241): 'So human agreement decides what is true and false?'
   |
   v
REPLY: what is TRUE OR FALSE is what human beings SAY; what they AGREE in is
   the LANGUAGE they use -> 'not agreement in OPINIONS but in FORM OF LIFE'
   |
   +--> 242  agreement in DEFINITIONS *and* in JUDGMENTS
   |      TEST: wildly varying measurements = NO MEASURING, not inaccurate
   |      measuring  ->  removal gives NO SUBJECT-MATTER, not mass error
   |
   +--> 217  justifications exhausted, spade turned, 'This is simply what I
   |      do'   [a RULE-FOLLOWING remark - extending it here is interpretation]
   |
   +--> LION (PPF / II xi): vocabulary alone does not confer understanding
   |      NOT: cultures are sealed. Wittgenstein describes alien practices
   |
   v
TWO READINGS: NATURALIST (25, 415 natural history) | CULTURALIST (plural
   occurrences, 23's historicity).  SHAPE natural, CONTENT largely cultural
   |
   v
TENABLE AS  the enabling background of determinate speech
UNTENABLE AS  a theory of truth · of incommensurability · of ultimate
   justification
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: you cannot disagree with someone until you already agree with
   him about a great deal that neither of you has ever mentioned
```

### SESSION 7 — RULE-FOLLOWING AND THE PARADOX AT §201: THE REGRESS OF INTERPRETATIONS

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Suppose you are taught "add 2" and you write 2, 4, 6, 8 … up to 1000, and then continue 1004, 1008, 1012. You insist you are doing exactly what you were told. Nothing in the examples you were given rules this out — you were never shown what happens after 1000. Wittgenstein's problem is that this is not an odd case: *every* rule can be read in more than one way, so a rule cannot fix its own application by itself. His answer is not a better rule. It is that following a rule is something you do, not something you interpret.

**Technical definition:** The **rule-following considerations** (**§138–242**, with the decisive statement at **§201**) show that no formulation of a rule, and no finite set of examples, determines a unique continuation, because any continuation can be brought into accord with the rule under some interpretation. **§201** states the paradox — *"this was our paradox: no course of action could be determined by a rule, because every course of action can be made out to accord with the rule"* — and immediately identifies the misunderstanding that generates it: that we are giving one interpretation after another, *"as if each one contented us at least for a moment, until we thought of yet another standing behind it."* The resolution is that there is *"a way of grasping a rule which is **not** an interpretation, but which is exhibited in what we call 'obeying the rule' and 'going against it' in actual cases."*

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The rule-following paradox is not a sceptical result but a reductio of a picture: if grasping a rule were attaching an interpretation to it, no rule could ever determine anything — and since rules plainly do determine things, grasping a rule must be something other than interpreting it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **regress of interpretations** — the mechanism, named in three words
- **"every course of action can be made out to accord with the rule" (§201)** — quoted
- **a way of grasping a rule which is not an interpretation (§201)** — the resolution, quoted
- **"add 2" / the pupil at 1000 (§185)** — the worked case
- **"This is simply what I do" (§217)** — bedrock
- **normativity ≠ regularity** — the discrimination that blocks the behaviourist and the consensus reading

**How to use them:** state the paradox as a **conditional** ("*if* grasping a rule were interpreting it, then …"), because that single grammatical choice already signals that you know it is a reductio; then give §185's worked case; then quote the resolution; and reserve **normativity ≠ regularity** for the critical paragraph, where Kripke's reading is assessed.

#### Plain-language visual — the pupil who has done nothing wrong by his own lights

```text
   THE ORDER:  '+2'   -   write the series
   ==========================================================================
   THE PUPIL WRITES   2  4  6  8 ... 996  998  1000  1004  1008  1012
                                                 ^
                                                 |
                              here we say: 'No! Look what you've done!'
   ==========================================================================
   THE PUPIL'S REPLY (185, in substance):
     'But I went on in the same way. Isn't this what you wanted? I did
      exactly what you showed me - I just did it for these numbers.'
   ==========================================================================
   CAN WE REFUTE HIM BY POINTING TO THE RULE?
     'Add 2'          ->  he says he added 2 - in HIS reading of 'add 2'
     The examples      ->  finite; none of them covered 1000+
     'Add 2 EVERY time' ->  'every' also needs a reading
     'Add 2, and by "add 2" I mean...'  ->  the explanation needs a reading
   ==========================================================================
   THE MECHANISM: REGRESS OF INTERPRETATIONS
     rule -> needs an interpretation to be applied
          -> the interpretation is another formulation
          -> which needs an interpretation to be applied
          -> ... and nothing ever gets applied
   ==========================================================================
   WITTGENSTEIN'S MOVE (201): the regress shows that the FIRST STEP was
   wrong. Grasping a rule is not attaching an interpretation. There is a way
   of grasping a rule EXHIBITED IN OBEYING IT AND GOING AGAINST IT.
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** the point is not that we cannot tell the pupil is wrong — of course we can, and we do. The point is that our certainty does not come from a further sign, formula or mental item that pins the rule down. It comes from having been trained into a practice in which 1002 is what you write and 1004 is a mistake. The philosopher's mistake was to look for a *thing* — a formula, a picture, an act of meaning — that does the work that training does.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The problem is **not** about doubt | nobody is unsure what "+2" requires; the problem is what makes it required |
| Interpretation means **substituting one expression for another** | §201's own gloss; that is why it cannot terminate |
| The resolution is **not** "there are more rules" | more rules would need more interpretations |
| Bedrock is **action**, not agreement | §217: "This is simply what I do" |
| Practice ≠ mere regularity | otherwise "correct" would collapse into "usual" |

> **One-line memory:** a rule does not contain its own applications; a trained practice contains them, which is why explanations end in doing rather than in saying.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the paradox, the two misreadings, and the resolution

```text
   STEP 1  THE TEMPTING PICTURE
     To follow a rule is to have the rule 'in mind' and to derive each
     application from it. Meaning something determines in advance every
     future step - 'the steps are really already taken' (188, in substance).
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   STEP 2  THE PRESSURE  (138-155, 185-190)
     * The formula does not apply itself.  * The examples are finite.
     * The mental picture is another sign, and needs applying too (139-141).
     * The 'aha!' of sudden understanding can occur and be followed by
       failure (151-155) - so it is not what understanding CONSISTS in.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   STEP 3  THE PARADOX  (201, first half)
     'THIS WAS OUR PARADOX: no course of action could be determined by a
      rule, because EVERY course of action can be made out to accord with
      the rule.'
     'If everything can be made out to accord with the rule, then it can
      also be made out to CONFLICT with it. And so there would be neither
      accord nor conflict here.'
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   STEP 4  THE DIAGNOSIS  (201, second half)
     'It can be seen that there is a MISUNDERSTANDING here from the mere
      fact that in the course of our argument we give one interpretation
      after another; as if each one contented us at least for a moment,
      until we thought of yet another standing behind it.'
     -> INTERPRETATION = 'the substitution of one expression of the rule
        for another'. That is why it can never be the terminus.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   STEP 5  THE RESOLUTION  (201, third part; 202; 217)
     'There is a way of grasping a rule which is NOT an interpretation, but
      which is exhibited in what we call "obeying the rule" and "going
      against it" in actual cases.'
     202: 'to obey a rule is a PRACTICE. And to THINK one is obeying a rule
      is not to obey a rule.'
     217: justifications exhausted -> 'This is simply what I do.'
   ==========================================================================
   THE TWO MISREADINGS - REJECT BOTH EXPLICITLY IN AN ANSWER
   MISREADING A  'So nothing determines meaning; the sceptic wins.'
     -> 201 calls the result a MISUNDERSTANDING, not a discovery. The
        paradox is a REDUCTIO of the interpretation-picture.
   MISREADING B  'So the community's actual behaviour is the standard.'
     -> Then 'correct' would mean 'what most people do'. But majorities can
        be wrong, and the whole community could go astray in a case without
        thereby changing the rule. 202's contrast between OBEYING and
        THINKING one obeys is a distinction, not a head-count.
   ==========================================================================
   KRIPKE'S READING - REPORT IT, THEN RULE ON IT                        1982
     Kripke, Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language: reads 201 as a
     SCEPTICAL PARADOX (no fact about me constitutes my meaning addition
     rather than 'quaddition') answered by a SCEPTICAL SOLUTION (assertion-
     conditions plus communal agreement).
     ESSENTIAL CAUTION: Kripke presents it as 'Wittgenstein's argument as it
     struck Kripke' - the standard shorthand is 'Kripkenstein'.
     THE OPPOSING READING: Baker and Hacker, McDowell - Wittgenstein
     DISSOLVES the paradox rather than conceding it; a sceptical solution
     would be the sort of constructive theory his method rejects.
     WHAT TO WRITE: name both, take a side, give one reason.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** This session is where the private-language argument gets its premise, and the connection should be made explicit in any answer to the 2019, 2020 or 2024 parts: if grasping a rule requires a practice in which obeying and going against it are distinguishable, then a "rule" for which that distinction cannot arise is not a rule.

#### 1. The setting: understanding and continuing a series ✅

✅ The considerations grow out of §138–155, where Wittgenstein asks how the meaning of a word can be grasped "in a flash" when its use is extended in time. He examines the case of a pupil learning to continue "1, 3, 5, 7 …" and then the order "+2". §151 catalogues what actually happens when understanding "dawns" — the formula occurs to me; I recognise the differences; I simply say "now I can go on" — and §152 concludes that none of these processes *is* the understanding, because each can occur without understanding following.

⚠️ Note the shape of the argument: it is the same shape as §28–31 in Session 3 and §143–155 in Session 2. A candidate item is proposed as what a mental achievement **consists in**; a case is produced in which the item is present and the achievement absent, and another in which the achievement is present and the item absent; the identification therefore fails. Naming this shape once in an answer is worth a mark, because it shows the sessions are one argument rather than four.

#### 2. §185: the pupil who goes on differently ✅

✅ **§185.** The pupil has been taught the series of natural numbers and then the "+2" series up to 1000. We ask him to continue beyond 1000, and he writes 1000, 1004, 1008, 1012. We say: look at what you have done. He does not understand; he says: *"But I went on in the same way."* And, Wittgenstein adds, it would be no use to say "But can't you see …?" and repeat the old examples and explanations — *"such a one as we should perhaps say: it comes natural to this person to understand our order with our explanations as we should understand the order: 'Add 2 up to 1000, 4 up to 2000, 6 up to 3000 and so on.'"*

⚠️ **What the case is designed to show.** Not that the pupil is right — he is not. Not that we cannot correct him — we can. What it shows is that the correction cannot be **derived** from anything already possessed: not from the sign, not from the examples, not from a mental image, and not from any further explanation, since each of those would need the same kind of uptake. ⚠️ Wittgenstein's phrase "*it comes natural to this person*" is worth quoting, because it locates the divergence at the level of **reaction**, which is where the form-of-life background (Session 6) does its work.

#### 3. §201, in its three parts ✅

The remark must be given in three parts, because scripts routinely quote the first and omit the third, which converts a reductio into scepticism:

| Part | Text (in substance) | Function |
|---|---|---|
| **(i) The paradox** | *"No course of action could be determined by a rule, because every course of action can be made out to accord with the rule."* And if everything can be made to accord, everything can be made to conflict, so *"there would be neither accord nor conflict here"* | States the consequence of the interpretation-picture |
| **(ii) The diagnosis** | *"It can be seen that there is a misunderstanding here from the mere fact that in the course of our argument we give one interpretation after another; as if each one contented us at least for a moment, until we thought of yet another standing behind it."* | Identifies the paradox as a **symptom**, not a result |
| **(iii) The resolution** | *"There is a way of grasping a rule which is not an interpretation, but which is exhibited in what we call 'obeying the rule' and 'going against it' in actual cases."* | Supplies the alternative and closes the regress |

⚠️ §201 itself glosses **interpretation** as *"the substitution of one expression of the rule for another"*. That gloss is the key to the whole argument: because an interpretation is another expression, it stands in exactly the same need as the rule it was meant to fix, and no series of such substitutions can ever terminate. The regress is therefore not a contingent inconvenience but a structural feature of the picture, which is why the picture must go.

#### 4. Practice, and why it is not consensus ✅⚠️

✅ **§202:** *"'obeying a rule' is a practice. And to think one is obeying a rule is not to obey a rule. Hence it is not possible to obey a rule 'privately': otherwise thinking one was obeying a rule would be the same thing as obeying it."*

⚠️ This remark does two jobs at once, and both should be stated. It completes the rule-following argument by naming its terminus (**practice**), and it announces the private-language argument forty remarks in advance by drawing the consequence: where seeming and being cannot come apart, there is no rule. §258's diary argument is §202 applied to a sensation word.

⚠️ **The discrimination that carries the most marks here is normativity against regularity.** A practice is not merely a regularity of behaviour:
- A regularity can be described from outside; a practice involves **standards** by reference to which a performance is criticised.
- A regularity has exceptions; a practice has **mistakes** — and a mistake is not a statistical outlier but a failure by a standard the agent himself acknowledges when it is pointed out.
- A regularity can be shared by a crowd; a **standard** can be misapplied by the whole crowd at once, which is why "what we all do" is not the criterion.
❌ Writing "for Wittgenstein, correctness is whatever the community agrees on" is the most damaging single sentence available in this session, because it recreates at group level exactly the collapse of "right" into "seems right" that §258 exploits at individual level.

#### 5. Kripke's reading, and the ruling to give ❓

❓ **Kripke, *Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language* (1982)** reads §201 as presenting a **sceptical paradox**: there is no fact about my past usage, dispositions or mental history that constitutes my having meant *addition* rather than the deviant function usually called *quaddition*, and therefore no fact that makes "125" rather than "5" the correct answer to "68 + 57". Kripke then attributes to Wittgenstein a **sceptical solution**: we abandon truth-conditions for **assertion-conditions**, and what licenses the ascription of meaning is the agreement of a **community**.

⚠️ **Three things must be said about this in an answer, and they can be said in three sentences.**
1. **Provenance.** Kripke explicitly presents the argument as *"Wittgenstein's argument as it struck Kripke"*, not as exegesis, which is why the scholarly shorthand is "**Kripkenstein**"; saying so is the cheapest available demonstration that the candidate knows the difference between a text and a reading.
2. **The opposing reading.** Baker and Hacker, and McDowell from a different direction, deny that Wittgenstein concedes any scepticism: §201 calls the paradox a **misunderstanding** and offers a third part that Kripke's reconstruction under-weights, so the text dissolves the problem rather than answering it.
3. **The ruling.** ⚠️ The most defensible position, and the one this package recommends, is that Kripke's reconstruction is philosophically valuable and exegetically doubtful: it sharpens why *no interpretation and no disposition* can by itself fix normativity, and it then supplies a **constructive theory** — assertion-conditions plus community — of exactly the kind §109 and 133 forbid. Say that, give the reason, and the "critically discuss" requirement is met.

⚠️ ❓ It is also worth marking that the community question is genuinely open in the literature: whether Wittgenstein requires an **actual** community or only standards that are **in principle shareable** is a live dispute, and it is treated in the optional advanced block rather than here, because no routed question requires it.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The rule-following paradox is a reductio of the picture on which grasping a rule consists in attaching an interpretation to it, and its conclusion is not that rules determine nothing but that rule-following is exhibited in trained practice rather than constituted by interpretation.
> **Named evidence.** §185 imagines a pupil taught the "+2" series up to 1000 who continues 1004, 1008, 1012 and protests that he went on in the same way; no repetition of the examples refutes him, because it comes natural to him to take the order as we should take "add 2 up to 1000, 4 up to 2000". §201 draws the consequence — no course of action could be determined by a rule, because every course of action can be made out to accord with it, and hence also to conflict with it — then identifies the misunderstanding in the fact that the argument gives one interpretation after another, each contenting us for a moment until another stands behind it, interpretation being "the substitution of one expression of the rule for another". The remark closes by asserting a way of grasping a rule which is not an interpretation but is exhibited in obeying and going against it in actual cases, and §202 adds that obeying a rule is a practice and that thinking one is obeying a rule is not obeying it.
> **Analysis.** The argument's force lies in the structural character of the regress: because an interpretation is itself another expression, it stands in precisely the need it was introduced to satisfy, so no series of interpretations can terminate. Removing the first step rather than extending the series therefore dissolves the paradox, and it locates normativity in a trained practice in which correction, mistake and the distinction between obeying and merely seeming to obey have application. That result is immediately load-bearing for the third printed limb, since §202's denial that a rule can be obeyed privately is §258's conclusion stated in advance.
> **Qualification.** Two readings must be resisted. Treating the paradox as a sceptical discovery ignores §201's own word "misunderstanding" and its third part; and treating community behaviour as the criterion of correctness reinstates at group level the collapse of "right" into "seems right", since a practice involves standards by which even a general performance can be criticised, whereas a regularity involves only frequencies. Kripke's 1982 reconstruction, which he himself offers as the argument as it struck him rather than as exegesis, is valuable for showing that neither interpretation nor disposition fixes normativity, but its sceptical solution supplies the constructive theory Wittgenstein's method disavows.

#### Exam link

- **2024 Q1(d)** (10 marks) and **2020 Q4(c)** (15 marks): §202 is the premise — no rule can be obeyed privately, because seeming and being would coincide. Cite it before the diary.
- **2019 Q3(b)** (15 marks): the solipsist's vocabulary must be rule-governed; §201 is what shows that a private determination of the rule is unavailable.
- **2023 Q4(c)**, **2025 Q3(a)**: the rule-following considerations are the deepest reason the *Tractatus*'s determinate logical form could not be retained — the determination it required is exactly what no formulation can supply by itself.
- **2018 Q1(c)**: family-resemblance concepts are extended by trained judgment, which is rule-following without a formula.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "§201 proves that meaning is indeterminate" | §201 calls the paradox a **misunderstanding** and supplies a third part |
| "The community's agreement is the standard of correctness" | That collapses "correct" into "usual"; §202 contrasts obeying with **thinking** one obeys |
| "Kripke's sceptical solution is Wittgenstein's view" | Kripke offers it as the argument **as it struck him**; "Kripkenstein" marks the difference |
| "The answer is that we follow further rules" | Further rules need further interpretations; the regress is structural |
| "Rule-following is just behavioural regularity" | A practice has **mistakes**; a regularity has only exceptions |
| Quoting §217 as if it meant "whatever I do is right" | It marks where **justification** ends, not where correctness is decided |

#### Revision notes

- **§138–155** understanding "in a flash": every candidate inner item can occur without understanding, and understanding without it.
- **§185** the pupil at 1000 writes 1004, 1008, 1012 — *"But I went on in the same way"*; the divergence is at the level of **reaction**.
- **§201 (i)** paradox: every course of action can be made out to accord — and so to conflict — with the rule.
- **§201 (ii)** diagnosis: the giving of one interpretation after another; **interpretation** = substituting one expression of the rule for another.
- **§201 (iii)** resolution: a way of grasping a rule **which is not an interpretation**, exhibited in obeying and going against it.
- **§202** obeying a rule is a **practice**; thinking one obeys is not obeying; hence a rule cannot be obeyed **privately**.
- **§217** bedrock: *"This is simply what I do."*
- **Normativity ≠ regularity:** practices have mistakes and standards; regularities have exceptions and frequencies.
- **Kripke 1982:** sceptical paradox + sceptical solution; "Wittgenstein's argument as it struck Kripke"; opposed by Baker and Hacker and by McDowell.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — RULE-FOLLOWING

```text
PICTURE: grasping a rule = having it in mind and DERIVING each application
   |
   +--> 138-155  no inner item IS the understanding (flash without success;
   |               success without flash)
   +--> 185  the pupil at 1000: 1004, 1008, 1012 - 'I went on in the same way'
   |
   v
201 (i) PARADOX: every course of action can be made to ACCORD with the rule
        -> and therefore also to CONFLICT -> neither accord nor conflict
   |
   v
```
