```text
   illness.'
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   'What is your aim   309   THE GOVERNING IMAGE. Der Fliege den Ausweg aus
   in philosophy? - To       dem Fliegenglas zeigen. The fly is not held by
   show the fly the way      anything; it is repeating a move that sustains
   out of the fly-bottle.'   the trap. What is needed is not force but a
                             SHOWING.
   ==========================================================================
   THE POSITIVE METHOD - NOT 'DO NOTHING'
     122  PERSPICUOUS REPRESENTATION (uebersichtliche Darstellung): an
          arrangement that produces 'just that understanding which consists
          in SEEING CONNECTIONS'. Wittgenstein calls it 'of fundamental
          significance for us' and says it earmarks the form of account we
          give.
     127  'The work of the philosopher consists in ASSEMBLING REMINDERS for
          a particular purpose.'
     128  'If one tried to advance THESES in philosophy, it would never be
          possible to debate them, because everyone would agree to them.'
     129  'The aspects of things that are most important for us are hidden
          because of their SIMPLICITY AND FAMILIARITY.'
     133  'There is not A philosophical method, though there are indeed
          METHODS, LIKE DIFFERENT THERAPIES.'
   ==========================================================================
   OBJECTION 1  QUIETISM / CONSERVATISM
     '"Philosophy leaves everything as it is" (124) removes philosophy's
      critical function, including social critique.'
     REPLY  124 is about GRAMMAR: philosophy 'may in no way interfere with
     the actual use of language; it can in the end only describe it'. The
     claim concerns conceptual description, not social practice; therapy
     removes confusion and does not forbid reform.
     RESIDUE  the range of what philosophy MAY do is deliberately and
     severely narrowed. The charge is not fully answered, and saying so is
     better than pretending it is.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   OBJECTION 2  SELF-APPLICATION
     'The therapeutic method is itself a philosophical thesis about
      philosophy, so it refutes itself.'
     REPLY  Wittgenstein accepts a version: his remarks are ladder-like,
     and 133 speaks of METHODS in the plural, so there is no single
     doctrine to be self-refuting.
     RESIDUE  structurally the same problem as the Tractatus ladder (6.54).
     THE CONTINUITY BETWEEN EARLY AND LATE ON THIS EXACT POINT IS A
     FIRST-CLASS EXAM OBSERVATION.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   OBJECTION 3  SOME PROBLEMS ARE REAL
     'Personal identity, free will, the status of mathematics survive any
      amount of grammatical clarification.'
     REPLY  Wittgenstein would treat persistence as evidence that the
     picture holding us captive has not yet been fully exhibited.
     RESIDUE  UNFALSIFIABLE AS STATED. This is the most serious
     methodological complaint, and an honest answer says so.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** No routed question asks about therapy on its own, and that is exactly why this session matters: it supplies the **verdict paragraph** for the 2023 and 2025 transition parts and the **methodological frame** for every private-language answer, since the private-language argument is itself a piece of grammatical therapy.

#### 1. Language idling: the diagnosis ✅

✅ **§38** occurs while Wittgenstein is discussing the word "this" and the temptation to treat it as the only genuine name. He observes: *"For philosophical problems arise when language goes on holiday."* ⚠️ The German is *"wenn die Sprache feiert"*. *Feiern* is used of a machine or a workman that is **idle** — not working — and the image is of an engine running while disconnected from its load. Anscombe's "holiday" is idiomatic English but suggests recreation, which is the wrong picture. ⚠️ Giving the German with a one-clause gloss is the single most economical way to show mastery of the provenance discipline in this clause.

⚠️ **What idling means concretely.** A word's surface grammar survives its removal from a language-game; its **use** does not. "It is 5 o'clock on the sun" (§350–351) is the standard demonstration: nothing is grammatically wrong, and nothing has been said, because our practice of telling the time is tied to the sun's position relative to a place on the earth. The philosophical question "but what *is* time?" is diagnosed as the same phenomenon at a deeper level — and §89's citation of Augustine ("If no one asks me, I know; if I wish to explain it to one who asks, I know not") is the classic case.

#### 2. The picture that holds us captive ✅

✅ **§115:** *"A picture held us captive. And we could not get outside it, for it lay in our language and language seemed to repeat it to us inexorably."*

⚠️ **Three features of this remark are examinable.**
1. The captor is a **picture (*Bild*)** — not a false proposition. One can refute a proposition; a picture has to be **exhibited**, and the exhibiting is the therapy.
2. The captivity is **linguistic**, not psychological: it lies *in our language*, which is why resolving to think differently does not help and why the treatment must be grammatical.
3. The picture is usually **natural and useful** somewhere. The object-and-designation model is right for "table"; the trouble begins when it is carried where it does not belong. ⚠️ That is why the method is comparison rather than refutation: the aim is to show the picture's proper home, not to abolish it.

#### 3. Perspicuous representation, and what the method positively does ✅

✅ **§122:** *"A main source of our failure to understand is that we do not command a clear view of the use of our words. — Our grammar is lacking in this sort of perspicuity. A perspicuous representation produces just that understanding which consists in 'seeing connections'. Hence the importance of finding and inventing intermediate cases."* And: *"The concept of a perspicuous representation is of fundamental significance for us. It earmarks the form of account we give, the way we look at things."*

✅ **§127:** *"The work of the philosopher consists in assembling reminders for a particular purpose."* ✅ **§129:** what is most important is hidden by its **simplicity and familiarity**. ✅ **§128:** if one advanced theses in philosophy, they could never be debated, because everyone would agree with them.

⚠️ **The method is therefore not passivity.** It has identifiable operations: *find intermediate cases*; *invent language-games for comparison*; *ask how the word was taught*; *ask what would count as a mistake*; *place the puzzling use beside an unpuzzling one*. ⚠️ A script that says "Wittgenstein thinks philosophy should just describe" has stated a slogan; a script that lists two of these operations has described a method.

#### 4. "Leaves everything as it is", correctly bounded ✅⚠️

✅ **§124:** *"Philosophy may in no way interfere with the actual use of language; it can in the end only describe it. For it cannot give it any foundation either. It leaves everything as it is."*

⚠️ **The scope of the remark is fixed by its first clause**, and quoting it without that clause is what generates the quietism charge. What is left as it is, is the **use of language**: philosophy is not entitled to legislate that people are misusing "know" or "time", nor to supply grammar with a foundation. It does not follow, and Wittgenstein does not say, that social arrangements should be left as they are, that conceptual criticism is illegitimate, or that reform is forbidden.

⚠️ **The concession that must accompany the reply.** The range of what philosophy may legitimately *do* is deliberately and severely narrowed. Wittgenstein forbids explanatory theory, foundational justification and revisionary legislation over grammar. A critic who says that this leaves philosophy with very little is not misreading him; the honest position is to state the reply and then concede that the reply narrows the discipline drastically, which is a cost Wittgenstein accepts rather than an objection he answers.

#### 5. Continuity with the *Tractatus*, and the self-application problem ⚠️❓

⚠️ The self-application objection is the strongest available, and it is the point at which the two Wittgensteins meet. The *Tractatus* ends by declaring its own propositions nonsensical, to be used as a ladder and thrown away (6.54). The *Investigations* denies that it advances theses (§128) and speaks of **methods**, in the plural, *"like different therapies"* (§133), while its own preface calls the book an album of sketches.

❓ Whether this is a coherent position or a self-undermining one is genuinely disputed, and both readings should be named:
- **The resolute reading:** the plurality is the point — there is no doctrine to be self-refuting, only a family of clarificatory practices whose success is measured case by case.
- **The critical reading:** any claim of the form "philosophical problems are grammatical confusions" is a general thesis about philosophy, and the method cannot exempt itself from its own strictures.
⚠️ **The recommendation:** report both, then rule that the self-application charge has real force but is mitigated by §133's plural, and note that the *structural* similarity to the ladder shows this to be a problem Wittgenstein inherited rather than acquired — an observation that reads as scholarship rather than as evasion.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The therapeutic conception reclassifies philosophical difficulty as disorientation rather than ignorance, and it accordingly replaces explanation with a grammatical description whose test of success is the removal of a compulsion rather than the establishment of a further truth.
> **Named evidence.** §38 locates the origin — philosophical problems arise when language goes on holiday, *wenn die Sprache feiert*, an image of machinery idling rather than of recreation — and §350–351 exhibit the phenomenon in "It is 5 o'clock on the sun", a sentence with an impeccable surface and no specified game. §115 names the mechanism: a picture held us captive, and we could not get outside it, for it lay in our language and language seemed to repeat it to us inexorably. §123 reclassifies the difficulty as "I don't know my way about"; §122 supplies the positive method of a perspicuous representation producing the understanding that consists in seeing connections, with intermediate cases found and invented for the purpose; §127 describes the philosopher's work as assembling reminders; and §255 and §309 state the analogy and its governing image, the treatment of an illness and the showing of the fly the way out of the fly-bottle.
> **Analysis.** The reclassification explains why further information does not help: someone lost in a familiar town needs an arrangement of what he already knows, not additional facts, which is why §129 says that the aspects most important for us are hidden by their simplicity and familiarity. It also explains the method's characteristic operations — inventing comparison cases, asking how a word was taught, asking what would count as a mistake — and why §128 holds that a philosophical thesis could never be debated, since anything genuinely established by such description would command immediate assent.
> **Qualification.** The quietism charge rests on §124, and the remark's own first clause bounds it: what philosophy may not interfere with is the actual use of language, not social arrangements, so therapy removes confusion without forbidding reform — although it must be conceded that the range of what philosophy may legitimately do is thereby narrowed very severely. The self-application charge is stronger: the claim that philosophical problems are grammatical confusions is itself general, and although §133's talk of methods in the plural, like different therapies, mitigates it, the structural parallel with the *Tractatus* ladder shows this to be a difficulty Wittgenstein inherited rather than solved. The most serious methodological complaint remains that persistent problems can always be redescribed as pictures not yet fully exhibited, which makes the method unfalsifiable as stated.

#### Exam link

- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks): the therapeutic conception is the **destination** of the shift, and naming it converts a list of reasons into a direction of travel.
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks): the verdict paragraph — the transition is from a **theoretical** to a **therapeutic** conception of philosophy, with quietism as the cost.
- **2020 Q4(c)**, **2024 Q1(d)**: the private-language argument is itself grammatical therapy; §350–351's model of "not false, no game specified" is the verdict form used on "S".
- **2022 Q1(c)** (10 marks): §124 explains why describing forms of life is not endorsing them.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "'Language goes on holiday' means language is being used casually" | *feiert* = **idles**; an engine running without load |
| "'Leaves everything as it is' means political conservatism" | §124's first clause limits it to the **use of language** |
| "Wittgenstein has a method" | §133: **methods**, plural, "like different therapies" |
| "Therapy means philosophy should stop" | §122, 127: perspicuous representation, assembled reminders, invented intermediate cases |
| "The fly-bottle shows philosophy is trivial" | The image is about a **self-sustaining move**, not about triviality |
| Ignoring the self-application objection | It is the strongest objection and links early to late — raise it yourself |

#### Revision notes

- **§38** *"philosophical problems arise when language goes on holiday"* — *wenn die Sprache feiert*, i.e. **idles**.
- **§109** philosophy is *"a battle against the bewitchment of our intelligence by means of language"*; description replaces explanation.
- **§115** *"a picture held us captive"* — the captor is a **picture**, and it lies **in our language**.
- **§122** **perspicuous representation** → *"seeing connections"*; find and invent **intermediate cases**.
- **§123** *"I don't know my way about"* — **disorientation**, not ignorance.
- **§124** *"philosophy… may in no way interfere with the actual use of language… It leaves everything as it is."*
- **§127** assembling **reminders**; **§128** theses would be undebatable; **§129** hidden by **simplicity and familiarity**.
- **§133** *"not a philosophical method, though there are indeed methods, like different therapies"*; the real discovery makes it possible to **stop**.
- **§255** treatment of an illness; **§309** show the fly the way out of the **fly-bottle**.
- Three objections: **quietism** (bounded, but the discipline is narrowed), **self-application** (real force, mitigated by §133's plural, inherited from 6.54), **persistent problems** (the reply is **unfalsifiable as stated**).

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — PHILOSOPHY AS THERAPY

```text
WORD AT WORK IN A GAME
   |
   +--> TAKEN OUT OF THE GAME (38): surface grammar survives, USE does not
   |      'wenn die Sprache feiert' = language IDLES, engine without load
   |      demonstration: 'It is 5 o'clock on the sun' (350-351)
   |
   +--> A PICTURE FILLS THE GAP (115): held CAPTIVE; it lies IN OUR LANGUAGE
   |
   +--> THE DIFFICULTY IS DISORIENTATION (123): 'I don't know my way about'
   |
   v
TREATMENT (122, 127, 128, 129, 133, 255, 309)
   assemble REMINDERS · find and invent INTERMEDIATE CASES · produce a
   PERSPICUOUS REPRESENTATION -> 'SEEING CONNECTIONS' · no THESES ·
   the important is hidden by SIMPLICITY AND FAMILIARITY · METHODS, LIKE
   DIFFERENT THERAPIES · treatment of an ILLNESS · show the fly the way out
   of the FLY-BOTTLE
   |
   v
TEST OF SUCCESS (133): being able to STOP doing philosophy when one wants
   |
   v
OBJECTIONS
   QUIETISM (124)      -> bounded by its own first clause: the USE OF
                          LANGUAGE. RESIDUE: the discipline is narrowed hard
   SELF-APPLICATION    -> mitigated by 133's PLURAL; structurally the same
                          as the Tractatus ladder (6.54) - EARLY/LATE LINK
   PERSISTENT PROBLEMS -> reply available but UNFALSIFIABLE AS STATED
   |
   v
ANSWER LINE: the philosopher is not ignorant but lost, and what he needs is
   a map of a town he already knows
```

### SESSION 12 — BOUNDED ENRICHMENT: ASPECT-SEEING, THE DUCK-RABBIT, AND HINGE CERTAINTY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Two topics sit just outside the printed clause and are constantly imported into answers by candidates who do not know they are outside it. The first is *seeing-as*: the duck-rabbit figure that changes without changing. The second is the certainty of *On Certainty*: the propositions we never check because checking is done by means of them. Both illuminate the owned material. Neither is a limb of the syllabus, and both must therefore be used in the exam as **one or two sentences**, never as a paragraph.

**Technical definition:** **Aspect-seeing** — the **dawning of an aspect (*das Aufleuchten eines Aspekts*)** — is the phenomenon in which a figure is seen *as* one thing and then *as* another while nothing in the object or the retinal image changes; Wittgenstein's formula is *"I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently."* It is treated in the material formerly printed as *Investigations* Part II §xi and now, in the fourth edition of 2009 revised by Hacker and Schulte, as ***Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment* (PPF)**, where the sections are renumbered. **Hinge certainty** is the doctrine of ***On Certainty*** (notes written 1949–51, published 1969) that some propositions — *"I have two hands"*, *"the earth existed long before my birth"* — function not as things we know on evidence but as the **hinges** on which enquiry turns.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Seeing-as is the clearest evidence that concepts are not applied *after* perception but are already present in what we see, and it is therefore the best single illustration of the later Wittgenstein's method — provided it is used as an illustration, since the printed clause does not own it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **the dawning of an aspect (*das Aufleuchten eines Aspekts*)** — the technical term
- **"I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently"** — the formula, quoted
- **aspect-blindness** — the imagined limiting case, which carries the argument
- **Jastrow, 1899** — the figure's actual author
- **PPF / Part II §xi** — the citation form, with the renumbering caution
- **hinge propositions (*On Certainty*, 1969)** — named, dated, and marked as enrichment

**How to use them:** signpost the boundary explicitly — *"strictly, this belongs to the material printed as Part II / PPF rather than to the three printed limbs, but it illuminates the account of meaning as follows"* — because that sentence converts what looks like drift into demonstrated scope-awareness; and always attribute the **figure** to **Jastrow**.

#### Plain-language visual — the change that is neither in the object nor in a judgement

```text
   THE DUCK-RABBIT   (figure: JASTROW, 1899; reproduced by Wittgenstein)
   ==========================================================================
   BEFORE                                  AFTER
   I see a duck                            I see a rabbit
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   WHAT CHANGED IN THE DRAWING?            NOTHING. Same ink, same lines.
   WHAT CHANGED ON MY RETINA?              NOTHING measurable.
   DID I INFER the rabbit?                 NO. The change is immediate and
                                           visual, not a conclusion.
   DID I MERELY REINTERPRET?               NO. 'I see it differently' is
                                           reported as a VISUAL fact.
   ==========================================================================
   SO WHAT KIND OF THING IS IT?
     Wittgenstein: 'the dawning of an aspect' (das Aufleuchten eines
     Aspekts). Neither pure perception nor pure judgement - the seeing is
     'half visual experience, half thought'.
   ==========================================================================
   THE CONDITION THAT MAKES THE ARGUMENT WORK
     To see the RABBIT-aspect one must be FAMILIAR WITH THE SHAPE OF A
     RABBIT. Aspect-perception PRESUPPOSES MASTERY OF A TECHNIQUE.
     -> Concepts are not applied AFTER perception. They are already at
        work in what is seen.
   ==========================================================================
   THE LIMITING CASE: ASPECT-BLINDNESS
     Imagine someone with full visual acuity who can identify both figures
     on request but is INCAPABLE OF THE DAWNING. He is not blind, and not
     stupid. What he lacks is the EXPERIENCE OF MEANING - hearing a word
     'in a particular sense', feeling that a word 'fits'.
     -> This is why aspect-seeing illuminates MEANING and not merely
        perception.
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** you can hear the word "bank" and mean the river; a moment later hear the same sound and mean the building. Nothing acoustic changed. The duck-rabbit is that experience made visible, which is why Wittgenstein uses it to talk about meaning rather than about optics.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The figure is **Jastrow's** (1899) | Wittgenstein reproduces it; never attribute the drawing to him |
| Citation is **Part II §xi / PPF** | the 2009 edition renamed and **renumbered** Part II |
| It is **not** a printed limb | no routed PYQ owns it; budget one or two sentences |
| The key is **aspect-blindness** | the imagined deficit shows the tie to the experience of meaning |
| *On Certainty* is **1949–51 / 1969** | unrevised notes; the last entries days before his death |

> **One-line memory:** the drawing does not change and the seeing does — which is only possible if the concept was in the seeing all along.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the numbered argument, what it damages, and the fence around it

```text
   THE ARGUMENT, NUMBERED
   1  Nothing in the drawing alters - lines, ink and retinal image are
      identical before and after.
   2  Yet something genuinely changes: I now see a RABBIT where I saw a
      DUCK. Wittgenstein calls this the DAWNING OF AN ASPECT.
   3  So the change is neither in the OBJECT (nothing physical altered) nor
      merely in my INTERPRETATION (I do not INFER the rabbit; the change is
      immediate and visual).
   4  Therefore 'seeing-as' is a THIRD category, neither pure perception
      nor pure judgement: 'half visual experience, half thought'.
   5  Therefore concepts are not applied AFTER perception: the ability to
      see the aspect PRESUPPOSES MASTERY OF A TECHNIQUE - one must know the
      shape of a rabbit to see the rabbit-aspect at all.
   6  ASPECT-BLINDNESS: we can conceive someone with full acuity who
      identifies both figures on request yet never experiences the dawning.
      His relation to MEANING would be impoverished in the same way -
      which is why aspect-seeing illuminates the EXPERIENCE OF MEANING.
   ==========================================================================
   WHAT IT PRESSURES - AND HOW HARD
   TARGET                          DAMAGE DONE - STATED AT ITS TRUE STRENGTH
   Sense-data / the GIVEN          PRESSURES the claim that all seeing is
                                   conceptually innocent. It does NOT by
                                   itself refute every acquaintance theory.
   The PICTURE THEORY              The role of a picture depends on
                                   practices of USE, which presses the early
                                   claim that picturing structure alone
                                   explains representation.
   MEANING AS AN INNER PROCESS     The dawning FEELS like an inner event,
                                   yet its criteria are what one does and
                                   says next. 580 applies here as elsewhere.
   PHILOSOPHY'S OWN METHOD         A therapeutic comparison can make a
                                   familiar use appear under a NEW ASPECT
                                   without adding any empirical discovery.
                                   An illuminating ANALOGY - not a stated
                                   identity of doctrines.
   ==========================================================================
   ON CERTAINTY - THE SECOND ENRICHMENT, FENCED THE SAME WAY
     TEXT     notes written 1949-51; published 1969; UNREVISED; the last
              entries date from days before Wittgenstein's death
     TARGET   G. E. Moore's 'Here is one hand' and 'A Defence of Common
              Sense'                                            [TOPIC 06]
     DOCTRINE Some propositions function as HINGES: 'I have two hands',
              'the earth existed long before my birth'. They are not known
              on evidence; they stand fast and make doubting and checking
              possible. 'The questions that we raise and our doubts depend
              on the fact that some propositions are exempt from doubt, are
              as it were like hinges on which those turn.'
     RELATION Continuous with FORMS OF LIFE: what stands fast belongs to
              the inherited background against which we distinguish true
              from false. But it is a DIFFERENT TEXT with a DIFFERENT
              TARGET, and no routed 2018-2025 part in this clause requires
              it.
     USE      one comparative sentence, at most, and only where certainty,
              bedrock or the limits of justification are already in play
   ==========================================================================
   THE FENCE - WRITE IT, DO NOT JUST OBSERVE IT
     'Aspect-seeing and hinge certainty are not printed limbs of this
      clause; they are used here only to illuminate the account of meaning
      and the limits of justification.'
     One sentence of this kind protects an answer that would otherwise look
     as though it had drifted.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** This session exists to make two things possible at once: to have the enrichment available for a twenty-mark answer, and to have the boundary statement ready so that using it costs nothing.

#### 1. Aspect-seeing: the phenomenon and its correct citation ✅

✅ The discussion belongs to the material formerly printed as *Investigations* **Part II §xi**, renamed in the fourth edition (2009, revised by **Hacker and Schulte**) as ***Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment*** and **renumbered**. ⚠️ Cite it as *"Part II §xi / PPF"* rather than by a bare section number, since a number that is correct in one edition is wrong in another. ⚠️ The **duck-rabbit figure is Jastrow's**, published in 1899; Wittgenstein reproduces it, and attributing the drawing to him is a factual error an examiner can check.

✅ The governing formula is: *"I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently."* Wittgenstein distinguishes **continuous seeing** of an aspect from the **dawning** of an aspect, and observes that the dawning is reported in the same breath as a perception and as a thought — the expression of a perception, yet also *"half visual experience, half thought"*.

#### 2. Why the phenomenon matters for meaning ⚠️

⚠️ Step 5 of the numbered argument is where the philosophical work is done: one must already be **familiar with the shape of a rabbit** to see the rabbit-aspect. Aspect-perception therefore presupposes **mastery of a technique** — exactly the notion Session 2 used to redescribe understanding. The consequence is that concepts are not a coat of paint applied to a neutral perceptual given; they are already operative in what is seen.

⚠️ **Aspect-blindness** is what turns this from an observation into an argument. Imagine a person of normal acuity who can, on request, identify the duck and the rabbit but never experiences the dawning. He is not visually defective and not conceptually incapable; what he lacks is something Wittgenstein connects to the **experience of meaning** — hearing a word "in a particular sense", feeling that a word "fits", experiencing the meaning of a word as one says it. ⚠️ That connection is the reason aspect-seeing belongs in a *philosophy of language* discussion at all, and it is the sentence to write if the topic is used.

#### 3. The four things it pressures, at their true strength ⚠️

The table in the diagram states them. Two require emphasis:

⚠️ **Against the Given.** Aspect-seeing pressures the empiricist claim that perception delivers a conceptually innocent given upon which judgement then operates. ❌ It does **not** by itself refute every acquaintance theory: a defender may say the aspect-change is a change in the *organisation* of a given that remains innocent. State the pressure, not a knock-down.

⚠️ **Against meaning as an inner process.** The dawning *feels* like a pure inner event — which is precisely why it is a good test case. Wittgenstein's treatment applies §580: what shows that an aspect has dawned is what the person **does and says next** — the exclamation, the redescription, the new way of tracing the figure. The inner feel is real and is not the criterion.

#### 4. *On Certainty*: what it is, and why it stays outside ✅⚠️

✅ ***On Certainty*** consists of notes written between **1949 and 1951** and published in **1969**. ⚠️ They are **unrevised**, they were not prepared for publication, and the final entries date from days before Wittgenstein's death — so they should be cited as notes, and no settled doctrine should be attributed to them without that qualification.

✅ The occasion is **G. E. Moore's** "Here is one hand" and *A Defence of Common Sense* — material owned by **Topic 06**. Wittgenstein's response is that Moore's propositions are neither known in the ordinary way nor doubtable in the ordinary way: they function as **hinges**, propositions that stand fast and thereby make doubting, testing and knowing possible. *"The questions that we raise and our doubts depend on the fact that some propositions are exempt from doubt, are as it were like hinges on which those turn."*

⚠️ **Why it stays outside the fence.** Three reasons, and giving them is itself a boundary mark: the text is a **different work** with a **different target** (Moore, not the referential model of meaning); **no routed 2018–2025 part** in this clause requires it; and its natural exam home is either Topic 06's Moore parts or, in Paper II, discussions of certainty and belief. ⚠️ Its legitimate use here is a **single comparative sentence** connecting hinge certainty to the form-of-life background of §241–242 and the bedrock of §217 — a connection that is genuine, since both mark where justification stops, but which must be presented as a comparison rather than as a doctrine of this clause.

#### 5. How to use enrichment without losing marks ⚠️

| Situation | Correct budget | The sentence to write |
|---|---|---|
| **10-mark** part (2018, 2022, 2024) | **Zero**. Do not use it | — |
| **15-mark** part (2019, 2020, 2023) | One sentence, only if it earns its place | *"The dependence of what we see on techniques we have mastered — the duck-rabbit's dawning aspect in the material printed as Part II / PPF — shows how far the later account displaces meaning from inner objects to trained practice."* |
| **20-mark** part (2025) | Two sentences maximum, in the assessment | *"Beyond the printed limbs, the treatment of aspect-seeing and the hinge certainty of the posthumous* On Certainty *(notes of 1949–51, published 1969) show the same movement extended to perception and to the limits of justification, though neither is required for the transition itself."* |
| Any part where **certainty or bedrock** is already being discussed | One comparative clause | *"…a thought later developed in* On Certainty*'s image of hinge propositions."* |

⚠️ ❌ **What loses marks:** a paragraph on the duck-rabbit in a private-language answer; attributing the figure to Wittgenstein; citing "PI Part II §xi" as though numbering were stable; and treating *On Certainty* as though it stated the doctrine of forms of life.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Aspect-seeing illuminates the later account of meaning by showing that concepts are not applied to a perceptual given after the fact but are already operative in what is seen, and it does so without being one of the clause's printed limbs.
> **Named evidence.** In the material formerly printed as *Investigations* Part II §xi, and renamed *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment* with renumbered sections in the fourth edition of 2009 revised by Hacker and Schulte, Wittgenstein reproduces Jastrow's duck-rabbit figure of 1899 and records the formula "I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently", calling the change the dawning of an aspect and describing the experience as half visual experience, half thought. He observes that one must be familiar with the shape of a rabbit in order to see the rabbit-aspect, and he imagines aspect-blindness — a person of normal acuity who identifies both figures on request yet never experiences the dawning — connecting that deficit to an impoverished relation to the experience of meaning.
> **Analysis.** The argument's force lies in the exclusion of both easy answers: the change is not in the object, since nothing physical alters, and it is not merely an interpretation, since it is reported as immediate and visual rather than as a conclusion. What remains is a third category in which mastery of a technique is a condition of the perception itself, which is the same relation between concept and capacity that §199 asserted for understanding a language and §580 for psychological concepts generally.
> **Qualification.** The pressure must be stated at its true strength: aspect-seeing embarrasses the claim that perception delivers a conceptually innocent given, but it does not by itself refute every acquaintance theory, since a defender may treat the change as one of organisation within a given that remains innocent. And the boundary must be stated explicitly, because no routed question in the 2018–2025 corpus owns this material or the hinge certainty of *On Certainty*, whose unrevised notes of 1949–51 were published in 1969 and answer Moore rather than the referential model of meaning; both are legitimate as one or two illustrative sentences and illegitimate as a paragraph.

#### Exam link

- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks): two sentences maximum, in the assessment, showing the movement extended beyond the printed limbs.
- **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks): one sentence, if it earns its place — the displacement of meaning from inner objects to trained practice.
- **2020 Q4(c)** (15 marks): at most one clause connecting the dawning's felt quality to §580, since the temptation here is to over-use it.
- **2018 Q1(c)**, **2022 Q1(c)**, **2024 Q1(d)** (10 marks each): **do not use it**. Ten marks does not have room.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Wittgenstein drew the duck-rabbit" | The figure is **Jastrow's**, 1899; Wittgenstein reproduces it |
| Citing "PI Part II §xi" as a stable reference | The 2009 fourth edition renamed it **PPF** and **renumbered** it; cite both forms |
| Treating aspect-seeing as a syllabus limb | It is **enrichment**; no routed part owns it |
| "Aspect-seeing refutes sense-data" | It **pressures** the innocent given; it is not a refutation |
| Using *On Certainty* as the doctrine of forms of life | Different text, different target (**Moore**), unrevised notes |
| Writing a duck-rabbit paragraph in a 10-mark answer | Budget: **zero** at ten marks |

#### Revision notes

- **Citation:** Part II §xi / **PPF**; 4th edn **2009**, revised by **Hacker and Schulte**, renamed and **renumbered**.
- **Figure:** **Jastrow**, 1899. **Formula:** *"I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently."*
- **Term:** the **dawning of an aspect (*das Aufleuchten eines Aspekts*)**; *"half visual experience, half thought"*.
- **Condition:** seeing the rabbit-aspect presupposes **familiarity with the shape of a rabbit** → mastery of a technique.
- **Aspect-blindness:** full acuity, correct identification, no dawning → an impoverished relation to the **experience of meaning**.
- **Pressures:** the Given (pressure, not refutation) · the picture theory · meaning as inner process (§580) · illuminates therapy by analogy.
- ***On Certainty***: notes **1949–51**, published **1969**, unrevised; answers **Moore**; **hinge** propositions stand fast and make doubt possible; connect to §241–242 and §217 **as a comparison**.
- **Budget:** 10 marks = none; 15 marks = one sentence; 20 marks = two sentences.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — BOUNDED ENRICHMENT

```text
   |
   +--> 'I see that it has not changed; AND YET I SEE IT DIFFERENTLY'
   |
   +--> not in the OBJECT (nothing altered) · not mere INTERPRETATION
   |      (immediate, visual, not inferred)
   |
   v
THIRD CATEGORY: the DAWNING OF AN ASPECT - 'half visual experience, half
   thought'
   |
   +--> CONDITION: must know the shape of a rabbit -> MASTERY OF A TECHNIQUE
   +--> LIMIT CASE: ASPECT-BLINDNESS -> impoverished EXPERIENCE OF MEANING
   |
   v
PRESSURES: the Given (pressure, NOT refutation) · picture theory · meaning
   as inner process (580 applies) · analogy for therapeutic reorientation
   |

   HINGE propositions stand fast and make doubting possible
   -> compare with 241-242 agreement in judgments and 217 bedrock
   -> DIFFERENT TEXT, DIFFERENT TARGET, NO ROUTED PART REQUIRES IT
   |
   v
BUDGET: 10 marks NONE · 15 marks ONE SENTENCE · 20 marks TWO SENTENCES
   and always write the fence sentence
   |

ANSWER LINE: the drawing does not change and the seeing does - which is only
   possible if the concept was in the seeing all along
```

### SESSION 13 — COMPARATIVE SYNTHESIS, THE CRITICISM LEDGER, AND ANSWER CRAFT

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** This session does three jobs at once. It places the later Wittgenstein against the positions he is most often confused with, so that a comparison question can be answered without a scramble. It ranks the objections to him, so that a "critically discuss" instruction has a structure to follow. And it sets out how the seven routed questions are actually built, so that the practice section is entered with a plan rather than with material.

**Technical definition:** A **synthesis session**: it consolidates the doctrine of Sessions 1–12 into (i) an **inter-thinker comparison grid** covering early Wittgenstein, logical positivism, Cartesian and empiricist inner-foundationalism, and the ordinary-language successors; (ii) a **ranked criticism ledger** with each objection's best reply and its residue; (iii) a **directive decoder** matching UPSC command words to structural obligations; (iv) a **graded verdict bank** supplying closing positions at 10, 15 and 20 marks; and (v) the **provenance and translation discipline** that governs every citation in this clause.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The later Wittgenstein is best assessed by what he refuses to do: he will not give a criterion, will not build a theory, and will not legislate over ordinary use — and the honest question is whether that triple refusal is a discipline that dissolves confusion or a restriction that leaves genuine problems standing.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **no criterion, no theory, no legislation** — the triple refusal, in six words
- **grammatical dependence, not equivalence** — the anti-behaviourist formula
- **dissolution, not refutation** — the verdict-word for solipsism and for metaphysics alike
- **normativity without an essence** — what the account claims to deliver
- **provenance discipline** — posthumous texts, renderings, editions
- **quietism / self-application** — the two standing objections, named

**How to use them:** every fifteen- and twenty-mark answer should end with a **defended ruling**, and the graded verdict bank in §4 exists to supply it; do not report the debate and stop, since "critically discuss" and "do you agree" both require a side.

#### Plain-language visual — five positions on one question

```text
   QUESTION: WHAT MAKES A SIGN MEANINGFUL?
   ==========================================================================
   EARLY WITTGENSTEIN      It depicts a possible state of affairs through a
   (Tractatus 1921/22)     shared LOGICAL FORM. One general propositional
                           form. Metaphysics: SHOW what cannot be said,
                           then be silent.               [TOPIC 06]
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   LOGICAL POSITIVISM      It is ANALYTIC or EMPIRICALLY VERIFIABLE.
   (Vienna Circle, 1929-)  ONE CRITERION of cognitive significance.
                           Metaphysics: REJECT as pseudo-statement.
                                                          [TOPIC 07]
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   DESCARTES / CLASSICAL   Meaning traces to IDEAS or IMPRESSIONS, many of
   EMPIRICISM              them PRIVATE and immediately given. The inner is
                           the foundation of the outer.   [TOPICS 02, 03]
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   LATER WITTGENSTEIN      It has a USE in a language-game embedded in a
   (Investigations 1953)   form of life. NO single essence, NO single
                           criterion. Metaphysics: DISSOLVE as language
                           idling.                        [THIS CLAUSE]
   --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   ORDINARY LANGUAGE       Ryle on category-mistakes; Austin on speech acts
   SUCCESSORS              and performatives; Strawson on descriptive
                           metaphysics. Related in spirit, SEPARATE OWNERS.
                                                    [TOPIC 11 and beyond]
   ==========================================================================
   THE THREE CONFUSIONS THIS GRID EXISTS TO PREVENT
   X 'Later Wittgenstein is a positivist'  - he rejects ONE CRITERION as
     firmly as he rejects ONE ESSENCE; the Circle's mistake is the
     Tractatus's mistake in a new vocabulary
   X 'He is a behaviourist'  - criteria are DEFEASIBLE, not definitions
   X 'He is a relativist'  - agreement in JUDGMENTS is what makes
     disagreement possible, not what makes claims true
   ==========================================================================
```

**In simple words:** four of these five positions are looking for the one thing that makes signs work — a form, a criterion, an idea. The later Wittgenstein is the only one who says the search itself was the mistake. That is what makes him hard to place, and it is why he is so often mis-assimilated to whichever of the others a candidate happens to know best.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Positivism is a **rival**, not an ally | one criterion is exactly what he refuses |
| Early Wittgenstein is a **target**, not a stage | the same man, but the doctrine is Topic 06's |
| Ryle, Austin, Strawson are **cousins** | related method, separate ownership |
| The refusal is **triple** | no criterion, no theory, no legislation |
| The claim is **normativity without essence** | which is what every objection tests |

> **One-line memory:** everyone else was hunting the hidden thing; he was explaining why the hunt felt compulsory.

⚠️ **Caption.** Parts B and C are the two things a candidate should be able to reproduce under time pressure. Part B supplies the "critically" content for any question; Part C converts the printed directive into a structure before a single sentence of content is written.

#### 1. The comparison grid, worked ⚠️

⚠️ **Against early Wittgenstein.** The change is set out in Session 1 and should be stated as *the withdrawal of a demand*, not as a change of subject-matter. ⚠️ The single most useful comparative sentence available: *both books hold that philosophy is clarification and that confusion arises from misreading language; they differ over whether clarification requires exhibiting one hidden essence.*

⚠️ **Against logical positivism.** This comparison is tested indirectly (the Circle read the *Tractatus* aloud, and Wittgenstein's 1929–32 conversations with Schlick and Waismann contain verificationist formulations) and it is routinely mishandled. Three discriminations:
1. The positivists impose **one criterion** of cognitive significance. The later Wittgenstein's objection to the *Tractatus* — that one essence was mistaken for many uses — applies to **any** single criterion, including verifiability.
2. The positivists **reject** metaphysical sentences as pseudo-statements; the later Wittgenstein **dissolves** metaphysical puzzles by showing where the words came from. Rejection is a verdict; dissolution is a treatment.
3. The positivists treat **fact-stating** as the paradigm of language; §23 lists describing as one item among many. ⚠️ Naming any one of these three earns the boundary mark in a comparison question.

⚠️ **Against Cartesian and empiricist inner-foundationalism.** The private-language argument is directed at exactly the assumption both share: that the inner is epistemically and semantically prior to the outer. §580 inverts the priority for **concepts** without denying the inner life.

⚠️ **Against ordinary-language successors.** Ryle's category-mistakes, Austin's performatives and speech acts, and Strawson's descriptive metaphysics are methodologically related and separately owned. Name the relation in a clause; do not expound them.

#### 2. The criticism ledger, and how to use it ⚠️

⚠️ The ledger is **ranked**, and the ranking is itself examinable judgement. A candidate who deploys the objections in the order given — self-application, normativity, Kripke, quietism, behaviourism, relativism, Crusoe, circularity — has demonstrated that he can tell a structural objection from a local one.

⚠️ **The rule for a fifteen-mark answer:** one objection, its reply and its residue. **For twenty marks:** two, and preferably one from the top of the list and one specific to the question's subject-matter.

⚠️ **What never earns marks:** listing five objections without replies; or supplying replies without residues, which reads as advocacy rather than assessment.

#### 3. Provenance and translation discipline ⚠️

⚠️ This clause is unusually rich in provenance marks, and each is a cheap sentence:

| Item | Status | Practice |
|---|---|---|
| *Philosophical Investigations* | composed 1936–49; **published posthumously 1953**; edited **Anscombe and Rhees**; German–English facing pages | ❌ Never "Wittgenstein published the *Investigations*". The arrangement of remarks is partly editorial |
| Translations | **Anscombe** 1953; revised **Hacker and Schulte**, 4th edn **2009** | The 4th edition renames Part II as *Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment* and **renumbers** it. Cite "Part II §xi / PPF" |
| Citation by **§** | Part I remarks are **§1–§693**; numbering identical across editions | ✅ Always cite by § rather than by page |
| "Meaning is use" (§43) | hedged: *"for a large class of cases — though not for all"* | ❌ Never quote without the hedge |
| "Language goes on holiday" (§38) | German *wenn die Sprache feiert* — closer to **idles** | Give the § and gloss the image |
| Fly-bottle (§309) | stable across editions | ✅ Safe verbatim |
| Beetle (§293) | stable | ✅ Safe with §293 |
| Diary "S" (§258) | renderings of "whatever is going to seem right to me is right" vary slightly | Give §258; paraphrase if unsure |
| *Blue and Brown Books* | **dictated 1933–35**, published **1958** | Say "in the *Blue Book*", not "Wittgenstein wrote" |
| *On Certainty* | notes **1949–51**, published **1969**, unrevised | Mark as notes; it is his last work |
| Kripke | *Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language*, **1982** | Say "**Kripke's Wittgenstein**" / "Kripkenstein" |
| "Forms of life" | occurs a handful of times (§19, §23, §241, PPF) | Mark large doctrinal constructions as **interpretation** |
| Duck-rabbit | figure is **Jastrow's**, 1899 | Never attribute the drawing to Wittgenstein |
| 2022 printed "motion" | the official English line prints **"motion"** | Retain the printed word; gloss in a clause |
| 2025 unmatched quotation mark | the official line has an unmatched opening quotation before "logical structure" | Reproduce as printed; do not silently correct |

❌ **Never write:** that Wittgenstein "denied the existence of inner mental states" (§304 refuses this explicitly); that the private-language argument is directed at codes or at talking to oneself; or that "meaning is use" is offered as a general definition (§43 says otherwise).

#### 4. Graded verdict bank ⚠️

| Sub-topic | **10-mark verdict** | **15-mark verdict** | **20-mark verdict** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The break from the *Tractatus*** | The later work revises the demand for one general form while retaining philosophy as clarificatory activity. | …Linguistic diversity, the insufficiency of ostension and colour incompatibility together pressure the early system; the *Tractatus* already regarded ordinary propositions as logically in order. | Continuity and discontinuity must both be argued: the early limits of factual saying become later grammatical therapy, and neither book may be reduced to a slogan. The deepest change is that determinacy is relocated from logical form to trained practice. |
| **Meaning and use** | For a large class of cases, meaning is clarified by an expression's role in practice rather than by a hidden referent. | …Use is not frequency, utility or verification; ostension and understanding alike depend on training and grammar, so the account is a redirection rather than a rival theory. | Normativity remains the pressure: practice distinguishes mistake from innovation, but the appeal to use cannot be allowed to become "whatever people do is correct", and Wittgenstein supplies no general criterion for the hard cases. |
| **Family resemblance** | It shows that a general term needs no single essence, which is why "language is a game" is a description rather than an evasion. | …The thread analogy answers the anxiety that a concept without a core must dissolve, and open texture is bounded by training and correction rather than by definition. | The unlimited-resemblance objection is real, and the reply — that practice selects the relevant similarities — is persuasive without being demonstrative; the doctrine is best read as removing a requirement, not as a theory of concepts. |
| **Language-games / forms of life** | Language comprises varied activities without one required essence, resting on shared practices. | …At bedrock trained action replaces further interpretation; §217 belongs to rule-following and should not be made a definition of form of life. | Naturalist and cultural readings differ; shared practice enables disagreement rather than entailing relativism, and the real gap is the absence of a theory of rational adjudication between practices. |
| **Rule-following** | No interpretation fixes its own application; trained practice displays a grasping of a rule that is not another interpretation. | …Practice is normative, not mere regularity or majority agreement, and §202 already forbids private rule-following. | Kripke's sceptical-community reading is influential and textually disputed; the recommendation is that it is philosophically valuable and exegetically doubtful, since a sceptical *solution* is the constructive theory §128 and 133 disavow. |
| **Private language** | Private ostension fails because correctness collapses into present seeming, so no rule and hence no meaning is created. | …The argument denies neither sensations nor solitary speakers; §270's manometer variant shows that whatever work the sign does is done by a public criterion. | It pressures Cartesian and empiricist linguistic foundations decisively while leaving open the memory objection, the status of an actual community, and whether criterial grammar respects inner phenomenology. |
| **Criteria and symptoms** | Criteria are grammatical and defeasible; symptoms are inductive. That single distinction blocks the behaviourist reading. | …It lets Wittgenstein say behaviour is constitutive of the *concept* of pain without being identical to pain, and preserves the first/third-person asymmetry the behaviourist must deny. | The distinction is what makes this a genuine third way — anti-Cartesian without reduction. Its instability is §354's admission that the grammar *fluctuates*, so the firewall is secured case by case; say whether that is candour about concepts or an admission that the key notion is not sharp. |
| **Therapy / fly-bottle** | Philosophy treats conceptual disorientation with reminders and perspicuous comparison rather than with new theory. | …"Leaves everything as it is" concerns grammar, not social conservatism, but the discipline is narrowed very severely. | Self-application is the standing difficulty, structurally inherited from the *Tractatus* ladder; §133's plural mitigates it, and the reply to persistent problems remains unfalsifiable as stated. |
| **Solipsism** | The critique is a dissolution: the thesis is self-undermining, not false. | …The route changes from the early saying/showing limit to the later grammar of criteria, which is what "culminates" asks you to show. | Nothing establishes other minds; §303 concerns where doubt has application. The strength of the result is exactly the strength of the private-language argument on which it depends. |

| Year · Part · Marks | Demand in one line | The spine to write |

| **2018 Q1(c) · 10** | How does family resemblance *help* uphold "Language is a game"? | Challenge at §65 → "Don't think, but look!" → survey → thread → what it denies and permits → games have rules without essence → non-arbitrariness |
| **2019 Q3(b) · 15** | How does the critique of solipsism *culminate* in the critique of private language? | Dissolution not refutation → *Tractatus* 5.6/5.62/5.64 → change of route → the dilemma (§258 / conceded practice) → what is not proved |
| **2020 Q4(c) · 15** | Critically discuss Wittgenstein's answer to §243's own question | Reproduce the printed quotation → §243's narrowing → private ostension → §258 → §265 → §270 → memory and Crusoe objections → verdict |
| **2022 Q1(c) · 10** | Establish the tenability of language as form of life | Note the printed "motion" → §19, §23 → §241/§242 with the measuring test → naturalist/culturalist readings → tenable as background, untenable as a theory of truth |
| **2023 Q4(c) · 15** | Main reasons for the shift from picture-theory to use-theory | Five commitments and five pressures → §43 with its hedge → language-games and forms of life → therapy as destination → continuity of clarification |
| **2024 Q1(d) · 10** | Why can there be no essentially private language? | Target paragraph (four non-targets) → five-step core → beetle in one sentence → solitary-speaker qualification |
| **2025 Q3(a) · 20** | Explain the transition, with reference to the quoted statement | Reproduce the printed wording including the unmatched quotation mark → what the *Tractatus* actually held → the pressures → the later account → therapy → verdict on quietism and normativity |

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The later Wittgenstein's position is best assessed through his triple refusal — no single criterion of meaning, no explanatory theory, and no legislation over ordinary use — since each of the standing objections is an attempt to show that one of these refusals cannot be sustained.
> **Named evidence.** The refusal of a criterion is stated in §43's hedge and enforced by the family-resemblance survey of §66–67; the refusal of theory is stated at §109, where description takes the place of explanation, at §128, where theses would be undebatable, and at §133, where there are methods in the plural, like different therapies; the refusal of legislation is stated at §124, where philosophy may in no way interfere with the actual use of language. Against these, the self-application objection presses that the second refusal is itself a thesis; the normativity objection presses that the first leaves correct and incorrect use undistinguished; Kripke's 1982 reconstruction presses that only communal agreement can supply what interpretation cannot; and the quietism objection presses that the third refusal removes philosophy's critical function.
> **Analysis.** Ranking the objections is itself an act of judgement, and the ranking is defensible on a single principle: an objection is stronger the closer it comes to the method rather than to a doctrine. Self-application is therefore first, because it attacks the possibility of the whole enterprise; the normativity of use is second, because it attacks the positive account's ability to do the one job an account of meaning must do; behaviourism and relativism rank lower because each has a specific and textually grounded reply — defeasible criteria at §304 and 307–308, and the separation of agreement from truth at §241.
> **Qualification.** None of the replies is a closure. §354's admission that the grammar fluctuates between criteria and symptoms means the anti-behaviourist firewall is secured case by case rather than in advance; the absence of a theory of rational adjudication between practices is a genuine gap rather than a misreading; and the reply to persistent philosophical problems — that the captivating picture has not yet been fully exhibited — is unfalsifiable as stated. What can be defended is the narrower claim that the method dissolves a large and identifiable class of confusions, and that its cost is a severe narrowing of what philosophy may attempt.

#### Exam link

- **All seven routed parts**: §5's table gives each spine, and §4's verdict bank gives each closing position at the right mark value.
- **2025 Q3(a)** (20 marks) draws on this session most heavily, since a twenty-mark transition answer needs both a comparison and a ranked assessment.
- **2019 Q3(b)** and **2023 Q4(c)** (15 marks each) each need exactly one objection with its residue; take it from the top of Part B unless the question's own subject supplies a more specific one.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Later Wittgenstein = logical positivism" | He refuses **one criterion** as firmly as **one essence**; positivism is a rival |
| Reporting objections without replies or residues | A "critically discuss" answer needs objection **+** reply **+** residue |
| Treating Ryle, Austin and Strawson as part of this clause | Related method, **separate owners**; name in a clause |
| Silently correcting the 2022 "motion" or the 2025 quotation mark | Reproduce the printed wording, then gloss |
| Ending a 15- or 20-mark answer without a ruling | The verdict bank exists for exactly this |
| "He denied inner mental states" | §304 explicitly refuses that reading |

#### Revision notes

- **Triple refusal:** no single **criterion**, no explanatory **theory**, no **legislation** over use.
- **Grid:** early Wittgenstein (depiction / logical form) · positivism (one criterion) · Descartes and empiricism (inner foundation) · later Wittgenstein (use, no essence) · Ryle, Austin, Strawson (cousins, separate owners).
- **Ledger, ranked:** self-application · normativity of use · Kripke · quietism · behaviourism · relativism · Crusoe · circularity of practice. Each with **reply** and **residue**.
- **Directive decoder:** "discuss use" = contrast then account · "critically examine private language" = five-step core then defence then ruling · "elucidate a remark" = §, doctrine, work done · "solipsism" = dissolution without a proof of other minds · "compare" = specific changes and pressures · "establish tenability" = untenability test, refutation, concession · "assess family resemblance" = denies/permits + objection + reply.
- **Provenance:** posthumous 1953, Anscombe and Rhees · 4th edn 2009 Hacker and Schulte, Part II → **PPF**, renumbered · §1–§693 stable · §43's hedge · *feiert* = idles · *Blue Book* dictated 1933–35, published 1958 · *On Certainty* 1949–51, published 1969 · Kripke 1982 = "Kripke's Wittgenstein" · duck-rabbit = **Jastrow 1899**.
- **Printed anomalies:** 2022 "**motion**"; 2025 **unmatched opening quotation** before "logical structure". Reproduce both.

## BASIC MCQS / REMEDIATION

### 32 original MCQs — 24 core diagnostics and 8 remedial error-targeting drills

**Answer placement:** each correct option was fixed **independently**, so the key sequence is unpatterned rather than rotated, and no cycle such as `ABCD` can be used to guess. Options are matched for length, specificity, grammar and visible formatting so that the answer cannot be spotted by shape alone. Every question carries four question-specific option explanations and one unique examiner trap.

**Coverage:** the twenty-four core questions run across the whole printed clause — *Meaning and Use; Language-games; Critique of Private Language* — together with the status of the Augustinian picture, the §43 hedge, what "use" excludes, understanding as mastery of a technique, the ambiguity of ostension and the prepared place, samples and the standard metre, family resemblance and the thread, the builders and objects of comparison, §23's list and its closing clause, surface and depth grammar, agreement in judgments, the lion remark, the three parts of §201, §202's ban on private rule-following, Kripke's provenance, §243's target, the diary and its manometer variant, the beetle's exact conclusion, criteria against symptoms, and §580 with the behaviourism reply. The eight remedial drills attack the confusions that cost marks every year: the posthumous status of the *Investigations*, the 2009 renumbering and Jastrow's figure, colour exclusion as a pressure rather than a refutation, the boundary against logical positivism, dissolution against refutation in the treatment of solipsism, the solitary-speaker objection, the scope of "leaves everything as it is", and the ownership fence around Topics 06, 07, 11 and Paper II.

#### MCQ 1. The status of the Augustinian picture of language

In §1–4 of the *Investigations*, what verdict does Wittgenstein pass on the picture of language he draws from Augustine's *Confessions*?

A. That it is straightforwardly false, since no word whatever acquires its meaning by being correlated with an object that speakers can indicate.

B. That it fits one primitive language-game exactly, and is defective only as a claim about the essence of all language.

C. That it is a correct account of how children learn language, though a poor account of how adults later come to use it.

D. That it is unintelligible, because the notion of a word standing for an object cannot be given any coherent sense at all.

**Answer: B.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: Wittgenstein grants that the picture works for "table" or "chair", and §2–4 construct a language for which it is exactly right.
- **B:** Correct: the builders' language of "block", "pillar", "slab" and "beam" is offered as a case the picture describes perfectly, which fixes its status as one game generalised into an essence.
- **C:** Incorrect: this makes the point developmental, whereas §28–32 attack the picture's adequacy at every stage, including adult ostensive teaching.
- **D:** Incorrect: unintelligibility is a verdict Wittgenstein reserves for sentences with no specified use, and naming plainly has a use in the games he describes.

**Examiner trap 1:** Writing "Wittgenstein refuted Augustine's theory of meaning" throws away the characteristic later move, which is to shrink an alleged essence into one case among many rather than to declare it false.

#### MCQ 2. What §43 actually states

Which one of the following reproduces the doctrinal content of §43 most accurately?

A. The meaning of a word is its use in the language, a definition Wittgenstein offers as covering every occasion on which the word "meaning" is employed by any speaker.

B. The meaning of a word is the object it stands for, except in those cases where no object can be found, which are handled by the notion of a language-game.

C. The meaning of a word is fixed by the speaker's intention in using it, since intention is what selects one use of a word rather than another on a given occasion.

D. For a large class of cases — though not for all — in which we employ the word "meaning", it can be defined thus: the meaning of a word is its use in the language.

**Answer: D.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: it drops the scope limiter, and §40's separation of a name's bearer from its meaning shows the hedge is doctrine rather than politeness.
- **B:** Incorrect: this preserves the referential model as the default and demotes use to a residual category, reversing the order of §1–12.
- **C:** Incorrect: it reinstates a private mental determinant of meaning, which §258 goes on to dismantle at length.
- **D:** Correct: the remark is stated with its own limitation, and the limitation is what prevents "use" from becoming a second essence.

**Examiner trap 2:** Quoting the remark without "for a large class of cases — though not for all" is the commonest misquotation in this syllabus item, and it converts a careful observation into the slogan Wittgenstein spent §65–71 avoiding.

#### MCQ 3. What "use" does not mean

Consider the following glosses on Wittgenstein's term "use".

1. How frequently the word occurs in a population of speakers.
2. The norm-governed role the word has within a practice.
3. Whatever application the individual speaker finds convenient.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct as an account of §43's "use"?

A. 2 only

B. 1 and 2 only

C. 2 and 3 only

D. 1, 2 and 3

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: use is a role with standards of correct and incorrect application, which is why a rare sentence is fully meaningful and a speaker can misuse a word.
- **B:** Incorrect: frequency is a statistical property of a population and has no bearing on whether a given application is correct.
- **C:** Incorrect: statement 3 makes the individual the sole judge, which is precisely the position §258 shows to be empty.
- **D:** Incorrect: it combines both errors, leaving "use" with neither normativity nor determinacy.

**Examiner trap 3:** Treating "use" as "usage" invites the objection that meaning is decided by majority behaviour, which recreates at population level the collapse of "right" into "seems right" that the private-language argument exploits.

#### MCQ 4. Understanding a word

On the account developed at §143–155 and stated at §199, in what does understanding a word consist?

A. In a distinctive inner experience of comprehension that accompanies the word and is available to introspection whenever the word is grasped.

B. In possessing a mental image of the object designated, which can then be compared with new cases in order to settle whether the word applies.

C. In mastery of a technique, exhibited in going on correctly with the word in the appropriate circumstances of a shared practice.

D. In having formulated to oneself a definition of the word in terms of necessary and sufficient conditions for its correct application.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: §151–152 show the flash of comprehension can occur and be followed by failure, and that fluent understanding often involves no such episode at all.
- **B:** Incorrect: an image is another sign standing in need of application, so it postpones the question rather than answering it.
- **C:** Correct: §199 states that to understand a sentence is to understand a language, and to understand a language is to be master of a technique.
- **D:** Incorrect: §66–71 show that many concepts admit no such definition, yet are understood perfectly well by competent speakers.

**Examiner trap 4:** Identifying understanding with performance turns the account into behaviourism; the correct statement is that performance is the criterion, and criteria are defeasible in both directions.

#### MCQ 5. The ambiguity of ostensive definition

What does Wittgenstein establish at §28 by pointing at two nuts and saying "This is called 'two'"?

A. That ostensive teaching never in fact succeeds, so children must acquire their first vocabulary by some entirely non-ostensive route.

B. That the learner will always take the word to name the objects indicated rather than any of their properties, which is why naming feels basic.

C. That the pointing is unnecessary, since the accompanying words already convey everything required for the learner to fix the term.

D. That the gesture leaves the lesson undetermined, since "two" might be taken for a number, a colour, a shape, a material or a proper name.

**Answer: D.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: Wittgenstein never denies that ostensive teaching works; he denies that the pointing is what makes it work.
- **B:** Incorrect: §28 lists exactly the range of readings the gesture permits, and the object-naming reading is only one of them.
- **C:** Incorrect: §29–30 show that adding "this number" merely borrows an understanding of "number" and restarts the same difficulty.
- **D:** Correct: the remark states that an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted in every case, which is the argument's premise.

**Examiner trap 5:** Reading §28 as scepticism about ostension inverts its purpose: it is an inventory of what ostension needs, not a denial that ostension teaches.

#### MCQ 6. The chess-king and the prepared place

At §31 Wittgenstein says that being shown a piece and told "This is the king" teaches the use of the piece only under a certain condition. What is that condition?

A. That the learner already occupies the practice far enough to recognise that pieces have roles and that a word is being introduced for one of them.

B. That the learner has already been given a complete verbal statement of the rules of chess, including every legal move available to each piece.

C. That the learner already possesses the concept of a king, since otherwise the sentence conveys no information that he could put to any use.

D. That the learner is able to reproduce the shape of the piece from memory, since recognition of the shape is what the ostensive definition supplies.

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: §31 requires that "the place is already prepared", and the preparation is entry into the practice — attending, imitating, being corrected — not possession of the concept taught.
- **B:** Incorrect: an explicit rulebook is neither necessary nor sufficient, and §68 and 84 note that games are not everywhere circumscribed by rules.
- **C:** Incorrect: requiring prior possession of the concept would make learning it impossible and turn the account viciously circular.
- **D:** Incorrect: §31 mentions the shape of the king as something already known to a chess-learner, not as what the definition delivers.

**Examiner trap 6:** Glossing "the place is already prepared" as "already has the concept" makes Wittgenstein's condition unsatisfiable; the preparation is practical and pre-conceptual, which is why §5–6 place training before explanation.

#### MCQ 7. The standard metre

Why does §50 say that of the standard metre in Paris one can say neither that it is one metre long nor that it is not?

A. Because its length has been shown by later measurement to differ marginally from the metre as that unit is now internationally defined.

B. Because the bar possesses an extraordinary metaphysical property that removes it from the ordinary range of things to which lengths are ascribed.

C. Because no measurement of any object can be exact, so that every ascription of length is strictly speaking neither true nor false of that object.

D. Because that is its peculiar role in the language-game of measuring: the bar functions as the standard rather than as an object measured by it.

**Answer: D.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: the remark is grammatical rather than metrological, and it would hold whatever the bar's physical history.
- **B:** Incorrect: §50 explicitly says that this is not to ascribe any extraordinary property to it.
- **C:** Incorrect: general inexactness would affect every object equally and would not single out the standard at all.
- **D:** Correct: the bar belongs to the means of representation, which is also why §16 counts colour samples as part of the language rather than as its subject-matter.

**Examiner trap 7:** Missing §50 costs marks in the private-language answer, because a sample that carried its own instructions would let a remembered sensation serve as a private standard.

#### MCQ 8. What family resemblance denies

Which one of the following states most precisely what the family-resemblance considerations of §66–67 deny?

A. That any two things called by the same general term ever share a feature, since the network consists only of resemblances of detail.

B. That concepts can be taught by examples, since examples always underdetermine the boundary of the concept being conveyed.

C. That a general term must be backed by a feature common to all its instances if speakers are to use it correctly and intelligibly.

D. That sharply bounded concepts exist at all, since inspection shows every concept to possess a blurred and extensible edge.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: many games plainly share several features; the survey shows only that no feature runs through all of them.
- **B:** Incorrect: §69–71 treat teaching by examples as the normal and adequate method for such concepts.
- **C:** Correct: the target is the requirement expressed by "there *must* be something common", which §66 identifies as a demand carried to the facts.
- **D:** Incorrect: "triangle" and "prime number" retain strict definitions, and Wittgenstein nowhere denies this.

**Examiner trap 8:** Overstating the doctrine into "definitions are impossible" makes it self-defeating, since the claim would then apply to the definition of family resemblance itself.

#### MCQ 9. The thread, and the status of the boundary

Consider the following statements about §67–71.

1. The strength of a spun thread lies in one fibre running through its whole length.
2. We do not know the boundary of "game" because none has been drawn.
3. A boundary may be drawn for a special purpose without discovering a hidden one.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

A. 2 and 3 only

B. 1 and 2 only

C. 3 only

D. 1, 2 and 3

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: §69 states that no boundary has been drawn and that one may be drawn for a special purpose, which is a decision rather than a discovery.
- **B:** Incorrect: statement 1 reverses §67, whose whole point is that the strength lies in the overlapping of many fibres.
- **C:** Incorrect: statement 2 is also correct, and omitting it loses the distinction between an undrawn boundary and a hidden one.
- **D:** Incorrect: it includes the inversion of the thread analogy, which is the very image Wittgenstein uses against the demand for a core.

**Examiner trap 9:** Saying that the boundary is unknown because it is hidden restores the essence-demand in epistemic clothing; §69's point is that nothing has been concealed, because nothing has been drawn.

#### MCQ 10. What the builders' language establishes

The language of §2, consisting only of "block", "pillar", "slab" and "beam", is introduced in order to show which one of the following?

A. That the earliest human languages consisted of single-word commands, out of which sentential structure gradually developed over long periods of time.

B. That a language can be complete for its purpose while consisting of trained responses to calls rather than of descriptions of anything.

C. That every language, however complex, can in principle be translated without remainder into a vocabulary of four basic terms.

D. That naming is the fundamental linguistic function, from which ordering, questioning and describing are all derived by extension.

**Answer: B.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: §130–131 warn that the invented games are objects of comparison, not hypotheses about the origins of language.
- **B:** Correct: Wittgenstein instructs us to conceive it as a complete primitive language, and §19–20 show that "Slab!" functions as an order because of its place in the transaction.
- **C:** Incorrect: no reductive translation is proposed, and §23's list of countless kinds runs in the opposite direction.
- **D:** Incorrect: the example is designed to displace naming from that position by exhibiting a working language that does not describe.

**Examiner trap 10:** Narrating the builders without stating what they establish converts an argument into an anecdote, and the argument is that completeness for a purpose is all a language needs.

#### MCQ 11. Objects of comparison

According to §130–131, what is the methodological status of the simple language-games Wittgenstein invents?

A. Idealised models which display, in simplified form, the structure that our own language possesses in a more complicated arrangement.

B. Preliminary hypotheses about the earliest stages of language, to be confirmed or refuted by anthropological and historical evidence.

C. Objects of comparison meant to throw light on our language by way of dissimilarities as much as by way of similarities to it.

D. Thought-experiments intended to establish which features of language are necessary and which are merely contingent additions.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: treating them as simplified models reinstates the assumption that one structure underlies all language, which the games are designed to loosen.
- **B:** Incorrect: no empirical claim is made, and §130–131 explicitly disclaim the role of preliminary studies awaiting confirmation.
- **C:** Correct: the remark says the games are set up as objects of comparison illuminating by dissimilarity as well as by likeness.
- **D:** Incorrect: sorting the necessary from the contingent is precisely the essence-seeking project that family resemblance dismantles.

**Examiner trap 11:** Calling the invented games "models of language" is a quiet reintroduction of the *Tractatus* assumption, and an examiner alert to method will notice it immediately.

#### MCQ 12. §23 and its closing clause

Which one of the following best captures the argumentative function of the list at §23?

A. To classify the sentence-kinds of natural language into an exhaustive taxonomy on which a systematic theory of meaning can be founded.

B. To exhibit a deliberately disorderly multiplicity, which is not fixed once for all, and to conclude that speaking is part of an activity or form of life.

C. To demonstrate that all the apparent varieties of sentence reduce, on a sufficiently careful analysis, to the three basic types of assertion, question and command.
