- **Competing readings.** A **quietist/therapeutic** reading, on which the remark is a piece of method; a moderate **positive use-theory**, which treats it as the beginning of an account later developed by others; and a **hybrid** reading, on which §43 is a rule of philosophical procedure rather than a universal definition — which is what the hedge "for a large class of cases" most naturally suggests.
- **Objection.** If Wittgenstein offers no theory, his remarks look empty, and the appearance of explanation in the *Investigations* is then an embarrassment rather than a contribution.
- **Counter-reply.** The point is precisely that philosophical clarity often comes from reminders of ordinary grammar rather than from positing hidden mechanisms — and the presence of arguments, comparisons and reductios in the text shows that "no theory" was never "no reasoning".
- **Safe 15/20 use.** ⚠️ Works best as the final evaluative note in a transition question (2023, 2025).
- ❌ **What not to over-study.** Do not import the whole quietism debate into a basic language-games answer.
- **Promote to Core if:** a future PYQ asks directly whether the later Wittgenstein is therapeutic or theoretical.

### A4. What the promotion record shows ⚠️

⚠️ The **rule-following paradox itself** and the **one-line Kripke summary** were promoted into Core because a candidate cannot take a defended side on the private-language argument without them; only the second-order evaluative question in A1 remains optional. ⚠️ Modules A2 and A3 would be promoted only if a future question asked directly whether language is essentially social, or whether the later philosophy is therapeutic rather than theoretical. ⚠️ Stating this ordering is itself a mark of judgement, because it shows the candidate knows which optional material has already earned its place in a core answer and which has not.

### Conceptual links — not current-affairs items

⚠️ No dated official or academic development is claimed for this clause. Its live relevance is **disciplinary and static**: meaning-as-use, criteria and forms of life underwrite the present-day agenda of the philosophy of language and mind — pragmatics, use-based and inferentialist semantics, the philosophy of psychology, and debates about rule-following in the theory of content. That is stated as analysis, not as a news event, and no blog or commentary item is promoted to a current-affairs anchor.

### Source trail for this block

Ludwig Wittgenstein, *Philosophical Investigations* · Saul Kripke, *Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language* (1982) · Gordon Baker · P. M. S. Hacker · John McDowell · Marie McGinn · Stanley Cavell.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic. They cover every subtopic in the Basic block and preserve the Basic / Optional-Advanced distinction; they do not repeat the teaching introductions. Read them after the sessions, not instead of them. The complete ASCII master flow diagram closes the package.

### A. The clause, its limbs and what is not owned

- **Three printed limbs, one method.** *Meaning and Use* fixes how meaning is to be investigated; *Language-games* is the unit in which use is organised; *Critique of Private Language* is what the investigation does when it is turned on the inner life. Write them in that order, because the third is a **consequence** of the first two.
- **Four indispensable supports** without which the limbs cannot be executed: **ostensive definition** (why pointing is not a foundation), **family resemblance** (why no essence is required), **rule-following** (where normativity comes from), and **therapy** (what the whole exercise is for).
- ❌ **Not owned:** picture theory, logical atomism, saying and showing, *"Whereof one cannot speak"* as **doctrine** (**Topic 06** — a target here, not a limb) · verification, pseudo-statements, the Vienna Circle (**Topic 07** — a rival, not an ally) · Quine's holism and Strawson's persons (**Topic 11**) · fideist and religious-language uses of *form of life* (**Paper II**) · Ryle, Austin and speech-act theory (broader ordinary-language owners).
- **Corpus signal:** **7 of 112** parts, 2018–2025; **2021** is the only year without an owned part, and that year's Wittgenstein part — 2021 Q4(c), *"Whereof one cannot speak"* — belongs to **Topic 06**.

### B. Chronology and provenance — examiner-checked

- **1921** *Logisch-Philosophische Abhandlung* in German; **1922** bilingual *Tractatus*. ❌ Never date the English text 1921.
- **1922–28** Wittgenstein outside academic philosophy. **1929** returns to Cambridge; **"Some Remarks on Logical Form"** acknowledges **colour exclusion**; the repair fails and he later disowned the paper. **1929–32** conversations with **Schlick** and **Waismann** — he was **never** a Circle member.
- **1933–35** the ***Blue and Brown Books*** are **dictated** (published **1958**). Source of the **criterion/symptom** distinction and the **angina** example. Say *"in the Blue Book"*, not "Wittgenstein wrote".
- **1936–49** *Investigations* Part I composed; **1945** preface. **1949–51** the notes later published as ***On Certainty***. **1951** Wittgenstein dies, having published one philosophical book and one article.
- **1953** ***Philosophical Investigations***, **posthumous**, edited by **G. E. M. Anscombe and Rush Rhees**, Anscombe translating, German and English on facing pages; Part I = **§1–§693**, numbering identical across editions.
- **1969** *On Certainty*. **1982** **Kripke**, *Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language* — "as it struck Kripke"; **Kripkenstein**. **2009** 4th edition revised by **Hacker and Schulte**: Part II renamed ***Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment*** and **renumbered**, so cite "Part II §xi / PPF".
- **1899** the **duck-rabbit** is **Jastrow's**; Wittgenstein reproduces it. ❌ Never attribute the drawing to him.
- ⚠️ **Four marks available from this rail alone:** the posthumous and editorially arranged status of the *Investigations*; the 1921/1922 split; the 2009 renaming and renumbering; and that Kripke's reading is a **reading**.

### C. Meaning and use — the thesis and its hedge

- **§1** Augustine's picture: every word names an object; sentences combine names; meaning = the correlated object. **§2–4** the **builders' language** ("block", "pillar", "slab", "beam") fits it **exactly** — so the picture is **one primitive language-game generalised into an essence**, not a falsehood.
- **§11** words are **tools in a tool-box**; **§12** locomotive handles look alike and work differently — uniform appearance, diverse function.
- **§40** the **bearer** of a name ≠ the **meaning** of a name; **§79** proper names handled separately — both show why §43 is hedged.
- ✅ **§43:** *"For a large class of cases — though not for all — in which we employ the word 'meaning' it can be defined thus: the meaning of a word is its use in the language."* ❌ Never quote without the hedge.
- **Use = a norm-governed role** in a practice: circumstances, training, permitted moves, standards of correctness. ❌ Not frequency · ❌ not usefulness · ❌ not the speaker's intention · ❌ not a licence.
- **§199** to understand a language is to be **master of a technique**; **§143–155** every candidate inner item can occur without understanding, and understanding without it.
- ⚠️ **Live tension, conceded:** how established use is distinguished from **innovation** and from **error** — poetic extension, metaphor, technical redefinition.

### D. Ostensive definition, samples and training

- **§28** an ostensive definition *"can be variously interpreted in every case"* — number, colour, shape, material, proper name, direction.
- **§29–30** the category-word repair borrows the understanding it was to supply: *"one has already to know (or be able to do) something in order to be capable of asking a thing's name."*
- **§31 chess-king:** "This is the king" teaches only one who knows the shape of a game; the definition works **when the place is already prepared**. Preparation = occupying the practice, **not** possessing the concept.
- **§5–6 training (*Abrichtung*)** precedes explanation; the German is the word used for training an animal, and the choice is deliberate — mark this as interpretation.
- **§16, §50** samples belong to the **means of representation**; the standard metre is neither one metre long nor not — a **role**, not an extraordinary property.
- **Structure:** ambiguity → failed repair → prepared place → **regress** → **trained action** (the terminus §201 names).
- ⚠️ **Direct consequence:** private ostension is ostension **minus** training, correction and prepared place. §258 is the heir of §28.

### E. Family resemblance

- **§65** the interlocutor's challenge: you never say what the **essence** of language is. Family resemblance is the **reply**, and the 2018 verb "help … to uphold" asks for that service.
- **§66** *"Don't say: 'There must be something common' … but look and see."* The survey defeats **amusement, competition, winning and losing, skill, rules, luck**; result = *"a complicated network of similarities overlapping and criss-crossing."*
- **§67** the name (**family resemblances**: build, features, colour of eyes, gait, temperament) and the **thread**: strength lies in **the overlapping of many fibres**, not in one running the whole length.
- **§68** number is itself a family; **§69** boundaries are **undrawn**, not hidden, and may be drawn **for a special purpose**; **§70** tennis has no rule for throwing height; **§71** the indistinct photograph is often **exactly what we need**.
- ❌ **Four things it does not claim:** that no concept has necessary and sufficient conditions · that no two games share a feature · that definition is worthless · that anything may be called a game.
- **Objection:** everything resembles everything. **Reply:** practice selects the relevant similarities. **Residue:** whether use always bounds the concept tightly enough.

### F. Language-games and grammar

- **§7** definition: *"the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven."*
- **Three uses of the term:** invented primitive languages (§2, 8, 48) · the training processes by which language is learned (§7) · actual complete practices (§23).
- **§2 builders**; **§19–20** whether "Slab!" is a word or a sentence is settled by **role**, not by length.
- **§130–131** invented games are **objects of comparison**, illuminating by **dissimilarity** as much as by likeness. ❌ Not simplified models, not hypotheses about origins.
- **§23** countless kinds; the multiplicity is **not fixed** — new games arise, old ones are forgotten; the list runs from orders and measurements through hypotheses, tables, story-telling, play-acting, riddles, jokes, arithmetic and translation to asking, thanking, cursing, greeting and **praying**; the closing clause: *"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life."* The **disorder is the argument**, and **describing appears as one item**.
- **Grammar:** §371 essence is expressed by grammar · §373 grammar tells what kind of object anything is · §496 description, not explanation · **§664 surface** vs **depth** grammar.
- **Diagnostic model:** **§350–351** "It is 5 o'clock on the sun" — impeccable surface, **no specified game**; not false. This is the exact verdict-form later used on "S".

### G. Forms of life

- **§19** *"To imagine a language means to imagine a form of life"* — said while asking whether "Slab!" is a word or a sentence.
- **§23** *"the speaking of language is part of an activity, or of a form of life."*
- **§241** the objection and the reply: *"It is what human beings say that is true and false; and they agree in the language they use. That is not agreement in opinions but in form of life."*
- **§242** agreement in **definitions** *and* in **judgments**; the test: wildly varying measurement results give **no measuring**, not inaccurate measuring — removal yields **no subject-matter**, not mass error.
- **§217** bedrock, spade turned, *"This is simply what I do"* — a **rule-following** remark; extending it to define form of life is **interpretation**.
- **§25, §415** commanding, questioning, chatting belong to our **natural history**.
- **Lion remark** (**Part II §xi / PPF**): a condition of **intelligibility**, not a proof of sealed cultures — Wittgenstein describes imagined alien practices throughout.
- **Verdict:** tenable as the **enabling background**; untenable as a theory of truth, of incommensurability, or of ultimate justification. **Gap:** no theory of rational adjudication between practices.

### H. Rule-following

- **§138–155** understanding "in a flash": no inner item **is** the understanding.
- **§185** the pupil at 1000 writes 1004, 1008, 1012 — *"But I went on in the same way."* The divergence is at the level of **reaction**.
- **§201 (i)** *"no course of action could be determined by a rule, because every course of action can be made out to accord with the rule"* — and so also to **conflict**, leaving neither accord nor conflict.
- **§201 (ii)** the **diagnosis**: we give one interpretation after another; **interpretation** = **substituting one expression of the rule for another**, so the regress is **structural**.
- **§201 (iii)** the **resolution**: *"a way of grasping a rule which is not an interpretation, but which is exhibited in what we call 'obeying the rule' and 'going against it' in actual cases."*
- **§202** obeying a rule is a **practice**; thinking one obeys is not obeying; hence **no private rule-following** — the premise imported into §258.
- ⚠️ **Normativity ≠ regularity:** a practice has **mistakes** and **standards**; a regularity has exceptions and frequencies. ❌ Community behaviour is **not** the criterion, or "correct" collapses into "usual".
- **Kripke 1982:** sceptical paradox + **sceptical solution**; "as it struck Kripke"; opposed by **Baker and Hacker** and by **McDowell**. Ruling: **valuable, exegetically doubtful**.

### I. The critique of private language

- **§243** monologuists **set aside** → the real target: words referring to *"what can only be known to the person speaking; to his immediate private sensations"*, so that **another person cannot understand the language**.
- ❌ **Four non-targets:** a secret code (a public language encrypted) · talking to oneself · private thoughts · physical **solitude**.
- **§258** diary "S"; inward ostension; **no criterion of correctness**; *"whatever is going to seem right to me is right"* → *"that only means that here we can't talk about 'right'."*
- **§260** "I believe this is S again" — perhaps you believe that you believe it. **§265** several copies of the **same newspaper**; an imagined table is not a table; justification appeals to something **independent**. **§270** the **manometer** variant — the sign acquires a use, and **correct inner identification becomes irrelevant**.
- **§293 beetle:** contents might **differ**, **change**, or the box be **empty** → on the **object-and-designation** model the object **drops out of consideration as irrelevant**. Expressly **conditional**; a supplement to §258, not the primary argument.
- **The five-step core:** rule → criterion of correctness → none available privately → "correct" has no application → no rule, no meaning, **no private language**.
- **Objections and residues:** **Crusoe** (logical privacy, not solitude; community question ❓ disputed) · **memory** (public checks use memory **plus** something independent; the reply is contested ❓) · **behaviourism** (answered in J) · **phenomenology** (§293 attacks a **model**; §304 protects the phenomenon ❓).

### J. Sensation language — criteria, avowals and the behaviourism reply

- **Source:** *Blue Book*, **dictated 1933–35**, **published 1958**.
- **Criterion (*Kriterium*)** grammatical, taught with the word, **defeasible** | **symptom (*Symptom*)** inductive, discovered by experience.
- **Angina:** the **bacillus** is the criterion (by definition); the **inflamed throat** is a symptom (by experience). **Pain:** pain-behaviour **in appropriate circumstances** is the criterion; a raised pulse or an actor's groan is a symptom.
- **§580** *"An 'inner process' stands in need of outward criteria."* Argument: concept = mastery of a technique → mastery needs right and wrong → the circumstances cannot be further **inner** items, since that restarts §258 → therefore **outward** — behaviour, situation, the **surroundings (*Umgebung*)**.
- **§304, 307–308 the reply in four moves:** the denial is of a **picture**, not a phenomenon (§304 *"not a something, but not a nothing either!"*) · §308 **the decisive movement in the conjuring trick** was made at the step we thought innocent · criteria are **defeasible**, so grammatical dependence ≠ **equivalence** · **first/third-person asymmetry** (§244 the verbal expression of pain **replaces** crying; §246 *"I know I am in pain"* is sayable of me only as a joke).
- ⚠️ **Not every** first-person psychological sentence is an avowal; past-tense uses are reports.
- **§354** the grammar **fluctuates** between criteria and symptoms — take a side: candour about concepts, or an admission that the key notion is not sharp.

### K. Solipsism

- **Early:** *Tractatus* **5.6** the limits of my language mean the limits of my world · **5.62** what the solipsist means is **quite correct** but **cannot be said** · **5.64** strictly carried out it **coincides with pure realism**. Mechanism: **saying and showing** (Topic 06's doctrine).
- **Later:** the **route changes** — from the **limit** of language to the **grammar** of the solipsist's words, and from saying/showing to **criterion against mere seeming**.
- **The dilemma:** either his terms are fixed by present seeming → §258 applies, no rule, nothing stated; or they are fixed by stable, repeatable techniques → he has conceded the **public-type practice** his thesis denies.
- **Support:** §244 avowal · §246 "only I can know" is false in one way and nonsense in another · §293 beetle · §580 outward criteria — together dismantling the **asymmetry** the solipsist needs, without an inductive argument for other minds.
- **Verdict: dissolution, not refutation**; the thesis is **self-undermining**. ❌ Not proved: other minds · the external world · the impossibility of solitude. **§303** "try, in a real case, to doubt someone else's pain" concerns where doubt **has application**.

### L. Philosophy as therapy

- **§38** *"philosophical problems arise when language goes on holiday"* — German *wenn die Sprache feiert*, i.e. language **idles**, an engine running without load.
- **§109** philosophy is *"a battle against the bewitchment of our intelligence by means of language"*; **description** replaces explanation.
- **§115** *"a picture held us captive… it lay in our language"* — the captor is a **picture**, and the captivity is **linguistic**.
- **§122** **perspicuous representation (*übersichtliche Darstellung*)** → *"seeing connections"*; find and **invent intermediate cases**. **§123** *"I don't know my way about"* — **disorientation**, not ignorance. **§127** assembling **reminders**. **§128** theses would be undebatable. **§129** hidden by **simplicity and familiarity**.
- **§133** *"not a philosophical method, though there are indeed methods, like different therapies"*; the real discovery makes it possible to **stop**. **§255** treatment of an illness. **§309** *"To show the fly the way out of the fly-bottle."*
- **§124** *"Philosophy may in no way interfere with the actual use of language… It leaves everything as it is."* ⚠️ The **first clause** bounds it: the subject is **grammar**, not society.
- **Three objections:** **quietism** (bounded, but the discipline is narrowed severely) · **self-application** (real force; mitigated by §133's plural; structurally inherited from the *Tractatus* ladder at **6.54**) · **persistent problems** (the reply is **unfalsifiable as stated** — the most serious methodological complaint).

### M. Bounded enrichment, comparison and exam craft

- **Aspect-seeing:** Part II §xi / **PPF**; figure = **Jastrow 1899**; *"I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently"*; **the dawning of an aspect (*das Aufleuchten eines Aspekts*)**; *"half visual experience, half thought"*; seeing the rabbit-aspect presupposes **familiarity with the shape of a rabbit**; **aspect-blindness** links it to the **experience of meaning**. Pressures the innocent **Given** without refuting every acquaintance theory. **Budget:** 10 marks none · 15 marks one sentence · 20 marks two.
- ***On Certainty***: notes **1949–51**, published **1969**, **unrevised**; answers **Moore**; **hinge** propositions stand fast and make doubting possible. Compare with §241–242 and §217 — a **comparison**, not a doctrine of this clause.
- **Comparison grid:** early Wittgenstein (depiction via logical form) · logical positivism (**one criterion**) · Descartes and classical empiricism (**inner foundation**) · later Wittgenstein (**use, no essence**) · Ryle, Austin, Strawson (cousins, separate owners).
- **The triple refusal:** no single **criterion**, no explanatory **theory**, no **legislation** over ordinary use.
- **Criticism ledger, ranked:** self-application · normativity of use · Kripke · quietism · behaviourism · relativism · Crusoe · circularity of practice. Each needs **objection + reply + residue**.
- **Directive decoder:** "discuss use" = the contrast replaced, then the account · "critically examine private language" = five-step core, then criteria/symptoms, then a ruling · "elucidate a remark" = §, doctrine, work done, and what it does **not** claim · "solipsism" = dissolution without a proof of other minds · "compare early and later" = specific changes and their pressures · "establish tenability" = untenability test, refutation, concession · "assess family resemblance" = denies/permits, objection, reply · "comment / do you agree" = a **defended ruling in line 1**.
- **Printed anomalies to reproduce, never to correct:** **2022** prints **"motion"**; **2025** carries an **unmatched opening quotation** before "logical structure".
- **Answer spine, all lengths:** *state the change of question → give the limb with its exact texts → give the support that makes it executable → name the objection and its residue → close with a defended ruling.*
- ❌ **Never write:** that Wittgenstein denied inner mental states (§304) · that the private-language argument targets codes or soliloquy · that "meaning is use" is a general definition (§43) · that language being public proves other minds exist.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/33: SAMPLES BELONG TO THE MEANS OF REPRESENTATION
==============================================================================

   PI 50  Of the STANDARD METRE in Paris one can say NEITHER that it is one metre
   long NOR that it is not - 'this is, of course, not to ascribe any extraordinary

   MEASURING with a metre-rule'.
                                     |
                                     v
   WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE THIRD LIMB
     If a sample were an ORDINARY OBJECT whose nature fixed the word's
     application, a PRIVATE sample - a remembered sensation - might do the same
     job in a private language. 50 removes that possibility IN ADVANCE: a sample
     is a STANDARD only because a PRACTICE of comparing, applying and correcting
     has given it that office.
   WHAT SURVIVES  ostension WORKS; samples are INDISPENSABLE instruments. What
   fails is only the PHILOSOPHICAL OFFICE of grounding meaning from outside all
   practice.
   ANSWER LINE  Meaning is constrained but not arbitrary: NO ESSENCE IS NOT NO NORM.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/33: THE THREAD, AND THE EXACT STATUS OF THE BOUNDARY
==============================================================================
   PI 67  'I can think of no better expression to characterise these similarities
   than FAMILY RESEMBLANCES; for the various resemblances between members of a
   family - BUILD, FEATURES, COLOUR OF EYES, GAIT, TEMPERAMENT, etc. etc. -
   overlap and criss-cross in the same way.'
   THE THREAD  'we extend our concept ... as in SPINNING A THREAD we twist fibre on
   fibre. And THE STRENGTH OF THE THREAD DOES NOT RESIDE IN THE FACT THAT SOME ONE
   FIBRE RUNS THROUGH ITS WHOLE LENGTH, BUT IN THE OVERLAPPING OF MANY FIBRES.'
     -> STRENGTH WITHOUT A CORE. This single image answers the anxiety that a
        concept without an essence must fall apart.
   THE BOUNDARY (68-71)
     68  NUMBER is itself a family: cardinal, rational, real - fibres, not species
     69  We can DRAW a boundary FOR A SPECIAL PURPOSE. We do not know the
         boundaries BECAUSE NONE HAVE BEEN DRAWN - not because they are HIDDEN
     70  'But then the use of the word is unregulated!' - it is not EVERYWHERE
         circumscribed by rules; NOR IS TENNIS, which has no rule for how high one
         may throw the ball - and tennis is still a game
     71  'Is an INDISTINCT PHOTOGRAPH always preferable to a sharp one? Isn't the
         indistinct one OFTEN EXACTLY WHAT WE NEED?'
   X TRAP  'the boundary is unknown because it is hidden' restores the essence-
   demand in epistemic clothing.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 15/33: FOUR DENIALS, THE STANDARD OBJECTION, AND THE SERVICE RENDERED
==============================================================================
   FOUR THINGS THE DOCTRINE DOES NOT CLAIM - each is a marked error
     X that NO concept has necessary and sufficient conditions ('triangle' does)
     X that no two games have anything in common (many share several features)
     X that definition is impossible or worthless
     X that ANYTHING may therefore be called a game
   THE OBJECTION  Everything resembles everything in SOME respect: a chair
   resembles chess in being man-made. A network of 'similarities' constrains
   nothing.
   THE REPLY  RELEVANCE IS FIXED BY THE PRACTICE, not by bare resemblance - by what
   we do with games, how the word is taught, which extensions are ACCEPTED and
   which are CORRECTED. The concept is bounded by USE, not by definition.
   THE RESIDUE  Whether use bounds it tightly enough in EVERY case is genuinely
   open. Concede it: that is the 'critically' mark.
   THE SERVICE RENDERED - the 2018 answer in four steps
     1 THE DEMAND DISARMED   refusing to define 'language' is no longer a defect
     2 THE ANALOGY EARNED    'game' IS the paradigm family-resemblance concept
     3 THE PICTURE SUPPLIED  games have TRAINING, RULES, MOVES and STANDARDS
                             WITHOUT an essence - exactly what the Tractatus
                             thought only a shared LOGICAL FORM could give
     4 RELATIVISM BLOCKED    games are norm-governed, so meaning is not arbitrary

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/33: THE BUILDERS, WORKED RATHER THAN TOLD

   PI 19-20  IS 'Slab!' A WORD OR A SENTENCE?
     It may be an elliptical form of 'Bring me a slab' - OR our 'elliptical'
     sentence may be a LENGTHENED form of theirs. Wittgenstein refuses to settle
     it by fiat: WHAT MAKES IT AN ORDER IS ITS PLACE IN THE PRACTICE, NOT ITS
     LENGTH.  <- this is where the example turns from illustration into ARGUMENT

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 18/33: PI 23 - THE LIST, ITS HISTORICITY AND ITS CLOSING CLAUSE
==============================================================================
   'How many kinds of sentence are there? ... THERE ARE COUNTLESS KINDS ... And
   this multiplicity is NOT SOMETHING FIXED, GIVEN ONCE FOR ALL; but NEW TYPES OF
   LANGUAGE, NEW LANGUAGE-GAMES, as we may say, COME INTO EXISTENCE, and others
   BECOME OBSOLETE AND GET FORGOTTEN.'
   THE LIST - deliberately disorderly
     giving orders and obeying them | describing the appearance of an object or
     giving its measurements | constructing an object from a description (a
     drawing) | reporting an event | speculating about an event | forming and
     testing a hypothesis | presenting the results of an experiment in tables and
     diagrams | making up a story, and reading it | play-acting | singing catches |
     guessing riddles | making a joke, telling it | solving a problem in practical
     arithmetic | translating from one language into another | ASKING, THANKING,
     CURSING, GREETING, PRAYING
   THE CLOSING CLAUSE  'the speaking of language is PART OF AN ACTIVITY, OR OF A
   FORM OF LIFE'   <- the textual bridge the 2022 answer must quote
   THREE CONSEQUENCES
     1 the DISORDER IS THE ARGUMENT against one essence
     2 DESCRIBING is ONE ITEM - demoted from the paradigm it was in the Tractatus
       and in logical positivism
     3 LANGUAGE IS HISTORICAL - conceptual change without a theory of it

   only in DEFINITIONS but also (QUEER AS THIS MAY SOUND) IN JUDGMENTS.'

        we should NOT have INACCURATE MEASURING - we should have NO MEASURING.
                                     |
                                     v
   THE ASYMMETRY THAT SETTLES THE QUESTION
     remove the shared background  ->  you do NOT get widespread ERROR
                                       (error presupposes a subject-matter)
                                   ->  you get NO SUBJECT-MATTER AT ALL
     THEREFORE agreement operates ONE LEVEL BELOW truth-value: it is the CONDITION
     OF DETERMINATE ASSERTION, not its GROUND.
     THEREFORE disagreement - including radical disagreement - is EVIDENCE OF a
     shared practice, not evidence against one.
   WORKED PICTURE  two people can argue whether the table is 180 or 182 cm; they
   cannot argue whether laying a rule alongside it is how length is settled.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 23/33: RULE-FOLLOWING - THE SETTING AND THE PUPIL AT 1000

   application; meaning something determines every future step in advance.
   THE PRESSURE (138-155)
     * the formula does not APPLY ITSELF     * the examples are FINITE
     * a mental picture is ANOTHER SIGN, needing application in turn (139-141)
     * the 'aha!' can occur and be followed by FAILURE (151-155)
                                     |

   PI 185  THE PUPIL
     taught the natural numbers, then '+2' up to 1000. Asked to continue, he writes
        ... 996  998  1000  1004  1008  1012
     We say: LOOK WHAT YOU HAVE DONE. He replies: 'BUT I WENT ON IN THE SAME WAY.'
     Repeating the examples does not refute him: 'IT COMES NATURAL TO THIS PERSON
     TO UNDERSTAND OUR ORDER WITH OUR EXPLANATIONS AS WE SHOULD UNDERSTAND THE
     ORDER: "Add 2 up to 1000, 4 up to 2000, 6 up to 3000 and so on."'
   WHAT THE CASE SHOWS  NOT that the pupil is right. NOT that we cannot correct
   him. THAT THE CORRECTION CANNOT BE DERIVED from anything already possessed -
   sign, examples, image, or further explanation.
   NOTE THE PHRASE  'it comes NATURAL to this person' locates the divergence at the
   level of REACTION - which is where the form-of-life background does its work.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 24/33: PI 201 IN THREE PARTS - THE REDUCTIO
==============================================================================
   (i) THE PARADOX
     'THIS WAS OUR PARADOX: no course of action could be determined by a rule,
      because EVERY COURSE OF ACTION CAN BE MADE OUT TO ACCORD WITH THE RULE.'
     'If everything can be made out to accord with the rule, then it can also be
      made out to CONFLICT with it. And so there would be NEITHER ACCORD NOR
      CONFLICT here.'
   (ii) THE DIAGNOSIS
     'It can be seen that there is a MISUNDERSTANDING here from the mere fact that
      in the course of our argument we GIVE ONE INTERPRETATION AFTER ANOTHER; as
      if each one contented us at least for a moment, until we thought of yet
      another standing behind it.'
     KEY GLOSS  INTERPRETATION = 'THE SUBSTITUTION OF ONE EXPRESSION OF THE RULE
     FOR ANOTHER'.  Because an interpretation is ANOTHER EXPRESSION, it stands in
     exactly the need it was introduced to satisfy -> THE REGRESS IS STRUCTURAL ->
     THE FIRST STEP WAS THE MISTAKE.
   (iii) THE RESOLUTION
     'There is A WAY OF GRASPING A RULE WHICH IS NOT AN INTERPRETATION, but which
      is EXHIBITED IN WHAT WE CALL "OBEYING THE RULE" AND "GOING AGAINST IT" IN
      ACTUAL CASES.'
   X FATAL OMISSION  quoting (i) and stopping converts a REDUCTIO into SCEPTICISM.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 26/33: KRIPKE 1982 - REPORT IT, THEN RULE ON IT
==============================================================================
   THE READING  Kripke, WITTGENSTEIN ON RULES AND PRIVATE LANGUAGE, 1982
     201 read as a SCEPTICAL PARADOX: no fact about my past usage, dispositions or
     mental history CONSTITUTES my having meant ADDITION rather than the deviant
     function usually called QUADDITION - hence no fact makes '125' rather than
     '5' the correct answer to '68 + 57'.
     Answered by a SCEPTICAL SOLUTION: abandon truth-conditions for ASSERTION-
     CONDITIONS; what licenses ascribing meaning is COMMUNAL AGREEMENT.
   THREE THINGS TO SAY - three sentences, and the scholarship mark is secured
     1 PROVENANCE  Kripke presents it as 'WITTGENSTEIN'S ARGUMENT AS IT STRUCK
       KRIPKE', not as exegesis. Scholarly shorthand: KRIPKENSTEIN.
     2 THE OPPOSING READING  BAKER and HACKER, and McDOWELL from another
       direction: 201 calls the paradox a MISUNDERSTANDING and supplies a THIRD
       PART that the reconstruction under-weights. The text DISSOLVES; it does not
       concede.
     3 THE RULING  PHILOSOPHICALLY VALUABLE - it shows sharply why neither
       INTERPRETATION nor DISPOSITION fixes normativity. EXEGETICALLY DOUBTFUL - a
       sceptical SOLUTION is precisely the constructive theory 128 and 133 forbid.
   OPTIONAL ONLY  the plus/quus reconstruction and the sceptical-solution debate.

     'The individual words of this language are to REFER TO WHAT CAN ONLY BE KNOWN

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 28/33: THE DIARY - PI 258, 260, 265, 270
==============================================================================
   SET-UP (258)  'I want to keep a DIARY about the recurrence of a certain
   SENSATION. To this end I associate it with the sign "S" and write this sign in
   a calendar for every day on which I have the sensation.'
     STEP 1  no definition can be formulated - 'but still I can give myself a kind
             of OSTENSIVE DEFINITION', by concentrating my attention and, as it
             were, POINTING INWARDLY
     STEP 2  what does the ceremony achieve? 'It serves to establish the connexion
             between the sign and the sensation' - but 'I impress it on myself' can
             only mean: this process brings it about that I REMEMBER THE CONNEXION
             RIGHT IN FUTURE
     STEP 3  'AND HERE WE HAVE NO CRITERION OF CORRECTNESS. One would like to say:
             WHATEVER IS GOING TO SEEM RIGHT TO ME IS RIGHT. And that only means
             that HERE WE CAN'T TALK ABOUT "RIGHT".'
   260  'Well, I BELIEVE that this is the sensation S again.' - Perhaps you BELIEVE
        THAT YOU BELIEVE it.
   265  the memory-check compared to buying SEVERAL COPIES OF THE MORNING PAPER to
        assure oneself that what it says is true; a table existing only in the
        imagination is NOT a table; 'justification consists in appealing to
        something INDEPENDENT'.
   270  THE MANOMETER VARIANT  suppose writing 'S' coincides with a rise in blood
        pressure a manometer registers. NOW THE SIGN HAS A USE - and note that
        WHETHER I IDENTIFIED THE SENSATION RIGHTLY HAS BECOME IRRELEVANT.
   NOT AN ARGUMENT FROM FALLIBLE MEMORY  the claim is that there is NOTHING HERE
   FOR MEMORY TO BE RELIABLE ABOUT.  WHAT COLLAPSES IS 'RIGHT', NOT 'S'.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 29/33: THE BEETLE, THE FIVE-STEP CORE, AND WHAT SURVIVES
==============================================================================
   PI 293  'Suppose everyone had A BOX with something in it: we call it a
   "BEETLE". No one can look into anyone else's box, and everyone says he knows
   what a beetle is ONLY BY LOOKING AT HIS BEETLE.'
     * the thing might be DIFFERENT in each box   * it might be CONSTANTLY CHANGING
     * THE BOX MIGHT BE EMPTY
   CONCLUSION  'IF WE CONSTRUE THE GRAMMAR OF THE EXPRESSION OF SENSATION ON THE
   MODEL OF "OBJECT AND DESIGNATION" THE OBJECT DROPS OUT OF CONSIDERATION AS
   IRRELEVANT.'   <- EXPRESSLY CONDITIONAL. The beetle attacks A MODEL.
   ORDER OF ARGUMENT  the DIARY carries the argument; the BEETLE removes a residual
   temptation about SHARED words. Leading with the beetle reverses the order.
                                     |
                                     v
   THE FIVE-STEP CORE - WRITE IT AS A NUMBERED ARGUMENT
     1  A meaningful sign requires a RULE OF USE.
     2  A rule requires a CRITERION OF CORRECTNESS independent of present
        seeming.                                                        [202]
     3  A purely private 'rule' has NO such criterion: the only check is a
        further seeming of the same kind.                          [258, 265]
     4  Therefore 'correct' and 'seems correct' COINCIDE - and where they
        coincide, 'correct' HAS NO APPLICATION.                          [258]
     5  Therefore no rule, no meaning, NO PRIVATE LANGUAGE.
   WHAT SURVIVES  sensations (304) | the solitary speaker (public-TYPE criteria) |
   the first/third-person asymmetry (244, 246) | the inner life as describable (580)
   WHAT IS CONTESTED  the memory objection | actual community vs shareable
   standards | whether criterial grammar respects felt phenomenology

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 30/33: CRITERIA, SYMPTOMS, PI 580 AND THE AVOWAL
==============================================================================
   SOURCE  the BLUE BOOK, DICTATED 1933-35, PUBLISHED 1958. Say 'in the Blue Book'.
   CRITERION (Kriterium)                    SYMPTOM (Symptom)
     INTERNAL / GRAMMATICAL - part of what    EXTERNAL / INDUCTIVE - a correlation
     it IS for the concept to apply; taught   experience has taught us
     with the word
     established BY THE GRAMMAR of the game   established BY EMPIRICAL INVESTIGATION
     if it failed we would no longer know     if it failed we would merely revise a
     what the word MEANS                      generalisation
   ANGINA  the BACILLUS in the blood, which we have DEFINED as the criterion
           the INFLAMED THROAT, which experience has taught us usually goes with it
   PAIN    criterion = PAIN-BEHAVIOUR IN APPROPRIATE CIRCUMSTANCES
           symptom   = a raised pulse, sweating, a groan produced by an ACTOR
   PI 580  'AN "INNER PROCESS" STANDS IN NEED OF OUTWARD CRITERIA.'
     1 to possess a concept is to master a technique of applying a word rightly
       and wrongly                                                    [143-202]
     2 mastery requires circumstances in which application is right and wrong
     3 for an 'inner' concept those circumstances CANNOT BE FURTHER INNER ITEMS -
       that restarts 258 one level down                                   [258]
     4 therefore they must be OUTWARD: behaviour, situation, what precedes and
       follows - the SURROUNDINGS (Umgebung) of the utterance
     5 therefore 580.
   PI 244  adults TEACH THE CHILD NEW PAIN-BEHAVIOUR: 'THE VERBAL EXPRESSION OF
   PAIN REPLACES CRYING AND DOES NOT DESCRIBE IT' -> AVOWAL (Aeusserung)
   PI 246  'only I can know whether I am really in pain' is FALSE in one way
   (others often do know) and NONSENSE in another - it can be said of me only AS A
   JOKE that I KNOW I am in pain.  CAUTION: not every first-person psychological
   sentence is an avowal; 'I was frightened last Tuesday' is a REPORT.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 31/33: THE BEHAVIOURISM CHARGE, THE FOUR-MOVE REPLY, AND PI 354
==============================================================================
   THE CHARGE, RAISED BY WITTGENSTEIN AGAINST HIMSELF                  307-308
     'Aren't you at bottom really saying that EVERYTHING EXCEPT HUMAN BEHAVIOUR IS
      A FICTION?' - 'If I do speak of a fiction, then it is of a GRAMMATICAL
      FICTION.'
   THE REPLY IN FOUR MOVES
     1 THE DENIAL IS OF A PICTURE, NOT A PHENOMENON                        304
       'It is NOT A SOMETHING, BUT NOT A NOTHING EITHER! The conclusion was only
        that A NOTHING WOULD SERVE JUST AS WELL AS A SOMETHING ABOUT WHICH NOTHING
        COULD BE SAID.'  What is rejected is the INNER-OBJECT MODEL - not pain,
        joy or memory.
     2 THE FIRST FALSE STEP                                                308
       We begin by talking innocently of mental 'processes and states', leaving
       their nature undecided - 'and now the analogy which was to make us
       understand our thoughts falls to pieces ... THE DECISIVE MOVEMENT IN THE
       CONJURING TRICK HAS BEEN MADE, AND IT WAS THE VERY ONE THAT WE THOUGHT
       QUITE INNOCENT.'
     3 CRITERIA ARE NOT DEFINITIONS
       Because criteria are DEFEASIBLE, 'he is in pain' is NOT EQUIVALENT to any
       statement about behaviour. THE BEHAVIOURIST ASSERTS AN EQUIVALENCE;
       WITTGENSTEIN ASSERTS A GRAMMATICAL DEPENDENCE. Stating that difference is
       what the examiner is looking for. Pretence is not a counter-example - it is
       a possibility the account PREDICTS.
     4 THE FIRST/THIRD-PERSON ASYMMETRY                                244, 246
       first person = AVOWAL; third person = BEHAVIOURAL AND SITUATIONAL CRITERIA.
   PI 354  'THE FLUCTUATION IN GRAMMAR BETWEEN CRITERIA AND SYMPTOMS MAKES IT LOOK
   AS IF THERE WERE NOTHING AT ALL BUT SYMPTOMS.'
     READING A candour about how concepts work | READING B the firewall is local
     RECOMMENDED  A, conceding that the firewall is secured CASE BY CASE.
   VERDICT  ANTI-CARTESIAN WITHOUT BEING A REDUCTIVE BEHAVIOURIST.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 32/33: SOLIPSISM, THERAPY AND THE ENRICHMENT FENCE
==============================================================================
   SOLIPSISM  EARLY (Tractatus 5.6 / 5.62 / 5.64): limits of MY language = limits
   of MY world | what the solipsist MEANS is correct but CANNOT BE SAID | strictly
   carried out it COINCIDES WITH PURE REALISM. Mechanism: SAYING and SHOWING.
   LATER: the ROUTE CHANGES - from the LIMIT of language to the GRAMMAR of his
   words. THE DILEMMA: either his terms are fixed by PRESENT SEEMING -> 258, no
   rule, NOTHING STATED; or by STABLE REPEATABLE TECHNIQUES -> he has CONCEDED the
   public-type practice he denies. VERDICT: SELF-UNDERMINING - a DISSOLUTION, NOT A
   REFUTATION. NOT PROVED: other minds | the external world | that solitude is
   impossible. 303 'try - in a real case - to doubt someone else's pain'.
   THERAPY  38 language IDLES (wenn die Sprache feiert) | 109 battle against the
   BEWITCHMENT of our intelligence | 115 A PICTURE HELD US CAPTIVE, and it LAY IN
   OUR LANGUAGE | 122 PERSPICUOUS REPRESENTATION -> SEEING CONNECTIONS, find and
   INVENT intermediate cases | 123 'I DON'T KNOW MY WAY ABOUT' | 127 ASSEMBLE
   REMINDERS | 128 theses would be UNDEBATABLE | 129 hidden by SIMPLICITY AND
   FAMILIARITY | 133 METHODS, LIKE DIFFERENT THERAPIES; the real discovery lets one
   STOP | 255 treatment of an ILLNESS | 309 SHOW THE FLY THE WAY OUT OF THE
   FLY-BOTTLE | 124 'leaves everything as it is' - BOUNDED BY ITS FIRST CLAUSE to
   THE USE OF LANGUAGE.
   ENRICHMENT FENCE  ASPECT-SEEING (Part II xi / PPF; figure JASTROW 1899; 'I see
   that it has not changed; AND YET I SEE IT DIFFERENTLY'; the DAWNING OF AN
   ASPECT; ASPECT-BLINDNESS ties it to the EXPERIENCE OF MEANING) and ON CERTAINTY
   (notes 1949-51, published 1969, UNREVISED; answers MOORE; HINGE propositions)
   are ENRICHMENT. BUDGET: 10 marks NONE | 15 marks ONE SENTENCE | 20 marks TWO.
